# Features

Single merged feature reference and process tracker for [An AI augmented author writing environment. .docx](<./An AI augmented author writing environment. .docx>).

This file merges the Word-document feature definitions with the implementation process headers and progress notes. Use this as the active reference when working through numbered features.

## Feature ID Rule

Feature IDs use a parent number plus a testable workflow number, then a lowercase letter for individual checks, for example `Feature 1.3c`. The parent number keeps the workflow tied to the original product feature area, the middle number identifies a specific feature workflow, and the letter identifies a precise interaction that can be tested, debugged, ported, or reported independently.

When a workflow grows large enough to need separate testing, add a new numbered feature set with indented lettered checks instead of folding it into a broad progress paragraph. Keep the same ID in:
- the parent feature's `Testable Subfeatures` list
- the `Feature Implementation Index`
- bug reports, porting notes, and manual test checklists

## Feature Confirmation Rule

When the user says `Feature working`, that phrase confirms the current feature or workflow is accepted and must be documented immediately. Update the relevant numbered feature section and the implementation index in this file before moving on to unrelated work.

The update must:
- describe the feature in product terms, not only as a code change
- place it under the closest numbered feature area
- assign or update the most specific feature/check ID, for example `Feature 1.6b`
- add or revise the `Feature Implementation Index` entry with code locations and line numbers
- include the execution flow from user action to state update, persistence, rendering, logging, or tests where relevant
- note any classification decision if the feature spans more than one feature area

## Feature 01 - Manuscript Issue Console

### Feature Definition

Gives the writer a structured, code-like review panel that logs detected issues in a clear event window and links each one directly to the relevant line or section of the manuscript. With optional AI augmentation, the system can analyse passages, explain what may need attention, and visually guide the user to exactly where changes should be made. The suite can also be used in an IDE-like mode, where manuscript lines are indexed and addressable, allowing the author to jump directly to flagged passages, navigate issues more easily, and work through revisions with software-development-style precision.

### Process Header

The writer reviews manuscript problems as structured diagnostics, with each issue linked to a stable manuscript anchor rather than vague prose feedback.

### Testable Subfeatures

1.1 Anchored manuscript diagnostics console

   The writer sees manuscript problems as structured issue records rather than loose comments. The code keeps each issue tied to a canonical manuscript anchor so clicking an issue can resolve back to a scene, block, line, or span even after the editor rerenders.
   1.1a Issue records include severity, category, source, confidence, and anchor data.
   1.1b Issue panel rows render as actionable diagnostics.
   1.1c Clicking an issue navigates back to the flagged manuscript location.
   1.1d Accepted issue records render as disposable manuscript diagnostics only when their anchors still resolve in the active scene.

1.2 Context-aware scene insertion

   When the author is actively editing scene `x`, pressing New Scene inserts the new draft scene immediately after that scene instead of appending it to the end of the binder. The code captures the active editor scene from pointer, focus, and typing events, then writes the resulting order into `structureDrafts.sceneOrder` so autosave and refresh preserve the placement.
   1.2a Pointer/focus/input events mark the scene currently being edited.
   1.2b New Scene resolves the active scene as the insertion anchor.
   1.2c The new scene is inserted at `x + 1` in the same chapter and selected immediately.

1.3 Manuscript spellcheck and project dictionary

   The manuscript editor underlines local spelling issues while the author writes and lets them resolve a word from an app-owned context menu or grammar-check panel. The code builds a project-aware lexicon from the base word lists, manuscript terms, project dictionary entries, and exception lists so invented names and world terms can become trusted project vocabulary. The same manuscript word targeting now also exposes a local English definition lookup without mutating spellcheck or project dictionary settings.
   1.3a Right-clicking a misspelled word opens a suggestions dropdown.
   1.3b Selecting a suggestion replaces the exact flagged word range in the scene draft.
   1.3c Add-to-dictionary and exception actions update project-scoped spellcheck settings.
   1.3d The grammar-check list supports batch approval of selected words.
   1.3e When a word is flagged, there is a red underline squiggle showing the user.
   1.3f The user can turn on and off the spell check feature with the grammar checkbox built into the UI.
   1.3g Grammar-check list rows show a center arrow from the flagged word to the best suggested correction; clicking the arrow or displayed suggestion accepts the guarded first-occurrence correction, and hovering, focusing, or right-clicking the suggestion area reveals a panel-specific replacement list. The grammar-panel dropdown shows only replacement choices, can continue over the panel footer, and does not reuse the global spellcheck popup with the flagged-word chip or dictionary action.
   1.3h Hovering or right-clicking a red-underlined flagged word opens a compact suggestions dropdown only for that flagged word; the popup shows the word beside `Add to project dictionary` at the top, then suggested replacements underneath without a grammar-check title or flagged-word count.
   1.3i Each grammar-check list row has a compact add-to-project-dictionary button beside the correction arrow; accepting it adds that one flagged word to the project dictionary, clears only that word from any current panel selection, persists through the normal project settings path, and refreshes the open panel so approved vocabulary disappears from the flagged list.
   1.3j The floating grammar-check panel can be dragged to a new location and resized from its lower-right grip; the final viewport-clamped bounds are saved as `editorPrefs.grammarCheckPanelBounds` so the panel reopens at the author's chosen size and location.
   1.3k Right-clicking a selected single manuscript word or right-clicking an unselected manuscript word exposes a `Dictionary` action, including inside the misspelled-word popup, and `Ctrl+T` opens the same transient definition window for the last word behind the manuscript cursor. Selection-first targeting prevents the dictionary action from drifting to a nearby pointer-derived word when the author has already highlighted a word. Lookup state is render-only UI state, definitions come from a bundled Open English WordNet 2025 offline shard index with local exception-form aliases, and no manuscript text, project spellcheck dictionary entry, or project save record is changed by viewing a definition.

1.4 Manuscript find and replace

   Ctrl+F opens a manuscript find panel that searches structured scene text without treating the editor as a flat browser document. The code preserves caret and scroll state while navigating matches, then applies replacements through scene-draft mutation paths so word counts, dirty state, and persistence stay consistent.
   1.4a Opening the find panel can preload selected text.
   1.4b Next/previous navigation selects and centers the matching scene range.
   1.4c Replace Current changes only the active match.
   1.4d Replace All mutates all matching scene drafts through the editor model path.

1.5 Anchored task, inspiration, research, and custom metadata notes

   The author can select manuscript text and turn it into a task, inspiration note, research note, or custom metadata note that remains linked to the passage. The code stores scene IDs, offsets, selected text, source type, note/task metadata, and custom metadata definition fields, then uses exact and fuzzy matching to recover the range if nearby text changes. Metadata groups can also hold project-level folders whose notes do not require a manuscript anchor, while still allowing individual folder notes to point to selected manuscript verses when the author chooses.
   1.5a Selected text can create a scene-linked task.
   1.5b Selected text can create an inline inspiration or research note.
   1.5c Side-panel note/task clicks navigate back to the saved manuscript range.
   1.5d Fuzzy drift recovery attempts to relocate a note when text has shifted.
   1.5e Right-console Tasks, Inspiration, and Research buttons can be hidden from a checklist customization overview and restored later without deleting their anchored records.
   1.5f The Metadata Console can create named custom metadata definitions with highlight colors, add them as horizontally scrollable right-console tabs, and attach custom metadata notes to selected manuscript ranges from the editor context menu.
   1.5g Inspiration, Research, and custom metadata panels can create folders for project-level notes; folder notes persist with the project, count toward the group badge, can optionally store a selected scene/span anchor for navigation back to the manuscript verse, and are mirrored into physical `metadata/<project>/<group>/<folder>/` package folders with `_folder.json` plus per-note JSON files during desktop path saves. Metadata folders can now contain child folders, the folder overview shows a compact folder icon beside the editable folder name instead of a written label, and loose anchored notes can be dragged from the panel list into a folder where they become folder notes that retain their manuscript anchor.
   1.5h The manuscript editor reserves a narrow ManuScriptInfographicLane beside the line-number gutter; open anchored tasks render as minimal exclamation markers, research notes as magnifying-glass markers, and custom metadata notes as scroll markers on the first visual line tied to their manuscript span. Markers on the same line stack horizontally, and clicking a marker opens the matching task or Metadata Console record without mutating manuscript text.
   1.5i Custom metadata definitions can include an uploaded image icon. The icon is normalized as a small project-local data URL on the metadata definition in project settings, rendered in Metadata Console tabs, customization checklists, custom metadata panel headings, right-click note actions, and manuscript ManuScriptInfographicLane markers, while highlight color remains the fallback and the anchor/persistence model for notes stays unchanged.
   1.5j A floating eye inside the ManuScriptInfographicLane hides or restores the vertical infographic marker pane without changing the anchored task, research, custom metadata, event, or World Spine records that feed it. Visibility persists through editor preferences as `manuScriptInfographicLaneVisible`, so the author can keep the lane collapsed across refreshes while retaining a compact restore eye.
   1.5k Scrivener-imported comments and footnotes become a generated `Comments and Footnotes` Metadata Console group. The importer creates scene-named metadata folders, stores each comment or footnote as a folder note with source IDs and binder-path provenance, and attaches a manuscript scene/span anchor when the matching Scrivener `scrivcmt://` field result resolves against the imported text.

1.6 Binder scene and chapter management

   The binder lets the author manage chapters and scenes as story structure, including creation, title editing, ordering, collapse state, trimming, and deletion. The code mutates draft structure overlays and cleans scene-linked metadata so tasks, notes, narration, voice jobs, and selected state do not point at deleted or moved scene records.
   1.6a Chapter and scene titles can be edited from binder/editor surfaces.
   1.6b Scene drag/drop writes an explicit `sceneOrder` overlay.
   1.6c Scene deletion cleans linked task, note, narration, voice, and selection metadata.
   1.6d Scene trimming normalizes whitespace while preserving the draft scene identity.
   1.6e The scene editor footer exposes a centered next-scene button with a hover tooltip; clicking it selects the next scene in current binder order without mutating manuscript content or project persistence, and the control is disabled at the end of the binder.

1.7 Scene editor focus, viewport, and line-aware navigation

   The scene editor remembers where the author is working so navigation, refreshes, and diagnostics land in useful manuscript positions instead of resetting the writing surface. The code captures selection offsets, viewport scroll, line metrics, and layout measurements, then restores them after rerenders or targeted navigation.
   1.7a Selection changes capture scene, offsets, line number, and viewport state.
   1.7b Issue/task/note navigation restores the intended caret and scroll location.
   1.7c Gutter, textarea, underline layer, and line metrics are synchronized.
   1.7d Clicking or click-dragging from the blank manuscript band beside a rendered line starts from that line's textarea position, so the author can select text from the left whitespace without aiming at the first glyph. This is a render/hit-target behavior in the textarea host; it does not persist new project data, and wrap metrics still use the centered manuscript text width for gutter and navigation alignment.
   1.7e Resizable manuscript side panels remember layout per browser workspace-size bucket, so a temporary compact layout caused by moving the window between screens does not overwrite the wider full-screen binder and metadata-console adjustments. Only an explicit completed panel-resizer drag can update a size bucket; passive window resize, maximize, render, console-collapse, and other layout syncs restore and clamp for display without saving profile widths. If an older wide profile already matches a compact bucket, the editor treats it as a clamped profile and recovers the binder to the normal wide default while preserving the right-console width.
   1.7f A compact top-chrome side-panel focus toggle sits in the title tool row under the program name and hides both side rails on Manuscript, Narration, and World pages without changing saved panel width profiles. Manuscript and Narration hide the binder, splitter handles, and Metadata/Narration console; World hides the fixed event rail, linked manuscript pane, and their resize handles; toggling restores the rails from the same project/browser layout state.
   1.7g The manuscript text measure stays visually centered in the scene editor even when ManuScriptInfographicLane marker icons are visible. The ManuScriptInfographicLane floats over the left-side editor margin instead of taking a grid column, and only the markers receive pointer events, so no manuscript, anchor, or metadata persistence changes.
   1.7h New and imported projects start with generous Manuscript binder and right-console widths so long chapter headings are visible on one line before the author manually resizes the rails. The default `binderPanelWidth` and `consoleDockWidth` are now 520px, the startup CSS mirrors that width with a responsive lower bound, and layout clamping accounts for the workspace grid gaps before restoring or saving side-panel profiles.
   1.7i The scene editor groups the Grammar Check chip, chapter breadcrumb, and live chapter word count in one chapter context rail above the scene title. The scene-title masthead is now reserved for the title and future scene-level tools, while chapter and grammar readouts remain live, clickable, and render-only with no new project persistence.

1.8 Writing targets, daily progress, and session tracker

   The writing target system turns manuscript edits into live progress, pacing, session, and calendar history rather than a simple static word count. The code separates total manuscript words from words written today, records session samples and baselines, and persists dashboard state with the project record.
   1.8a Header metrics show selectable writing target, projected-days, and pacing data.
   1.8b The session tracker records active, idle, split, and resumed writing sessions.
   1.8c The writing-goals dashboard shows calendar, streak, selected-day, and note views.
   1.8d Per-day history archives chapter, scene, issue, inspiration, and progress data.
   1.8e In daily-target planning mode, changing the daily target leaves the author-entered release date stable and moves the rendered projected completion date from today's date instead; the projection is derived in `apps/editor/public/features/writing-targets/writing-goals-state-service.js` and persists only the unchanged `releaseDate` unless the author edits that field directly.
   1.8f Top-chrome proofing, developer-log, writing-metric, and status cards can be hidden from a small eye control on the card and restored from right-click checklists opened from visible card surfaces or compact restore targets. Proof read, Developer logs, and writing metrics share one Writing panel checklist with scoped Show all and Hide all controls, while their visual arrangement remains proof/dev-log cards on the left and writing metrics on the right. Fully hidden target strips render one compact restore-eye target for the combined Writing panel instead of preserving full card-height whitespace. Visibility persists in project settings as page-scoped `topPanelVisibility` snapshots for `manuscript`, `world`, and `narration`, so hiding Word Target on the World page does not hide it on Manuscript. The settings mirror to browser compatibility storage for the active project and do not delete the underlying proof-read, developer-log, writing-goal, autosave, or revision data. The former Lines stat is benched from the live chrome with the stat renderer left intact for later revival.
   1.8g Optional milestone sound effects play short generated browser tones when manuscript progress crosses a session segment or daily target. The author can turn them off from Project Settings through `editorPrefs.milestoneSoundEffectsEnabled`; disabling the option prevents playback before an audio context is created and does not change writing-target history, session records, or project metrics.

1.9 Revision session banking

   Revision banking records a structured revision session around manuscript changes so future undo, review, and export workflows can inspect what changed. The code normalizes stored revision state, builds project digests, records events, stages diffs, and renders grouped banked sessions through a dedicated revision history model and a standalone revisions window mockup.
   1.9a Starting a session captures a baseline project digest.
   1.9b Revision events aggregate into a session ledger.
   1.9c Staging a session produces changed-entity and summary data.
   1.9d Banked sessions persist through the revision storage adapter.
   1.9e The revisions window opens beside the writing-goals control and presents a developer-style before/after compare surface.
   1.9f The old scene-editor local-draft revert affordance is hidden behind a disabled feature flag until draft comparison belongs to the revisions window workflow.

1.10 Manuscript inline formatting commands

   The scene editor routes bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, highlight, and clear-decoration erasing through shared manuscript decoration services so toolbar actions affect only the selected manuscript span or the active insertion point. Inline style state is stored as scene-draft range metadata or canonical author marks and rendered through the editor overlay, keeping literal markup out of the manuscript body.
   1.10a Selecting text and pressing a format control toggles only that selected span in structured range metadata.
   1.10b Pressing a format control at a collapsed caret creates a pending insertion point for newly typed formatted text.
   1.10c Bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, and highlight share the same command controller, selection resolver, range mutation path, toolbar state update, render layer, and tests.
   1.10d Highlight toolbar clicks recover the active manuscript selection captured before focus leaves the editor, so selected passages can be converted into user highlights from the toolbar.
   1.10e Pressing Highlight at a collapsed caret acts as a switch for incoming manuscript text, using the post-input caret to anchor typed highlight ranges before they are promoted into canonical marks.
   1.10f Pressing Bold uses the same author-mark behavior as Highlight: selected manuscript text becomes a canonical bold mark, collapsed Bold toggles pending bold typing, and typed insertions split or extend existing bold marks based on the switch state.
   1.10g Text-style author marks render visibly in the textarea overlay while unmarked overlay text stays transparent, so Bold can be seen without breaking highlight backgrounds.
   1.10h Newly created draft scenes reconcile visible editor text into addressable draft blocks before Bold or Highlight marks are applied.
   1.10i Applying a selected Highlight preserves earlier same-scene highlights that were originally created through pending/compatibility ranges.
   1.10j Planned host migration replaces visible text-format overlays with ProseMirror marks on the actual editable manuscript text while keeping canonical marks and anchors application-owned.
   1.10k Editor-scoped Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y undo and redo app-owned Bold/Highlight decoration mark commands by restoring canonical marks, mark sequences, and scene compatibility ranges for the affected scene.
   1.10l Applying or undoing/redoing Bold/Highlight decoration marks preserves the current scene-editor viewport instead of centering the selected range; automatic centering remains reserved for explicit anchored navigation workflows such as task, find, grammar, or issue-console jumps.
   1.10m When stacked active decoration settings are paint-applied to one manuscript passage in a single drag gesture, the app stores one grouped decoration history entry so one undo restores the passage to its pre-gesture formatting state.
   1.10n The Clear Decorations eraser turns off active format paint tools, becomes the active paint tool, and removes author-applied decorations from the selected manuscript span or from any later drag-selected span while it remains active. It clears canonical author marks and legacy inline-format ranges without changing manuscript text, and the selected-range erase participates in the app-owned decoration undo history.
   1.10o The Highlight colour palette uses stronger preset colour values and remembers up to five distinct committed custom RGB colours in editor preferences, rendering them as reusable recent swatches without duplicating an already stored colour.
   1.10p Typing over a selected manuscript passage replaces that exact range, including when repeated characters make a post-input text diff ambiguous. The browser keeps its standard native typing and undo behavior while the editor records the pre-input selection for canonical scene-block reconciliation.

1.11 Anchor-aware decoration drift pipeline

   The manuscript editor keeps issue, task, note, revision, event, narration, and future decoration anchors attached to the intended text while the author edits. The code treats live edit transactions as the primary way to shift or dirty anchors, uses hashes to validate saved anchors on project load, and uses bounded context/preview evidence to recover stale offsets without storing large manuscript excerpts in the project file.
   1.11a Scene edits produce explicit edit transactions before decorations are rerendered.
   1.11b Anchors before, after, inside, overlapping, or deleted by an edit receive deterministic offset/status updates.
   1.11c Load-time validation uses hash and bounded context recovery instead of relying on full selected-text excerpts.
   1.11d Render-only projections are regenerated from validated anchors and are never persisted as decoration objects.
   1.11e Revision-pass decoration tests use the shared anchor pipeline rather than a feature-specific highlight store.
   1.11f Canonical manuscript marks are schema-owned, anchor-backed, and compatible with the shared anchor index/drift path before editor writes migrate.
   1.11g User highlights write canonical manuscript marks, render through author-mark projections, and support pending highlight typing from a collapsed caret without adding a separate right-console panel.

1.12 Draft proof-read coverage runs

   The manuscript editor lets the author activate a proof-read run and deliberately mark manuscript spans they have read during that pass. Coverage expands from explicit text selection gestures and edits made while the run is active, so proof-read state behaves like an author-controlled highlighter rather than passive viewport tracking. When the author opens the proof-read eye control, the visible proof-read mark is a soft transparent underlay beneath author highlights; closing the eye hides the marks without changing the saved run.
   1.12a The top proof-read panel starts a durable proof-read run from an explicit new-run icon, pauses/resumes or continues the current run from play/pause icons, and finishes the current run.
   1.12b During an active run, click-drag text selection inside the scene editor marks that selected manuscript span as proof-read coverage; focus, caret movement, startup, and viewport scrolling do not mark text by themselves.
   1.12c Scene text edits shift existing proof-read spans and add the edited range to the active run.
   1.12d Proof-read coverage renders through a low-priority `draft-proof` projection channel beneath author highlights, persists with the project record, and keeps the latest completed pass available for recall when no run is active.
   1.12e Scene deletion prunes proof-read coverage for removed scene IDs.
   1.12f The proof-read panel eye toggles proof-read marks on and off as a view-only control without mutating coverage spans.
   1.12g The top Project menu opens a vertical Project Settings dropdown with stacked Proof read and Local AI actions; Proof read opens the project-scoped proof-read controls without persisting dropdown layout state.
   1.12h The Proof Read settings window stays control-focused: it persists the selected proof-read iteration's backdrop colour and editable neutral backdrop presets, omits run/scene/span summary counters, and can clear all proof-read run/version data after a guarded confirmation while preserving the project fallback visual settings.
   1.12i The proof-read panel pencil icon opens the same project-scoped Proof Read settings window, while a separate new-run icon prevents accidental second-pass creation from the pause/resume/continue control.
   1.12j A completed proof-read run shows a play control with a `Continue proof read run` tooltip so the author can reopen the completed pass and keep expanding the same coverage record.
   1.12k During an active proof-read run, Shift+drag selection inside the scene editor removes proof-read coverage from the selected manuscript span without crossing scene boundaries.
   1.12l Resuming or continuing a proof-read run turns proof-read marks on automatically and navigates the manuscript editor to the run's saved resume point, falling back to the latest covered span when no explicit pause point exists.
   1.12m Browser refresh/project activation restores proof-read marks as visible when the loaded project has a valid active proof-read run, while inactive or completed-only proof-read projects clear stale visible state until the author opens the eye control.
   1.12n The Proof Read settings window keeps stronger neutral backdrop presets and a recent custom-colour row, storing up to five distinct custom backdrop colours in proof-read settings so authors can reuse them without duplicating swatches.
   1.12o Picking or editing proof-read backdrop colours automatically turns proof-read marks visible so the author can preview the selected colour against the manuscript editor.
   1.12p The Proof Read settings window includes an iteration dropdown beside the `Proof Read` title, and each durable proof-read run stores its own backdrop colour, editable presets, and recent custom colours; draft-proof projections carry the run colour so multiple visible iterations can render with their saved visual setting.
   1.12q Starting a new proof-read iteration automatically sets proof-read marks to visible, so the author can see the fresh pass's coverage underlay without manually opening the eye control.
   1.12r Active proof-read text selection uses a distinct live selection colour and releases the browser selection after coverage is recorded, so selecting already-covered words remains visible and the next drag does not reuse the previous selected range.
   1.12s The Proof Read settings window lets the author tick specific proof-read iterations in Version Data and delete only those runs, while the existing guarded clear-all action remains available and project fallback visual settings are preserved.
   1.12t Dark mode proof-read coverage derives its visible mark from a colour lifted at least 40% brighter than each run's saved backdrop colour, then applies a stronger theme-specific fill, outline, and lower edge. The Proof Read settings swatches use the same lifted preview colour in dark mode while the colour input and saved project value remain the raw backdrop colour. The visual change is render-only CSS on the textarea projection layer and settings controls and does not alter proof-read span persistence or project data.
   1.12u Proof-read live selection keeps native textarea text transparent when inline-format overlays are active, so italic, bold, and highlight marks are not painted twice while the author is selecting or right-clicking marked text.
   1.12v The Project dropdown's User Settings section includes a Shortcuts window where the author can review, capture, clear, reset, and reset all application behavior shortcuts. The keymap persists as normalized `editorPrefs.keyboardShortcuts` through the existing `app-settings` project preference path, and shortcut dispatch resolves configured behavior IDs for project save/load/new/open, manuscript find, writing goals, pane switching, inline formatting, undo/redo, and inline note commit instead of relying on hard-coded key checks. This is classified under Feature 1 because it is an editor IDE shell workflow surfaced from the existing Project Settings dropdown; it stores user preference data only and does not mutate manuscript, proof-read, world, narration, or voice records.
   1.12w Day mode proof-read coverage uses a stronger theme-specific fill, outline, and subtle lower edge from each run's saved backdrop colour, while the default colour board and dark-mode proof-read paint remain unchanged. The change is render-only CSS on the `draft-proof` projection and does not alter run settings, span persistence, or project data.
   1.12x The Proof Read settings window closes on click-away only when the author clicks outside both the settings dialog and the proof-read chrome control. Clicks inside the settings controls or the settings trigger keep the window open. This is transient UI state only and does not mutate proof-read coverage, run settings, or project persistence.
   1.12y The Proof Read settings window shows only the current resolved editor theme's highlight-strength dial: Light mode edits the saved Light intensity and Dark mode edits the saved Dark intensity. Each selected proof-read iteration saves its own `highlightIntensityByTheme` values, new runs inherit the project fallback intensity settings, and manuscript proof-read projections render the saved intensity as theme-specific fill, outline, and edge strength without changing coverage spans.

1.13 Editor appearance theme

   The editor shell lets the author switch the workspace appearance between Light, Dark, and System from the top chrome. The choice is stored as an editor preference, resolves into document-level theme attributes for rendering, and keeps manuscript, issue, world, narration, and voice data unchanged.
   1.13a The top chrome exposes a compact segmented appearance control with Light, Dark, and System options in the title tool row under the program name.
   1.13b The selected appearance normalizes through `editorPrefs.appearanceMode`, persists through the existing editor preference/project-settings path, and mirrors to browser compatibility storage for the active project.
   1.13c The shell resolves the saved mode into `data-theme="light"` or `data-theme="dark"` on the document and app root, including live system-colour changes when System is selected.
   1.13d The first dark-mode rendering slice covers the app shell, top chrome, project menus, side panels, manuscript editor frame, editor overlays, common popovers/modals, World/Narration surface hooks, and the writing-goals dashboard without persisting render-only theme data.
   1.13e The dark-mode rendering slice now includes embedded console rows and form-like controls in the manuscript binder, Metadata Console task groups, Narration Console recording fields, saved-take placeholders, tracker monitor, grammar compact control, autosave card, World Spine rails/timeline/cards, linked-passage rail, and Dream Scaping suggestion cards so light-mode cards do not bleed into the dark workspace.
   1.13f Dark mode preserves the manuscript inline-format projection layering by keeping the native textarea text transparent while render-only overlay text is active, preventing doubled text weight in scenes with highlights or other manuscript projections.
   1.13g Dark mode gives author-applied Bold marks a stronger metric-neutral overlay stroke and brighter text colour while slightly softening unmarked overlay text, so Bold remains visible without changing manuscript metrics or stored mark data.
   1.13h The floating Grammar Check window uses dark modal, row, suggestion, disabled-control, footer, and close-button surfaces so its spellcheck review workflow remains readable in dark mode.
   1.13i The Writing Goals dashboard uses dark surfaces for settings presets, calendar status cells, indicator chips, selected-day stat cards, passage excerpts, notes, archive/list entries, and footer actions so future dashboard views inherit the appearance system without storing theme data in writing-target records.
   1.13j The session tracker circular WPM gauge uses a dark theme backdrop, muted dark-mode track, and a white animated pen body with dark detailing while retaining the dark pen treatment in light mode. The recolour is render-only through theme variables, so writing-target session records, WPM calculations, and persistence remain unchanged.
   1.13k Dark mode keeps display-like manuscript editing surfaces on the same theme surface instead of painting separate input rectangles. This covers the scene masthead title, inline binder title inputs, and main manuscript textarea, including focused editing state; focus is carried by caret, underline, or border treatment rather than a contrasting field fill. The behavior is render-only CSS and does not change scene-title, manuscript, narration metadata, or project data.
   1.13l Dark mode selected/active cards and buttons use a shared edge-glow treatment: the selected item keeps its normal dark interior while the border and outer ring carry the highlight. The rule covers the File menu recent-project cards, top chrome buttons, manuscript line cards, binder/console/task/note rows, Grammar Check rows, World Spine controls, Worldbuilding catalogue selections, and Writing Goals selected controls as render-only CSS without changing editor preferences or project data.

1.14 Shared form dismissal behavior

   Routine transient forms use a minimal cross in the top-right corner for cancel/dismiss actions, keeping Save/Create actions in the form action row. This render convention applies to anchored task/note composers, custom metadata creation, scene World Spine metadata, Dream Scaping catalogue entry forms, World Spine event catalogue assignment, and World Spine implication composer surfaces without changing their persistence handlers or saved project data.
   1.14a A shared `.form-dismiss-button` owns the minimal top-right cross styling, including dark-mode hover/focus treatment.
   1.14b Converted forms keep their existing `data-action` dismissal routes while removing visible full-width Cancel or Back action-row buttons.
   1.14c Form headings reserve enough space for the cross so long labels do not sit under the dismissal control.
   1.14d Scene World Spine metadata forms stay open while the author clicks or tabs through their input, textarea, datalist-backed picker, and Save controls. Generic document-click dismissal now treats the binder-backed form and World Spine context menus as owned transient surfaces, so fields can receive focus and form actions can read live values before the surface closes.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented.
- Repository coverage: `IssueRecord`, `ManuscriptAnchor`, local analysis issue suggestions, issue console records, editor navigation to flagged scene lines, simplified issue-console headings, collapsible chapter groups in the issue, inspiration, research, custom metadata, and task consoles, chapter-grouped open task lists, collapsible manuscript chapter tabs, double-click editable binder chapter titles, double-click editable binder scene titles, double-click editable manuscript scene titles, scene-editor chapter breadcrumb sync, resizable left and right sidebar splitters, browser keyboard shortcuts for save/new/open/writing-goals/pane switching, a User Settings shortcut editor under the Project dropdown that stores behavior bindings in editor preferences, right-click selected-text task creation with generated scene-order task titles, blue task-body instructions, thumbnail-hover task expansion, click-only manuscript references, draft-only inline editor inspiration/research/custom metadata bubbles with a normal manuscript verse field that preloads selected text or saves against the inserted typed verse, two-way inspiration/research/custom metadata navigation between saved manuscript ranges and side-panel note items, hover previews that glow the selected manuscript range, task-click navigation back to the editor range with fuzzy selected-text drift recovery, right-console feature-button customization from a right-click checklist, custom metadata tag creation with per-tag highlight colors, optional uploaded image icons, and horizontal mouse-wheel tab scrolling, whitespace-click writing focus, whitespace-origin manuscript drag selection, caret-centering while typing, pane-local editor scrolling, scene task completion, app-owned Grammar Check underlines with an app-owned suggestions popup in the manuscript editor, now backed by a SCOWL-derived default wordlist plus the prior supplemental alpha list and contraction-aware matching, plus project-scoped dictionary and exception lists stored on each project save file, bulk-add actions from multi-word selections, and a movable, resizable grammar-check list panel with per-word checkboxes, saved user-preference bounds, and batch project-dictionary approval, Ctrl+F manuscript find/replace that preserves the editor caret and scroll position when the panel opens, remaining-task chapter badges in the Manuscript panel, live manuscript word counts with release-date-aware projected-days forecasting and on-track/off-track hints, a Ctrl+Alt+T writing-target utility window, selectable top-header writing metrics, linked release-date and daily-target goal syncing, a session-split and inactivity timer panel with a 20-minute session time default, a 15-minute segment-close window, a 30-minute new-session window, resumable session history, and an idle session indicator that flips back to active on the first new manuscript edit, plus recent-snapshot words/minute pacing, red-to-blue-to-green progress signaling, and pulsing over-target glow, plus a full writing-goals dashboard modal with top summary cards, a month/week/list calendar, streak summary, selected-day detail panel, notes, and explicit save/cancel/reset actions, with the daily target tracker now counting words written today separately from the session tracker, a per-day progress archive with chapter/scene/issue/inspiration breakdown, a 30-day believable sample-history seeding action for tracker testing, a project save-file load command that emits manuscript, world, task, timeline, and template data with source provenance, nested source template sheets, full source-path provenance, retained source sheet text, file-backed desktop/browser logging, a saved-project library with browser load/save/create controls and file-backed Save As/load routes, and a documented project save model for the Serva Vitae reference fixture. The revision drafting UI is currently benched from the scene editor while a new revision-history service, diff/event/model modules, revision storage adapter, and standalone revisions window mockup preserve the bookkeeping and review path for future undo/redo and banking workflows. The right-hand Metadata Console now stays focused on Tasks, Inspiration, Research, and user-defined metadata; revision review has moved out of that dock. Modal dismissal is deliberate: a single outside click closes the writing-target window, but a pointer that starts inside the modal and is released outside should leave the window open; the revisions window now follows the same deliberate close behavior.
- Process update (2026-05-21): confirmed context-aware scene insertion. When the author is editing scene `x`, the New Scene action creates the next draft scene at `x + 1` in the same chapter and persists that placement through the scene-order overlay instead of appending to the end of the binder.
- Revisions window testing now includes a repeatable fixture seeder and on-disk revision package writer for `revision.json`, `events.json`, `project.diff.json`, and `summary.md` outputs derived from `SaveTestFile/RevisionsTest/RevisionsTestOriginFileproject-serva-vitae.abe-project.json`, and that source fixture now carries seeded `REVISIONSTEST` manuscript text plus banked revision sessions for window inspection.
- Process update (2026-05-21): revision review now lives in a standalone window mockup opened from the top chrome beside Writing Goals, and the right-hand console no longer has a Revisions tab. The UI presents session filters, a changed-file rail, changed entities, and side-by-side before/after digest operations so the author can review banked revisions with developer diff ergonomics before final design implementation.
- Process update (2026-07-17): benched the scene-editor `Revert local draft` affordance behind a disabled feature flag. The reset handler remains parked for a future revision-perusal workflow, but authors no longer see a button that suggests a finished revision browser.
- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `1.6e` footer next-scene navigation. The scene editor footer now renders a centered icon button between the selection and scene word-count readouts, shows the destination scene title in a hover/focus tooltip, resolves the next scene from the live binder order on click, and disables the control on the final scene. This is render and selection state only; it reuses the existing scene selection path and does not write manuscript or project data.
- Process update (2026-05-22): manuscript inline formatting now stores bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, and highlight as scene-draft range metadata rendered by the editor overlay, replacing literal inserted HTML tags and the older scene-wide italic preference path.
- Process update (2026-05-23): established the manuscript mark/decoration projection boundary in `docs/architecture/manuscript-decoration-layer.md`. Author-applied inline formatting remains a scene-record compatibility field during the browser slice, now survives project JSON save/load and refresh through scene normalization, and must later be promoted to canonical anchor-backed manuscript marks rather than combined with spellcheck, AI-suggestion, hover, or narration visuals.
- Process update (2026-05-28): completed the current refactor checkpoint without changing the author-facing workflow contract. Manuscript find/replace derivation, input sequencing, selection policy, anchored task/note preview planning, projection selection, and textarea host rendering now have explicit feature/adapter owners; project-library normalization, project-record assembly, and activation orchestration now have explicit state owners. The visible workflows remain Features `1.3`, `1.4`, `1.5`, `1.7`, `1.10`, and `8.2-8.5`, with runtime projections kept outside durable project data.
- Process update (2026-05-28): completed the anchored diagnostic projection slice for Feature `1.1d`. The scene editor now derives `diagnostic` projections from open, anchor-backed issue records, the textarea host paints those diagnostics as disposable overlays, invalid or stale anchors do not render, and world/Dream Scaping suggestion queues remain excluded from manuscript highlights. `packages/shared-types` now stages an `AnchoredManuscriptSuggestion` DTO for later manuscript-range AI proposals without adding a `suggestion` projection channel.
- Process update (2026-05-28): continued Phase 2 by moving manuscript editor focus, range selection, bookmark, viewport, and offset-centering effects behind the textarea editor-host adapter for Feature `1.7`. Shell workflows still decide when navigation happens, but the textarea-specific DOM operations now sit behind host capabilities that a future editor adapter can replace.
- Process update (2026-07-17): refined Feature `1.7d` whitespace-origin manuscript selection. `styles.css` now lets the textarea own the blank horizontal band around the centered manuscript measure so native click-drag selection can start to the left of a rendered line; `textarea-editor-host.js` subtracts that side padding when deriving wrap metrics so gutter counts, offset centering, and line-aware navigation continue to track the manuscript text width.
- Process update (2026-07-17): refined Feature `1.12r` active proof-read selection handling. During active proof-read runs the scene editor tags the textarea as proofing-active, paints the temporary browser selection in a teal colour distinct from durable tan coverage, and collapses the selection after the coverage range is recorded so repeated click-drag selections over words such as already-covered dialogue labels start cleanly. Coverage persistence and projection rendering still route through the existing draft-proof service and textarea host.
- Process update (2026-07-18): refined Feature `1.12g` Project Settings dropdown layout. The top Project menu now renders its Proof read and Local AI settings actions as a vertical stack through a dedicated chrome CSS hook, preserving the existing menu actions while avoiding the shared File-menu horizontal action row.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `1.12v` configurable application shortcuts. The Project dropdown now has a User Settings -> Shortcuts window where authors can capture, clear, reset, and reset all behavior shortcuts. The normalized keymap lives in `editorPrefs.keyboardShortcuts`, persists through the existing `app-settings` project preference route, updates save prompts and writing-goal tooltips, and drives global shortcut dispatch by behavior ID for project, manuscript, formatting, history, pane, and inline-note commands.
- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `1.7e` browser-size-scoped side-panel layout profiles. Binder and Metadata Console resizer adjustments are now stored in `panelResizerLayoutProfiles` keyed by quantized workspace width, with a `default` profile preserving legacy pixel or percentage settings before automatic resize clamping. Window moves to narrower displays can clamp the live render, but only an explicit completed panel-resizer drag with `reason: "panel-resize"` updates the current size bucket; narrower buckets do not replace the wider fallback profile, passive maximize/render/console-collapse syncs are blocked from profile persistence, maximizing the browser recovers the nearest saved full-size profile when OS chrome or scrollbar differences land just outside the exact saved bucket, and the `LayoutState` logger records sync reason, browser geometry, selected profile key, restore/recovery decisions, clamp bounds, and persistence blocks/writes. If an older saved wide profile has the same below-default binder/console widths as a compact bucket, restore treats it as a clamped profile and recovers a usable wide binder instead of replaying the contaminated compact width.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `1.7f` cross-page side-panel focus mode. A compact icon-only control in the top chrome toggles `sidePanelsHidden`, persists it through project `app-settings` and browser compatibility storage, and applies `is-side-panels-hidden` to hide the manuscript/narration binder plus console rails and the World Spine event plus manuscript rails. While hidden, layout sync skips side-panel width restore, clamp, and profile persistence so saved resizer profiles are not contaminated.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `1.7g` centered manuscript measure with a floating ManuScriptInfographicLane. `styles.css` now keeps the scene-editor codeframe as a gutter-plus-document grid and positions the ManuScriptInfographicLane icon track absolutely over the left editor margin, so the marker strip remains visible and clickable without shifting the manuscript text measure to the right. The ManuScriptInfographicLane itself ignores pointer events while marker buttons remain interactive. This is render-only CSS and does not mutate manuscript text, anchors, metadata notes, or project persistence.
- Process update (2026-07-23): refined Feature `1.7h` for generous imported-project side-panel defaults. New, loaded-without-layout, and ported projects now seed the Manuscript binder and right Metadata/Narration Console rails at 520px instead of the prior narrow default, while explicit user-resized profiles still restore through `panelResizerLayoutProfiles`. The runtime clamp subtracts the workspace grid gaps before enforcing the manuscript minimum width, so the wider defaults are used when the viewport can support them and are reduced only for constrained windows.
- Process update (2026-08-04): added Feature `1.7i` chapter context rail controls. `features/scene-editor.js` now renders the Grammar Check chip and chapter word-count readout beside the chapter breadcrumb instead of in the scene-title masthead; `styles.css` keeps that rail centered on desktop and stacks it cleanly on narrow viewports. The existing grammar-panel toggle and live chapter-word-count data attribute remain unchanged, so this is a render-only placement change with no manuscript, issue, spellcheck dictionary, word-count, or project persistence mutation.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `1.13` editor appearance theme. The top chrome now offers Light, Dark, and System appearance buttons; `editorPrefs.appearanceMode` persists the selected mode through the existing project-settings/editor preference path; `app.js` resolves the active theme to document/app-root `data-theme` attributes and updates System mode on OS preference changes; and `styles.css` plus `writing-goals-dashboard.css` provide the first dark-mode token and surface override slice without changing manuscript, anchor, world, narration, or voice data.
- Process update (2026-07-19): revised Feature `1.13` dark-mode coverage from screenshot review. `styles.css` now darkens the sticky manuscript heading, autosave/grammar/count pills, Metadata Console task rows and inline task-title inputs, side-panel tabs, Narration Console status/verse/tracker fields, follow-scroll toggle, saved-take empty state, tracker monitor, and narration transport controls through `data-theme="dark"` overrides; the regression guard in `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` now checks these selector hooks.
- Process update (2026-07-19): revised Feature `1.13` World-pane dark-mode coverage from screenshot review. `styles.css` now darkens the World Spine workspace, event rail, linked-passage rail, timeline canvas, node cards, axis/connectors, detail card, shared World/Dream Scaping cards, and Dream Scaping suggestion gradient through `data-theme="dark"` overrides; the desktop regression guard now checks the new World Spine and Dream Scaping selector hooks.
- Process update (2026-07-19): fixed Feature `1.13` manuscript text weight in dark mode. The dark theme now explicitly restores `.editor-document-input.has-inline-format-projection` to transparent so the textarea does not paint underneath the inline-format overlay; `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the selector because projection layering is render-only and must not alter manuscript text or mark data.
- Process update (2026-07-19): refined Feature `1.13` dark-mode Bold readability. `styles.css` now softens normal inline-format projection text and gives `.editor-inline-format-bold` a brighter colour plus stronger metric-neutral text-shadow in dark mode, preserving overlay alignment while making Bold distinguishable; `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the dark Bold selector and prevents a real `font-weight: 700` regression.
- Process update (2026-07-19): revised Feature `1.13` floating Grammar Check dark-mode coverage from screenshot review. `styles.css` now darkens the movable grammar-check panel shell, close button, drag/header copy, grouped word rows, suggestion controls, disabled dictionary/apply buttons, suggestion menus, and footer through `data-theme="dark"` overrides; `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the grammar panel and suggestion-control selector hooks.
- Process update (2026-07-19): revised Feature `1.13` Writing Goals dashboard dark-mode coverage from screenshot review. `writing-goals-dashboard.css` now darkens the goal settings preset block, checkbox rows, calendar day and week status cells, indicator chips, selected-day stat cards, passage excerpt, notes textarea, archive/list entries, and footer buttons through `data-theme="dark"` overrides; `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the dashboard selector hooks.
- Process update (2026-07-21): refined Features `1.7f` and `1.13` top-chrome compaction. `apps/editor/public/shell/editor-chrome.js` now stacks the `Version: Test` environment badge, appearance segmented control, and side-panel focus toggle in `.desktop-title-tools` below the program name, leaving workspace tabs, Spotify, and Local AI in the center strip. `apps/editor/public/styles.css` scopes compact title-row control dimensions, and `test/editor-chrome.test.mjs` plus `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guard the grouping and CSS hooks. This is render-only chrome layout; it does not change `editorPrefs.appearanceMode`, `sidePanelsHidden`, `topPanelVisibility`, manuscript, world, narration, voice, or persistence data.
- Process update (2026-07-23): refined Feature `1.13j` session tracker dark-mode artwork. `session-tracker-icons.js` and the standalone session-tracker pen SVG assets now expose theme-controlled pen body/accent variables; `styles.css` gives the WPM gauge a dark backdrop, muted ring track, and white pen body when `data-theme="dark"` is active; `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the inline icon module, asset SVGs, and dark CSS hooks.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined Feature `1.13k` dark-mode manuscript editing surfaces. `styles.css` now overrides the global dark input fill for the scene title, binder title inputs, and main manuscript textarea in both inactive and focused states, and the Manuscript binder header uses theme surface tokens instead of a custom contrasting strip. `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the transparent editing-surface selectors.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined Feature `1.13l` dark-mode selected item highlighting. `styles.css` now defines shared dark selected-edge variables and applies them to selected/active chrome, recent-project, manuscript line-card, binder, console, task, note, Grammar Check, World Spine, and Worldbuilding catalogue controls; `writing-goals-dashboard.css` uses the same variables for selected dashboard controls. The selected item interior remains dark and only the border/ring illuminates. `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` guards the shared variables and representative selected-state selectors.
- Process update (2026-07-23): added Feature `1.14` shared form dismissal behavior. Routine transient forms now park cancellation in a minimal top-right cross using the shared `.form-dismiss-button`, while Save/Create remains in the action row. The change covers anchored task/note composers, custom metadata creation, scene World Spine metadata, Dream Scaping entry forms, the World Spine event catalogue assignment form, and World Spine implication composer without changing project persistence, world links, anchored records, or metadata definitions.
- Process update (2026-07-30): refined Feature `1.14` scene World Spine metadata dismissal. Field clicks inside the binder-backed form now return before generic task-surface dismissal, and action clicks inside owned binder/World Spine context surfaces are left to their specific handlers before any menu teardown. This preserves focus, datalist selection, Save, and close behavior without changing scene metadata persistence.
- Process update (2026-06-14): selected ProseMirror as the target primary manuscript host and added `docs/architecture/prosemirror-editor-roadmap.md`. A full feature-set review now gates the migration by deterministic canonical scene/mark round trips, application-owned split/merge/paste identity rules, repository-owned transaction DTOs with provenance, scene and project lifecycle flushing, anchor-service synchronization, projection persistence exclusion, numeric performance budgets, feature-specific parity evidence, and a retained textarea fallback. ProseMirror owns only runtime document, DOM, selection, history, transactions, and decorations for one open scene; project structure, anchors, marks, records, revisions, metrics, and persistence remain application-owned.
- Process update (2026-05-28): continued Phase 3 for Feature `1.3` by moving grammar-check panel summary modeling, grouped misspelling entries, panel state transitions, drag sessions, and panel markup into `features/spellcheck/grammar-check-panel.js`. Project dictionary and exception word normalization plus target-list mutation planning live in `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-project-settings.js`; spellcheck context-menu view modeling/markup lives in `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-context-menu.js`; selection, grammar-panel item, and editor word-range context-menu record derivation live in `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-context-controller.js`; and debounce timer state for refresh scheduling lives in `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-refresh-controller.js`. The shell still owns persistence effects, menu mounting/event dispatch, and host-specific projection refresh effects.
- Process update (2026-07-15): expanded Feature `1.3` grammar-check list correction controls. `features/spellcheck/grammar-check-panel.js` now derives suggestions, first-occurrence offsets, and row-level accept/dropdown markup for grouped flagged words; `app.js` reuses the existing guarded spellcheck correction path and refreshes the open panel immediately after an accepted correction; `styles.css` owns the row arrow, best-suggestion, and hover/focus alternate-suggestion layout.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.3i` grammar-check row dictionary approval. `features/spellcheck/grammar-check-panel.js` now renders a row-level dictionary button in the quick-action group immediately beside the correction arrow, including rows with no suggestion; `app.js` applies the approved word through the existing project spellcheck settings mutation and rerenders the manuscript/grammar panel; `styles.css` owns the shared quick-action button layout; and `test/grammar-check-panel.test.mjs` asserts the action markup and placement before the correction arrow.
- Process update (2026-07-15): refined Feature `1.3` grammar-check suggestion dropdown styling. Grammar-panel suggestions now render as a standalone floating replacement list anchored to the suggested word column, with no flagged-word chip and no dictionary action inside the dropdown. The shell suppresses the global spellcheck context menu inside the grammar panel, positions the panel-only list outside the scrollable list so it can continue over the grammar-window footer, and keeps it active while the cursor moves from the primary suggestion into the replacement choices.
- Process update (2026-07-15): refined Feature `1.3` flagged-word popup behavior. `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-context-menu.js` now renders a compact word chip plus top-row `Add to project dictionary` action followed by suggestions, with no grammar-check heading or flagged count. `features/spellcheck/spellcheck-context-controller.js` exposes a hover-only derivation path that refuses caret fallback, while `app.js` opens hover popups from underline hit testing and keeps right-click popups explicit.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `1.3j` grammar-check panel layout persistence. `state/grammar-check-panel-state.js` now normalizes and clamps persisted panel bounds, `features/spellcheck/grammar-check-panel.js` owns the resize controller and handle markup, `editor-model.js` stores `editorPrefs.grammarCheckPanelBounds`, `app.js` applies saved bounds on open and persists drag/resize results through the app-settings project mutation path, and `styles.css` keeps sized panels scrollable with light/dark resize grips.
- Process update (2026-08-11): added Feature `1.3k` local English dictionary lookup. `features/dictionary/dictionary-word-service.js` resolves manuscript words from pointer locations and from the word behind the cursor for `Ctrl+T`; `features/dictionary/english-definition-lexicon-service.js` initially loaded a small bundled local definition set with alias and simple inflection fallback; `features/dictionary/dictionary-context-controller.js` derives lookup requests for right-click and shortcut entry points; `features/dictionary/dictionary-window.js` renders loading, found, not-found, and error states; `app.js` coordinates transient lookup state without persisting it; `styles.css` adds light and dark window coverage; and focused dictionary/menu/shortcut tests cover the behavior.
- Process update (2026-08-12): installed Open English WordNet 2025 core as the offline dictionary provider for Feature `1.3k`. The initial small definition set was removed; `features/dictionary/open-english-wordnet-2025/manifest.json` now describes the CC-BY 4.0 source, attribution, and per-letter shard paths, `features/dictionary/open-english-wordnet-2025/shards/*.json` stores compact local lookup data generated from the OEWN JSON release plus WNDB exception files, and `scripts/build-open-english-wordnet-index.mjs` owns the reproducible conversion path. `features/dictionary/english-definition-lexicon-service.js` now loads the manifest first and fetches only the needed first-letter shard on demand, preserving simple inflection fallback while adding irregular-form aliases such as past-tense exception lookups.
- Process update (2026-08-14): fixed Feature `1.3k` selected-word dictionary targeting. `features/dictionary/dictionary-word-service.js` now resolves an explicit one-word selection, including punctuation-trimmed selections such as `"willow,"`, and rejects multi-word selections for selected-text lookup. `features/dictionary/dictionary-context-controller.js` now prefers that selected word before falling back to pointer hit-testing, so a right-click context menu cannot carry a nearby word such as `hum` when the author selected another word. `app.js` also preserves explicit manuscript selections when the context-menu event target is an editor overlay instead of the textarea, keeping the selected word available to the dictionary controller.
- Process update (2026-05-28): continued Phase 4 for Feature `1.5` by moving anchored task/passage-note context-menu and composer view modeling/markup into `features/anchored-records/task-context-menu.js`; task composer planning, inline passage-note draft seeding, composer-backed task/note record creation, inline passage-note typed-range policy, panel model grouping, and Local AI title request DTO/guard planning into `features/anchored-records/anchored-record-controller.js`; task and passage-note panel/chapter-group/item markup into `features/anchored-records/task-panel.js` and `features/anchored-records/passage-note-panel.js`; and delete-confirmation rendering/preferences normalization into `features/anchored-records/delete-confirmation-dialog.js`. The shell still owns context-menu event dispatch, inline manuscript insertion effects, persistence effects, and async AI title calls.
- Process update (2026-05-28): started Phase 5 store-facade work by moving collapsed binder/console chapter normalization, toggle transitions, and removed-chapter pruning into `state/editor-ui-state.js`. `app.js` still owns the persistence writes and render scheduling around those transitions.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.5e` console feature customization. Right-clicking blank right-console panel space opens a checklist for Tasks, Inspiration, and Research; unchecked panels remove their feature buttons and collapse their bodies while their anchored task/note records remain intact. The visibility snapshot is normalized in `state/editor-ui-state.js`, rendered by `features/side-panel-customization/side-panel-customization.js`, hydrated through project settings, and compared as an `app-settings` persistence domain.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.5f` custom metadata notes and renamed the task heading to Metadata Console. The right-console tab strip now supports user-defined metadata tabs such as Lore, stores their label and highlight color in project settings, scrolls horizontally with the mouse wheel when the metadata options overflow, and exposes matching right-click manuscript actions that create anchored custom metadata notes through the same passage-note persistence and navigation path as Inspiration and Research.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.5g` project metadata subgroups and fixed long Research panel tab access. The Metadata Console tab strip is now sticky inside the right-console scroll container so long Research lists cannot scroll the other metadata tabs out of reach. Inspiration, Research, and custom metadata panels can create subgroup containers with project-only notes; each subgroup note can remain unanchored or store a selected scene/span anchor for later verse navigation. Subgroup records persist as `metadataSubgroups` in project records and use the `metadata-subgroups` persistence domain rather than overloading anchored passage notes.
- Process update (2026-07-17): renamed the author-facing Feature `1.5g` subgroup workflow to metadata folders. The Metadata Console now labels the section as Folders with New folder/Delete folder actions, creates new records with `metadata-folder-*` IDs while still reading legacy `metadata-subgroup-*` records, persists edits through the `metadata-folders` dirty domain, and writes folder-backed desktop packages under `metadata/<project>/<group>/<folder>/`. Desktop path saves have the filesystem permission surface needed to create those directories through `/api/project-file/save`; browser file-handle saves remain single JSON files because the current browser handle is file-scoped, not directory-scoped.
- Process update (2026-07-17): extended Feature `1.5g` metadata folders with nested folder creation and drag-to-folder note filing. New folders created inside an existing folder are stored as child records and written as physical subdirectories under the parent folder in desktop project packages. Loose anchored Inspiration, Research, or custom metadata notes can be dragged into a matching metadata folder; the app creates a folder note with the original title/body and manuscript anchor, then deletes the loose note through the anchored-record service so the move persists cleanly.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `1.5h` manuscript ManuScriptInfographicLane markers. The scene editor now derives a render-only ManuScriptInfographicLane from open task anchors, research passage-note anchors, and custom metadata passage-note anchors; paints exclamation, magnifying-glass, and scroll icons beside the first visual line tied to each record; stacks same-line markers horizontally; and routes marker clicks back through the existing task/note navigation path so persistence stays with the anchored record.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `1.5i` custom metadata image icons. The New metadata form accepts a small PNG, JPG, WebP, or GIF upload, validates and normalizes it as an optional `icon` object on the custom metadata definition in project settings, and renders that icon in the Metadata Console tab strip, customization checklist, custom metadata panel heading, right-click note action, and manuscript ManuScriptInfographicLane marker. Existing definitions without icons continue to use their highlight-colour swatch and generic metadata marker.
- Process update (2026-07-20): added Feature `1.5j` ManuScriptInfographicLane floating eye visibility. The scene editor now renders a sticky eye control inside the ManuScriptInfographicLane shell and a separate marker track; clicking the eye toggles `editorPrefs.manuScriptInfographicLaneVisible`, persists it through the existing project `app-settings` path, and clears or restores only render-only marker projections while leaving all anchored task, note, event, and World Spine records intact.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `1.5k` Scrivener comments and footnotes import. Scrivener `content.comments` sidecars now populate a generated `Comments and Footnotes` Metadata Console group with scene folders, comment/footnote note titles, source provenance, and resolved manuscript anchors when the imported `scrivcmt://` field result text can be matched to the scene draft. This is classified under Feature `1.5` because the author reviews the imported records as metadata folders, while extraction is implemented through the Feature `8.6` Scrivener port.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 5 service-call internalization by moving Local AI title endpoint payload construction, default generation policy, title sanitization, and unavailable-provider mapping into `features/local-ai/local-ai-title-service.js`; project-source desktop loading, imported library normalization, merge, active-project selection, and save orchestration now live in `adapters/storage/project-source-service.js`. `app.js` still decides when to request these workflows and how to render/activate accepted results.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued service-call internalization by moving anchored task/note collection mutations, workflow dirty reasons, and persistence callback calls into `features/anchored-records/anchored-record-service.js`; narration/voice recording media save/load endpoint calls and blob/base64 conversion now live in `features/narration/narration-media-service.js`. `app.js` still owns DOM reads, recorder lifecycle, audio preview URL lifecycle, and render scheduling.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 voice extraction by moving editor voice profile/job normalization, placeholder render-job transitions, and voice narration preference snapshot load/save into `features/voice/voice-workflow-service.js`; saved voice recording preview audio/object-URL lifecycle now lives in `features/voice/voice-recording-preview-service.js`. The shell still owns voice surface event routing and full narration recorder/speech-recognition lifecycle.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving recorder runtime cleanup into `features/narration/narration-recording-runtime-service.js`. Normal recording finalization, failed-start aborts, and project activation teardown now share the same service-owned release path for recorder timers, speech recognition, and microphone streams while `app.js` still owns start policy, transcript event handling, and final take-record assembly.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving narration take DTO construction, transcript/status normalization, elapsed labels, MIME fallback, recording IDs, media filenames/paths, and final saved/failed recording records into `features/narration/narration-take-service.js`. The shell now calls feature-owned helpers while it continues to coordinate browser recording start/stop and persistence/render side effects.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving MediaRecorder construction/event handling into `features/narration/narration-media-recorder-service.js` and Web Speech API tracker setup/event interpretation into `features/narration/narration-speech-recognition-service.js`. The shell still sequences microphone permission, recording start/stop, final persistence, and renders, but recorder chunks/errors/stops and speech transcripts/errors/paused state now pass through feature-owned services.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving final recording blob construction, finalization context derivation, and saved/failed take record assembly into `features/narration/narration-take-service.js`. `app.js` now performs the project-media save and persistence/render side effects while final take metadata remains service-owned.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving initial narration recording runtime construction and initial "requesting microphone" session options into `features/narration/narration-take-service.js`. `app.js` still starts browser timers and requests microphone access, but it no longer hand-builds the runtime/session DTO.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving recording start/stop command sequencing into `features/narration/narration-recording-command-service.js`. The service now owns active-runtime guards, no-selection and missing-capability paused sessions, microphone request order, recorder and speech tracker attachment, recorder start, stop eligibility, and stop fallback finalization through injected shell/browser callbacks.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving stopped-runtime cleanup, final media-save result mapping, saved/failed take record creation, final paused session options, and recording failure logs into `features/narration/narration-recording-finalization-service.js`. `app.js` now commits the returned record to project state and schedules persistence.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 voice/narration extraction by moving saved recording collection initialization, active-project filtering, lookup, and upsert mutation into `features/voice/voice-recording-service.js`. Preview/open actions and final take commits now call the feature service rather than manipulating `workspace.voice.recordings` directly in `app.js`.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 voice/narration extraction by moving saved recording preview orchestration and manuscript verse navigation planning into `features/voice/voice-recording-action-service.js`. `app.js` now applies the returned scene/block selection and render only after the feature service resolves the recording anchor.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration extraction by moving armed verse selection derivation into `features/narration/narration-selection-service.js`. Scene/block/default selection and textarea context selection now produce feature-owned selection records while `app.js` keeps DOM reads, selected-state assignment, and render effects.
- Process update (2026-05-31): continued Phase 6 narration/voice extraction by moving narration session, alignment job, saved voice recording, and voice render-job metadata synchronization into `features/narration/narration-metadata-sync-service.js`. Scene moves/deletes still trigger the same synchronization, but the re-anchoring rules are no longer embedded in `app.js`.
- Process update (2026-06-01): added planned Feature `1.11` and updated the refactor roadmap for an app-owned anchor-aware decoration drift pipeline before any editor-library experiment. The next phase will use `Design notes/anchor-decoration-drift-handling-design.md` as the source design: live edit transactions update anchors first, load-time hashes validate stored offsets, bounded context recovers stale offsets, and render-only projections remain disposable.
- Process update (2026-06-01): started Feature `1.11` by adding the first `features/manuscript-anchors` service slice. Anchor DTO/evidence helpers, runtime-only edit transactions, live anchor mutation, hash/context validation, active-scene anchor indexing, and decoration projection helpers now have DOM-free services and focused tests. Current spellcheck underline projections now route through the decoration projection service while remaining runtime-only and excluded from project persistence.
- Process update (2026-06-01): continued Feature `1.11` by wiring live scene text commits into the anchor drift pipeline for offset-backed tasks and passage notes. `ManuscriptInputController` now has an injected anchor-update step before scene draft persistence; when manuscript text changes, the app derives a runtime edit transaction, shifts affected task/note offsets cheaply, and only refreshes bounded context/hash metadata when the edit overlaps, replaces, or deletes the anchored content. Pure shifts preserve existing evidence until lazy validation. The edit transaction text itself remains runtime-only and is not saved as anchor metadata.
- Process update (2026-06-01): continued Feature `1.11` by adding the load/lazy task/note validation path. Project activation and anchor navigation now resolve through `manuscript-anchor-record-service.js`, which can validate stored hashes, recover legacy selected-text anchors through the shared fallback path, emit record patches for bounded evidence/status repair, and mark stale anchors as non-renderable. Newly created task and passage-note records now receive bounded hash/context metadata at creation, while hover previews still avoid persistence writes.
- Process update (2026-06-13): corrected Feature `8.4` after runtime evidence showed a browser-cache fallback was clearing project-file dirty state when the browser rejected the real file write. Cache-only preservation now leaves autosave blocked and the JSON file explicitly out of sync, avoids repeated idle retries against the blocked target, and clears the state only after a successful file write. A passage-note regression serializes an edited inspiration note and verifies its body is present in the external JSON payload before autosave becomes clean.
