- Process update (2026-06-03): completed the current Phase 7 anchor-owner slice for Feature `1.11`. The shared anchor record service now live-shifts canonical `{ anchor }` records as well as offset-backed task/note records; `app.js` maps scene-level textarea edits back into block-local issue, event-tag, narration-session, and narration-alignment anchors before scene persistence. The same canonical update helper and anchor index path are ready for future revision-pass marker records when that durable collection exists. Idle validation is debounced, activation validation covers current anchor owners, and the test harness now covers canonical drift, nested narration anchors, generic revision-marker records, idle scheduler behavior, hash/context recovery, and runtime-only projection persistence boundaries.
- Process update (2026-06-03): continued Feature `1.11` by staging canonical manuscript marks in `packages/manuscript-schema`. `ManuscriptMark` records now have stable IDs, canonical anchors, source metadata, anchor status, bounded evidence fields, and an `addManuscriptMark` mutation with tests; the Phase 7 anchor index accepts `marks` as a named owner collection and canonical drift tests cover `manuscriptMark` records. The browser command path still writes `inlineFormatRanges` until the next migration slice moves author formatting writes and project JSON normalization to canonical marks.
- Process update (2026-06-03): continued Feature `1.11` by moving author-mark projections onto schema-shaped manuscript marks while keeping legacy writes intact. `manuscript-mark-service.js` derives anchor-backed marks from `inlineFormatRanges`, splits cross-block ranges into block-local anchors, uses bounded evidence for long ranges, and reports unmapped ranges back to the legacy path. `projection-selector.js` now prefers explicit `workspace.project.marks`, derives marks from compatibility ranges when marks are absent, and only falls back to `inlineFormatRange` projections when a stable block anchor cannot be created. Scene records and chunked scene normalization now preserve `paragraphId` when available so mark anchors can resolve more cleanly.
- Process update (2026-06-04): continued Feature `1.11` by synchronizing scene compatibility inline ranges into canonical `workspace.project.marks` before scene persistence. `manuscript-mark-service.js` now replaces only `mark-inline-*` compatibility marks for the edited scene, preserves future canonical marks, and reports unmapped ranges for the legacy path; `projection-selector.js` avoids deriving duplicate legacy projections when compatibility marks already exist. Project migration now defaults older save files to `workspace.project.marks: []`, so projection and migration code can inspect a stable canonical marks collection while direct formatting commands are still migrated off `inlineFormatRanges`.
- Process update (2026-06-04): prepared the manuscript marks mutation stage by adding a DOM-free direct mark toggle planner to `manuscript-mark-service.js`. The planner maps a scene selection into canonical `ManuscriptMark` additions/removals, allocates schema-style `mark-0001` IDs from project sequence state, preserves non-target marks, creates cross-block marks, removes fully covered marks, and splits partially toggled marks with refreshed bounded evidence. The toolbar still writes compatibility ranges until the next slice wires commands through this planner.
- Process update (2026-06-04): implemented canonical user highlights without a separate right-console panel. The editor Highlight control writes canonical `ManuscriptMark` records through the direct mark planner, removes highlight ranges from the scene compatibility field, persists marks through the project record path, and repaints the author-mark overlay after layout refresh without persisting runtime projection objects.
- Process update (2026-06-06): repaired the user-highlight toolbar workflow so a selected manuscript passage remains available when the author clicks Highlight. `user-highlight-command-service.js` now prefers the live textarea selection and falls back to the last scene-editor selection snapshot captured on toolbar pointerdown, while rejecting stale cached selections from other scenes. The regression test harness now covers live selection, toolbar-collapsed cached selection, clamped cached ranges, and stale-cache rejection.
- Process update (2026-06-06): repaired drag-selected manuscript Highlight commands through the toolbar path rather than a separate panel. The command routes through the same canonical user-highlight mutation path, and scene-editor drag selections refresh on pointerup before toolbar commands run.
- Process update (2026-06-06): fixed the visible render failure for user highlights. The textarea author-mark overlay was still hidden by `.editor-inline-format-layer { display: none; }`; it now renders as an aligned transparent-text layer so canonical highlight marks show as yellow backgrounds behind the manuscript text. `desktop-application.test.mjs` now guards against hiding the inline-format layer again.
- Process update (2026-06-06): fixed the remaining saved-project mapping failure for user highlights. `manuscript-mark-service.js` now maps selections against the actual loaded editor text even when saved scene blocks use a different separator than the current editor composer, then falls back to a scene-wide stable block anchor instead of returning `unmapped-selection`. `manuscript-mark-service.test.mjs` now covers the Serva Vitae `scene-0023` screenshot passage from `SaveTestFile/OriginFileproject-serva-vitae.abe-project.json`.
- Process update (2026-06-06): added switch-style user highlights for incoming typed text. `user-highlight-command-service.js` now resolves highlight commands into either a selected-range mutation or a collapsed-caret pending-format toggle, and `app.js` routes the pending case through the shared inline command controller so newly typed text receives a highlight range before the compatibility sync promotes it into canonical `ManuscriptMark` records.
- Process update (2026-07-15): fixed a textarea-overlay selection drift in Feature `1.10`. Italic author marks now use metric-neutral range layout with normal-width hidden tokens plus a true italic paint layer, so previously decorated text does not reflow away from the hidden textarea hit-testing surface or read as a faux-bold skew. Author-mark developer logs now include applied offsets plus a short selected-text preview.

- Process update (2026-07-15): refined Feature `1.10` decoration command behavior. Clicking bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, or highlight with manuscript text selected applies that decoration to the selected text and leaves that decoration setting active for future typing and paint-style range application. Active decoration settings stack, so turning on italic does not turn off bold, highlight, underline, or strikethrough. A decoration setting turns off only when the user explicitly toggles that same utility at a collapsed caret or uses a matching shortcut. The manuscript editor handles editor-scoped `Ctrl+B`, `Ctrl+I`, and `Ctrl+H` shortcuts for bold, italic, and highlight.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.10k` decoration mark undo/redo. Selected Bold/Highlight mark commands now capture a bounded runtime history entry with before/after `workspace.project.marks`, mark sequence state, and scene compatibility ranges; editor-scoped `Ctrl+Z` restores the prior decoration state and `Ctrl+Y` reapplies it before native text history runs. The history is cleared on later scene text edits so stale decoration snapshots are not replayed over changed manuscript text.
- Process update (2026-07-15): refined Feature `1.10` author-mark viewport behavior. Applying selected Bold/Highlight decorations, and undoing or redoing those app-owned decoration changes, now repaints the author-mark overlay while restoring the scene editor's previous scroll position and selected range instead of routing through centered anchored navigation.
- Process update (2026-07-15): refined Feature `1.10` grouped decoration undo. Paint-applying multiple active decoration settings to one selected passage now suppresses per-format Bold/Highlight history entries during the gesture and pushes one combined before/after snapshot, so one editor-scoped undo removes the whole decoration group.
- Process update (2026-07-15): added Feature `1.10n` Clear Decorations eraser. The scene-editor toolbar now exposes an eraser control that switches off active decoration paint settings, stays active as its own paint tool, removes author-owned canonical marks and legacy inline-format ranges from selected text, records selected-range erases in decoration undo history, and uses the same drag-selected paint gesture as active highlights.

The highlight palette renders those recent custom colours as selectable swatches; selecting one restores it as the active custom highlight without creating a duplicate stored colour.

Tests now cover the repeated-prefix drift case and verify the corrected range still promotes into a canonical highlight mark and author-mark projection.

The author-mark overlay now keeps base mirrored text transparent but gives text-style mark spans their own ink color, so `.editor-inline-format-bold` is visible instead of inheriting the transparent highlight-layer color. A follow-up centering fix replaced real `font-weight: 700` in the mirrored textarea layer with symmetric text-shadow faux-bold, preserving character metrics so the bold overlay stays aligned with the editable textarea text. Host and stylesheet tests now guard the Bold class, explicit Bold projection, and metric-preserving text-style overlay rule.

The author-mark command now reconciles the visible textarea text into scene draft text and stable draft blocks before running the canonical mark planner, and normalized paste always dispatches the editor input pipeline so pasted fresh-scene text does not remain model-empty. Regression tests cover no-block draft scenes, stale single-block reconciliation, and fresh-scene Bold/Highlight projection.

Before the direct selected-mark command deletes legacy highlight ranges, retained same-kind `mark-inline-*` compatibility marks are promoted to durable `mark-000x` records, so the follow-up scene compatibility sync no longer treats previous highlights as disposable migration output. Regression coverage adds the exact earlier-highlight plus later-selected-highlight sequence.

Active proof-read runs no longer seed coverage from viewport scrolling, focus, caret movement, activation, or paired clicks; normal click-drag text selection adds proof-read coverage, while Shift+drag text selection subtracts coverage from the selected span.

The Proof Read settings header now includes an iteration dropdown, legacy project-level proof-read colours are copied into each run during normalization, new runs inherit the project fallback setting, colour/preset/recent swatch edits update the selected run's `settings`, and draft-proof projections carry the run backdrop colour into the textarea underlay.
- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `1.12q` new-run proof-read visibility. The explicit new-run handler now sets `draftProofMarksVisible` to true when `startNewDraftProofRun` creates a run, so the proof-read underlay is visible from the start of the iteration; desktop smoke coverage slices `startDraftProofRun` to guard the behavior.
- Process update (2026-07-18): refined Feature `1.8f` top-chrome card visibility into per-feature-page settings. `topPanelVisibility` now normalizes to `manuscript`, `world`, and `narration` page snapshots, with legacy flat records migrated across all pages on load. Hide-eye actions, checklist toggles, Show all, and Hide all update only the active page, and the customization popover displays the current page scope so authors can hide writing targets or status cards in World Spine without changing the Manuscript chrome.
- Process update (2026-07-18): added Feature `1.8f` top-chrome card visibility. The proofing strip, writing-metric strip, and status-card strip now render each configurable card with a small hide-eye action. Proof read, Developer logs, and all writing metrics now share one Writing panel checklist opened from either side of the target strip, while the rendered arrangement remains proof/dev-log cards on the left and metrics on the right. The checklist provides Show all and Hide all group actions, and fully hidden target strips render one compact restore-eye target instead of split proofing/metrics controls or blank full-height panel rows. The setting persists through `topPanelVisibility` in project settings, mirrors to the active browser compatibility key, participates in the `app-settings` dirty comparison, and leaves underlying proof-read, developer-log, writing-goal, autosave, and revision data intact. The former Lines card is benched from the live chrome with a `BENCHED:` comment above the disabled render path while the stat renderer remains available for later revival.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `1.8g` optional milestone sound effects. Scene-edit transactions compare previous and current word counts against writing-goal baselines and queue small generated Web Audio tones when a session segment or daily target is crossed. Project Settings exposes a Milestone sounds checkbox backed by `editorPrefs.milestoneSoundEffectsEnabled`; disabling it stops playback before an audio context is created and leaves writing-target, history, and metric persistence unchanged.
- Process update (2026-07-21): restored Feature `1.8f` card-surface right-click routing. The top-panel customization slice now derives right-click context for visible card bodies and compact restore targets, while nested buttons, links, and form fields keep their native/control-specific behavior. This prevents Chrome's default context menu from replacing the intended Show cards checklist on the Writing and status panels.
- Process update (2026-07-18): added Feature `1.12s` selective proof-read iteration deletion. The Proof Read settings Version Data section now renders a checkbox list of durable proof-read iterations plus a Delete selected iterations action, while the existing guarded Clear all proof-read data path remains separate. Selected-run deletion routes through `deleteDraftProofRuns`, clears `activeRunId` only when the active run is removed, preserves non-selected runs and project fallback visual settings, refreshes the manuscript underlay, and persists through the normal `draft-proofing` domain.
- Process update (2026-07-20): refined Feature `1.12t` dark mode proof-read visibility. Durable proof-read coverage now keeps the selected run colour but switches to stronger theme-specific paint in dark mode, using an increased backdrop fill, higher outline strength, and an inset lower edge so covered manuscript text remains scannable on the dark editor page without changing saved run spans or settings.
- Process update (2026-07-21): tightened Feature `1.12t` brightness handling. The proof-read range now derives `--editor-draft-proof-highlight-color` by mixing the saved backdrop colour 60% with white 40%, so dark-mode coverage is visibly brighter than the configured backdrop before the dark fill, outline, and lower-edge paint are applied. The Proof Read settings colour, preset, and recent swatches now use the same 40% brightened preview in dark mode, so settings match the visible manuscript mark without changing the stored backdrop colour.
- Process update (2026-07-21): corrected Feature `1.12t` final dark-mode fill. The dark manuscript proof-read range now uses the 40% brightened highlight colour directly at full fill/outline strength instead of blending it back into the dark editor page colour, so the visible highlight remains at least 40% brighter than the saved backdrop colour.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `1.12u` proof-read selection overlay hygiene. The proof-read live selection CSS now keeps native textarea text transparent when `has-inline-format-projection` is active in light and dark modes, preventing the browser selection layer from doubling up under overlay-rendered italics while preserving the proof-read selection background.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `1.12w` day-mode proof-read coverage visibility. Light theme now raises only the `draft-proof` range fill and outline strength and adds a subtle lower edge, making default proof-read highlights more obvious in day mode while retaining the same saved colour board and leaving the dark-mode proof-read rules unchanged.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `1.12x` Proof Read settings click-away dismissal. The document click handler now asks the proof-read settings feature whether a click is outside the settings dialog, proof-read panel, and settings trigger before closing the window; internal settings controls stay interactive, and the behavior changes only transient UI state.
- Process update (2026-07-29): refined Feature `1.12y` proof-read highlight intensity dials. The Proof Read settings window now receives the shell's resolved Light/Dark theme, renders only that theme's strength slider for the selected iteration, saves normalized 0-100 values against the matching `highlightIntensityByTheme` key, previews existing proof-read marks when a dial changes, and passes the saved intensity through draft-proof projections into textarea CSS variables.

## Feature 02 - Local Writing Assistant

### Feature Definition

Provides real-time writing support by identifying potential issues as the author works, such as awkward phrasing, repetition, clarity problems, pacing concerns, or structural inconsistencies. It can run in a Local AI Only mode, where all analysis is performed directly on the user's own machine using their GPU, allowing the author to receive private, immediate feedback without sending manuscript content to any external service.

### Process Header

The writing assistant runs behind provider boundaries and returns local-first, anchored feedback that can be reviewed without making cloud execution a core dependency.

### Testable Subfeatures

2.1 Local AI provider routing

   The assistant routes local writing requests through provider contracts instead of calling a model directly from the editor. The code selects a model tier, builds a provider-safe prompt, and returns structured fallback results when a local provider is unavailable.
   2.1a Provider descriptors define local model capabilities.
   2.1b Routing policy selects Tiny, Standard, or Large model tiers.
   2.1c Unavailable providers return structured non-destructive failures.

2.2 Local AI editor preference

   The Local AI Only toggle lets the author control whether writing assistance should stay on the local machine. The code persists the preference with editor/project state so UI actions can respect local-first behavior after refresh or project reload.
   2.2a The Local AI toggle is exposed in the editor chrome.
   2.2b Preference state persists with project/editor settings.
   2.2c Local AI UI actions check the current preference before invoking assistance.

2.3 Local AI scene-title suggestion

   The author can request a scene title suggestion from the active scene editor. The code sends scene context through the local AI router and applies the returned title through normal scene-draft update logic so persistence, rendering, and dirty state stay consistent.
   2.3a The scene editor exposes a title suggestion action.
   2.3b Scene text and context are converted into a local AI request.
   2.3c Accepted title output updates the current scene draft title.

2.4 Anchored writing analysis suggestions

   Local analysis can identify writing issues and event candidates while preserving manuscript addressability. The code translates analysis output into issue/event suggestions with canonical anchors so the editor can navigate the author back to the relevant scene text.
   2.4a Rule analysis emits anchored issue suggestions.
   2.4b Event evidence emits anchored event suggestions.
   2.4c Suggestions remain reviewable and do not silently mutate manuscript structure.

2.5 Local AI model library and settings panel

   The author can manage the local model folder used by desktop-hosted AI adapters from a Local AI settings panel. The code scans a ComfyUI-style model-root architecture, reports registered and unregistered model artifacts, persists the model root in desktop-local settings rather than project manuscript data, and exposes browse shortcuts for finding Hugging Face model artifacts.
   2.5a The desktop host exposes a model-library scan route and a create-folder route.
   2.5b The Local AI panel renders the model root, folder architecture, installed/unregistered model rows, validation messages, and model-catalog shortcuts.
   2.5c Model-root changes persist through desktop settings and do not mutate project manuscript content.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented.
- Process update (2026-07-17): started Feature `2.5` by adding a desktop-backed Local AI model library scanner, model-root settings route, create-folder route, Local AI settings panel, model-folder validation rows, and Hugging Face browse shortcuts. Installed model files remain local artifacts; the browser renders the scan result while filesystem ownership stays with the desktop host.
- Repository coverage: local rule analysis provider, provider descriptors, analysis job contracts, anchored issue/event suggestions, Local AI Only settings, provider-bounded Local AI Router service, `llama.cpp` OpenAI-compatible provider route, Tiny/Standard/Large routing policy, selected `Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B-GGUF` via `llama.cpp` as the first lightweight model-adapter target, desktop local-AI HTTP routes, Local AI model-library scanner and settings routes, Local AI settings panel, Hugging Face browse shortcuts, Local AI title toggle, scene title suggestion button, editable task/inspiration/research titles, and top-level workspace pane tabs that replace status-card navigation.
- Next work: add incremental changed-block analysis, connect installed model descriptors into router tier selection, add runtime launch/stop controls, and configure Standard/Large local model adapters.

## Feature 03 - Event Pinning

### Feature Definition

Lets the program automatically detect and mark major story moments throughout a manuscript, such as deaths, first encounters, character introductions, key interactions, and other important plot developments. The writer can also describe a specific event in plain language, and the AI will locate the matching passage in the manuscript and place a user-defined tag directly on the relevant line, making it easier to track structure, revisit important beats, and navigate complex narratives.

### Process Header

Important story beats are modeled as anchored event tags that can be searched, navigated, and reused by continuity and worldbuilding workflows.

### Testable Subfeatures

3.1 Anchored event detection

   Event detection scans manuscript blocks for major story moments and returns suggestions rather than silently tagging the project. The code packages each suggestion with an excerpt and manuscript anchor so it can be reviewed, searched, and later reused by continuity or worldbuilding tools.
   3.1a Analysis detects candidate story events from manuscript text.
   3.1b Event suggestions include excerpt, kind, confidence, and anchor data.
   3.1c Detection remains advisory until accepted into canonical project data.

3.2 Event tag persistence model

   Accepted or seeded events use canonical `EventTag` objects instead of plain highlights. The code stores event kind, source, confidence, and anchor fields so timeline, issue-console, and worldbuilding workflows can resolve the same story beat consistently.
   3.2a `EventTag` records preserve source and kind metadata.
   3.2b Event tags attach to canonical manuscript anchors.
   3.2c Persisted tags can be reused by later continuity and timeline systems.
   3.2d Manual World Spine event tags created from the manuscript context menu persist as accepted `EventTag` records with canonical block-local anchors.

3.3 Event console navigation foundation

   Event suggestions share the same addressable navigation principle as manuscript issues. The code renders event-like records through the console path and relies on anchors to navigate back to the appropriate scene line or passage.
   3.3a Event rows can be rendered beside issue-console records.
   3.3b Clicking an event resolves the target manuscript location.
   3.3c Event navigation avoids screen-coordinate or DOM-only references.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented.
- Repository coverage: `EventTag`, event source typing, local event detection, event console records, manual World Spine event tag creation from selected/right-click manuscript spans, and scene-line navigation.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added manual World Spine event pinning from the manuscript context menu. The same selected-text or clicked-line context used by anchored notes can open a compact World Spine event composer, and saving creates an accepted `EventTag` with canonical scene/block/span anchor data plus legacy line fields for existing timeline reuse.
- Next work: add richer event taxonomy controls.

## Feature 04 - Narration Follow Mode

### Feature Definition

Is a live reading view that listens to the narrator's voice, matches the spoken words against the manuscript in real time, and automatically keeps the current line or sentence centered on screen as the text scrolls smoothly ahead. Designed for audiobook recording, rehearsed narration, and long-form manuscript review, it reduces manual scrolling and page hunting, helping the reader stay locked onto the script with minimal interruption even if they pause, repeat a phrase, or momentarily lose their place.

### Process Header

Narration follow tracks a live read-through against canonical manuscript spans, keeping alignment state separate from editor rendering state.

### Testable Subfeatures

4.1 Narration session service

   Narration follow state is represented as session and alignment data rather than being hidden inside the editor UI. The code keeps audio-session state separate from rendering state so a future live follow engine can pause, recover, and align against canonical manuscript spans.
   4.1a Narration sessions are explicit service records.
   4.1b Alignment jobs use typed request/status/result data.
   4.1c Session state can evolve independently from editor layout.
   4.1d Live transcript fragments resolve to runtime-only follow selections with confidence and recovery status before the editor scrolls or updates tracker display state.
   4.1e Microphone capture and local tracker lifecycle events surface listening, unavailable, paused, recovering, and stop states before or between transcript matches.
   4.1f Live follow matching uses a word-fit metric so near ASR substitutions can still attach spoken words to the correct manuscript verse when enough surrounding words fit.
   4.1g Live follow viewport movement is paced from observed reading lines per minute, catches up when the spoken target is far from center, and applies bounded upward or downward corrections instead of repeatedly recentering every transcript event.
   4.1h Live follow matching keeps separate current spoken-span offsets and cumulative recording coverage, prefers forward progress when repeated wording appears in the same block, and emits gated developer-log traces for alignment and viewport decisions.
   4.1i Live follow keeps follow-scroll as a local editor preference and samples manuscript word progress every three seconds to steer viewport movement from the active words-per-visual-line density, while live cursor projection remains runtime-only.
   4.1j Narration follow now streams microphone PCM frames to a desktop-local `sherpa-onnx` sidecar as the live cursor engine, with provider/session metadata logged through the realtime speech bridge rather than relying on browser speech recognition.
   4.1k Live follow matching uses the manuscript text currently visible in the narration viewport as the primary alignment search band, expands only to nearby blocks and then full-scene recovery when needed, and logs the visible range plus search pass that produced the match.
   4.1l Live follow paints cumulative already-read coverage separately from a more visible current tracking cue, using a non-yellow runtime focus mark for the active phrase so the narrator can see the follow target without creating durable manuscript highlight state.
   4.1m Follow-scroll blocks default backward viewport movement from transient reverse matches, reducing hopscotch jumps while retaining an explicit opt-in path for controlled backward correction tests.
   4.1n Live tracker transcript snapshots update the recording panel in place during active recording, keeping the Stop command stable while still repainting the follow overlay and showing the latest local Sherpa words.
   4.1o When Browser Web Speech is available, it can own the live read-along transcript instead of the weak local 20M Sherpa model, while a cleanup-only desktop tracker still captures local PCM for post-stop Whisper cleanup. If Browser Web Speech is unavailable, the desktop Sherpa tracker remains the fallback live provider.
   4.1p The live follow runtime may calculate display-only lead metadata to compensate for browser speech-recognition lag, but the cyan current overlay, recognized coverage, saved take anchors, and viewport pacing stay tied to the last confirmed transcript match.
   4.1q Live follow treats ASR as a responsive page-centering aid rather than a full transcription surface: Browser Web Speech and desktop Sherpa snapshots are trimmed to bounded transcript tails for alignment, microphone PCM is flushed to the desktop bridge in short background chunks, and the desktop live tracker keeps only one active plus one latest pending chunk so stale cursor work cannot queue behind the narrator.
   4.1r Live follow profiles and avoids the main compute hot spots during longer takes: scene block token ranges are cached, long-block matching searches bounded candidate starts around the active anchor and viewport, viewport word/line metrics are cached per textarea text and wrap width, and bursty interim ASR updates are coalesced before repainting the overlay.
   4.1s Follow-scroll keeps the reader's current area centered without half-page jumps by capping each viewport correction to a small number of visual lines, so a single far-away ASR match can only ease the manuscript toward that target rather than teleport the page.
   4.1t Follow-scroll waits until the live tracked line reaches the current viewport center line before moving the manuscript downward, so an early line near the top of the visible page stays readable instead of being pushed off screen mid-line.
   4.1u Display lead offsets no longer drive the viewport target or reading-rate sample, so starting a recording on the first visible manuscript line cannot hop the page downward before the actually recognized line reaches the center gate.
   4.1v Once live follow has a confirmed manuscript anchor, transcript recovery is constrained to the active block and the immediately following block, with same-block candidate starts bounded around the last confirmed token. Later repeated paragraphs remain in recovering state instead of becoming the viewport target, so follow-scroll and live highlighting cannot bounce to a distant similar line.
   4.1w Weak live recovery matches no longer replace the current follow anchor. Low-confidence matches, short next-block suffixes, and backward same-block repeats are logged as recovery evidence while the cursor remains on the last stable manuscript span, and the aligner keeps searching nearby current-anchor text before accepting a viewport-biased candidate.
   4.1x Live follow coverage expands back to a short paragraph-leading prefix when ASR begins matching at the second or third word. The current tracking span remains tied to the first confirmed token, but the read/saved coverage starts at the nearby paragraph or block start so a recorded passage does not drop initial spoken words such as `Making`.
   4.1y Live follow uses each ASR snapshot's newest changed transcript as a fast alignment pass before the fuller transcript tail, so two-or-more fresh words can advance the current cue with the narrator while the heavier tail remains a fallback for recovery. The local desktop fallback also emits shorter live PCM chunks to reduce cursor latency without changing stop-time Whisper cleanup.

4.2 Anchored narration take recording

   The author can select or arm a manuscript verse and record a take linked to that passage. The code records scene, block, and span metadata with the take so saved audio remains traceable after the editor rerenders or metadata is synchronized.
   4.2a Verse selection arms a passage for recording.
   4.2b Record, stop, and finalize actions create a take session.
   4.2c Saved takes retain manuscript anchor and media pointer data.
   4.2d Finalized takes use the best high-confidence live follow verse as their saved anchor; low-confidence or noisy follow states fall back to the originally armed verse.
   4.2e Saved take records preserve scene start/end offsets so recorded passages can render as manuscript coverage and later support range-level audio editing.
   4.2f Stop-time transcript cleanup uses repo-local `whisper.cpp` against the captured temp WAV, while live tracking remains owned by streaming `sherpa-onnx`.
   4.2g A plain click without a dragged text selection arms the clicked wrapped manuscript line rather than the entire paragraph, while an explicit selection still records the exact selected span.
   4.2h Stop-time Whisper cleanup prefers the strongest installed repo-local model, with `ggml-small.en.bin` installed as the current development default and an `ABE_WHISPER_CPP_MODEL` override available for targeted model tests.
   4.2i Stop recording releases the UI immediately into a disabled finalizing state, saves the canonical long take first, and runs Whisper cleanup in the background using sliding-window overlap chunks whose stitched transcript is retained as cleanup evidence when complete.
   4.2j Re-recording from a saved take is non-destructive and range-scoped: the saved-take card opens the review, the author selects a word span or oscillogram span, and the normal recorder starts a new take anchored to that narrower scene/block/start/end range rather than overwriting the previous media file or recording the whole saved verse by default.
   4.2k Background Whisper cleanup cannot overwrite a saved take's primary transcript when live Browser Web Speech or Sherpa text already exists. The cleanup text is stored separately on the recording record, and reused realtime PCM temp files are reset at session start so stale audio cannot contaminate cleanup.

4.3 Narration recording tools UI

   The narration controls stay tied to the active manuscript scene while rendering in the right-hand Audio metadata tab when Narration + Voice is open. The scene editor remains focused on a selectable, copyable script and render-only audio coverage overlays, while the RHS tab renders armed verse state, recording status, tracker text, and saved take cards.
   4.3a Narration mode displays recording controls in the right-hand Audio metadata tab for the active scene.
   4.3b Recording status updates as the take moves through runtime states.
   4.3c Saved take cards expose Play, Stop, and Delete actions where available; Play also opens the matched recording review above the manuscript.
   4.3d Speech tracker status now reports matched/recovering line state and can center the live matched span while preserving the original armed take target.
   4.3e Visible narration line labels use the same wrapped manuscript display line as the gutter, while saved take anchors keep canonical scene/block/span identifiers.
   4.3f Narration mode shows saved takes for the active scene in the RHS Audio tab with Play/Stop controls and renders saved-take coverage as a dedicated manuscript overlay.
   4.3g Playing a saved take selects and scrolls to the persisted recording span, falling back to the anchored verse/block for older recordings that do not yet have offsets.
   4.3h Opening Narration + Voice temporarily replaces the normal Metadata Console tabs with a single Audio tab so audio metadata and take blocks are not buried below the manuscript.
   4.3i Saved take cards expose a Delete action that removes the anchored recording record from the active project, attempts to remove the local project-media file, treats an already-missing media file as a completed cleanup, and refreshes the RHS Audio tab plus manuscript coverage overlay through the normal project persistence path.
   4.3j The Stop recording command remains visible as a sticky, high-contrast action in the right-hand Audio metadata tab while a take is active.
   4.3k The Audio metadata tab has a compact header transport cluster beside the scene title for record/stop, play-latest, and delete-latest actions, keeps saved-take actions inline beside take text, and reserves a bottom tracker monitor for live Sherpa text versus post-stop Whisper cleanup text.
   4.3l Playing a saved take opens an above-manuscript review strip with the saved take's primary transcript matched back to the manuscript line range, a playhead cursor, explicit Stop playback controls, a seek slider, and clickable transcript words that jump playback to that point in the take. The playhead/review cursor remains runtime-only UI state; word click timing prefers a saved transcript alignment map and falls back to transcript-duration estimates while alignment is unavailable.
   4.3m Saved take card and opened-take titles display the measured wrapped manuscript line range for the saved span, such as `Lines 10-15`, instead of the underlying block line number. Hovering or keyboard-focusing a saved take card marks that take's manuscript coverage as the active narration-recording overlay while keeping the saved take's canonical scene/block/start/end offsets unchanged.
   4.3n Clicking a word in the opened-take review starts saved-take playback from that word time even when the browser delays blob audio seeking until metadata is loaded. The clickable review words are derived only from the saved take's primary transcript; manuscript spans are used only as anchors for labels, highlighting, and re-record range mapping. If a take has no primary transcript yet, the review shows a pending state instead of substituting manuscript prose into the recorded-word strip. Older or unprocessed takes still use a duration-based estimate until local alignment has produced durable word timings.
   4.3o The opened-take review includes a zoomable oscillogram derived from the saved audio blob. Clicking the oscillogram seeks and starts playback from that point, dragging over it selects a re-record region, and light/day mode uses its own high-contrast waveform colours while dark mode keeps the black-green oscilloscope treatment. Zoom, cursor, selected range, decoded peaks, and click/drag diagnostics are runtime-only review state and are regenerated from project media instead of being saved into the take record.
   4.3p Saved-take playback can be paused and resumed with the spacebar when a track is loading, playing, or paused and the keyboard focus is not inside text editing. Pausing preserves the active audio element, object URL, and playhead instead of tearing down playback like Stop.
   4.3q Opening a saved take runs local transcript timing processing over that recording when its saved transcript has no current alignment. The editor loads the take media through the project-media service, decodes the audio in the browser, detects speech-active intervals, asks an available local word-timestamp provider for recognized word timings, persists a `transcriptAlignment` timing map on the voice recording record, and rerenders the review so word clicks seek to speech-bearing parts of the recording rather than leading silence, long gaps, or blank noise.
   4.3r Saved-take transcript alignment keeps a speech-island fallback. Each detected speech segment receives a contiguous transcript word range, words are timed only inside that segment, legacy alignment maps are treated as stale and reprocessed, and oscillogram clicks that land in a silent gap snap to the nearest speech boundary before playback starts. The alignment map remains durable recording metadata; waveform click position, snap telemetry, cursor state, and selection state remain runtime-only review data.
   4.3s Saved-take processing now prefers true word-level timing evidence from the repo-local `whisper.cpp` desktop bridge. The browser decodes the saved take to WAV, the desktop bridge runs `whisper-cli` in full JSON mode, token offsets are parsed into recognized word timings, and the editor deterministically aligns those recognized words back to the saved primary transcript. Directly matched words keep provider timestamps; missed transcript words are interpolated only between neighboring provider-backed words and marked with a lower-confidence source instead of being presented as exact model evidence.
   4.3t Saved-take word timing refresh now treats speech-activity-only alignment maps as upgrade candidates instead of final word timing. Opening a take with a stale fallback map reruns processing so the repo-local word-timestamp provider can anchor clicked transcript words to observed audio-word offsets; if the provider fails or cannot match enough words, the fallback map is still usable but carries explicit provider-attempt metadata so the UI and logs can distinguish estimated speech-island timing from real word timestamp evidence.
   4.3u Narration + Voice renders the central manuscript script as a read-only reference surface. The author can select and copy script text and can still arm narration spans, but typing, paste insertion, or shared input events from that page do not commit scene text changes; manuscript writing remains on the Manuscript page.
   4.3v Saved-take manuscript overlays and Open navigation recover stale or slightly shifted recording spans from the current scene before rendering. The recovery uses saved verse text and primary/cleanup transcript evidence, expands only tiny missed paragraph-start prefixes such as `A bright` or `Making` when evidence exists, and keeps legacy no-evidence recordings on their exact persisted offsets. New recordings also inherit the live-follow coverage-start expansion from Feature `4.1x` before the final take record is persisted.
   4.3w Saved-take review logs when the playback cursor cannot resolve an aligned word and falls back to duration-ratio positioning. The warning includes the current time, selected fallback word, first and last timed words, timing-source counts, alignment provider metadata, and match statistics so the author/developer can tell whether Whisper failed, matched the wrong section of the stored audio, or produced a valid timing map whose first aligned word starts later than the playhead. This is runtime/developer-log observability only; saved take media, transcript, anchors, and `transcriptAlignment` persistence remain unchanged.
   4.3x Saved-take transcript timing jobs are shared and awaited before project saves. When post-stop Whisper cleanup or review-open processing starts a long local word-timestamp alignment, duplicate callers reuse the same in-flight job, and project save waits for that job before writing the project file so a reload does not strand completed Whisper timestamps outside the recording record. The alignment map still persists on the take as `transcriptAlignment`; waiting state and pending job IDs remain runtime-only developer-log diagnostics.
   4.3y The narration-side manuscript viewport includes separate persisted Manuscript and Narration decoration checkboxes in its masthead. Turning off Manuscript suppresses manuscript-origin paint such as author-applied marks, diagnostics, grammar markings, and anchored-record projections while leaving saved-take and live-follow narration coverage visible. Turning off Narration suppresses saved-take coverage and live-follow paint while leaving manuscript-origin decorations under their own switch. Both settings preserve saved take records, opened-take review controls, live follow data, manuscript text, and the Manuscript pane's decoration rendering.

4.4 Narration metadata synchronization

   Narration records must survive ordinary manuscript edits where their original scene and block still resolve. The code synchronizes session and alignment metadata against current manuscript structure to keep take records tied to usable anchors.
   4.4a Session metadata is reconciled with current scene/block records.
   4.4b Alignment job metadata is synchronized after manuscript structure changes.
   4.4c Broken or stale references are isolated for later recovery handling.

4.5 Mobile dictated writing companion (planned architecture)

   A phone-oriented companion lets the author speak new prose while away from the desktop, optionally review nearby manuscript context before recording or when placing the transcript, and accept it into an anchor-backed manuscript insertion target. This is classified under the closest audio/manuscript pillar because it relies on phone microphone capture and transcription, but unlike narration follow it creates new author-reviewed text rather than aligning or rendering existing text.
   4.5a Compact and medium mobile layouts prioritize readable scene context, touch-safe recording controls, and transcript review without requiring desktop side panels.
   4.5b Dictation sessions save audio/transcript drafts through local-first adapter boundaries and identify whether speech recognition is on-device, desktop-local, or explicitly hosted.
   4.5c Accepted transcript text becomes a canonical manuscript edit only through an anchored insertion command and persistence/revision path.
   4.5d Concurrent desktop/mobile edits or unresolved anchors produce reviewable insertion conflicts rather than silent text overwrites.
   4.5e Existing issue, task, target, narration, voice, and world workflows are adapted for phone screens where usable, with dense production surfaces remaining desktop/tablet-first initially.

### MobileFriendlyArchitecture Process Header (Planned)

The mobile companion is a local-first, voice-first writing surface: the writer captures spoken prose through device capability adapters, optionally uses an addressable scene for context or placement, reviews the transcript as a proposed insertion, and deliberately commits accepted prose back to the canonical manuscript.

- Status: Foundation implemented.

- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `4.1g`, `4.2e`, and `4.3f` for the narration testing loop. The viewport scrolls through a paced line-rate tracker with far-target catch-up rather than repeated center jumps, saved take records preserve offsets, Narration mode renders saved-take passage coverage through a dedicated `narration-recording` projection channel, and each visible take card exposes Play through the existing voice-recording preview service. The `NarrationFollowTracker` and `NarrationViewportTracker` developer-log sources record transcript-tail matches, confidence, selected offsets, viewport target/scroll plans, and no-op scroll decisions when those log sources are enabled.

The viewport tracker now accepts a three-second reading-rate estimate from manuscript word progress and the active scene's words-per-visual-line density, continues short scroll pulses between uneven ASR events, and logs speech-result snapshots, reading-rate samples, disabled-scroll decisions, and viewport plans through the narration developer-log sources.
- Process update (2026-07-18): added Feature `4.1j` and `4.2f` as the local-first realtime speech runtime bridge for Narration Follow Mode. The editor now captures microphone frames with Web Audio only, streams PCM16 frames through the desktop `/api/realtime-speech/session/*` routes, uses a local Python `sherpa-onnx` sidecar as the live cursor engine, saves temp PCM/WAV files under `.tmp/realtime-speech/`, and runs repo-local `whisper.cpp` only after stop/finalize for cleanup. The oscillogram now has light/day colours plus selected-region styling, and saved-take playback can pause/resume with Space without releasing the active blob URL.
