The editor now derives a display-only follow lead from the last confirmed tracking end offset, default reading pace, and a capped word lookahead. The faint coverage trail still ends at the recognized transcript match, while viewport pacing remains separable from the predicted lead span so scrolling can stay governed by confirmed reading progress.
- Process update (2026-07-20; revised 2026-08-04): refined Feature `4.1l` and `4.1p` after live testing showed the read-ahead highlight implied the narrator had reached words that were only predicted. The `narration-follow` projection now paints the current cue only over the confirmed spoken span, confirmed already-read coverage remains a softer trail, and display lead offsets remain metadata for future pacing experiments instead of driving the live highlight or saved-take coverage. No project manuscript, take anchor, or Whisper cleanup data is changed.
- Process update (2026-07-20): added Feature `4.1q` after live testing showed follow mode could lag once recording passed roughly 15 seconds. The live ASR path now stores only bounded transcript windows needed for manuscript alignment, chunks Web Audio PCM into short background sends for the desktop sidecar, and bounds the desktop tracker queue to one active chunk plus the latest pending chunk. This keeps the author-facing behavior focused on keeping the current reading area centered; full transcription quality remains the stop-time Whisper cleanup path and does not drive live viewport responsiveness.
- Process update (2026-07-20): added Feature `4.1r` after a compute pass found the remaining long-take lag came from repeated manuscript scans and redundant overlay work after ASR results. The follow aligner now caches scene block token ranges, searches bounded candidate starts in long blocks, and logs search counts plus alignment duration. Viewport pacing now reuses cached word/visual-line metrics for unchanged textarea text and wrap width, recording-session updates coalesce live preview repaints to the next animation frame, and the textarea host skips duplicate follow-layer DOM writes when the live span has not moved.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `4.1s` and `4.2i` after manual testing showed follow-scroll sometimes skipped roughly half a page and Stop could require repeated clicks while Whisper cleanup ran. The viewport planner now caps each scroll correction to four visual lines, keeping the active reader area moving toward center without a single far-match jump. Stop now moves the session into a disabled `finalizing` state on the first click, saves the canonical long recording before waiting for Whisper, and stores cleanup evidence on the saved take after background cleanup finishes. The desktop Whisper bridge now performs cleanup as chunked inference with stride: it writes the full WAV as the durable recording, creates overlapping temporary WAV windows for Whisper, and stitches duplicate overlap words out of the final cleanup transcript.
- Process update (2026-07-23): added Feature `4.1t` after live reading showed follow-scroll could move while the tracked line was still near the top of the visible manuscript page. The viewport planner now receives the current viewport center line, returns a `center-wait` no-op for downward movement until the matched line reaches that center band, and logs that decision through `NarrationViewportTracker`; line progress and reading-rate state still update, but the page does not advance before the reader reaches the center line.
- Process update (2026-07-23; revised 2026-08-04): refined Feature `4.1p` and added Feature `4.1u` after recording from the first visible manuscript line still caused an immediate downward skip, then later testing showed the live highlight could sit on predicted text instead of the transcript words. Display lead selections now preserve confirmed tracking and viewport offsets for the actually recognized phrase, keep predicted lead offsets as metadata only, and let the shell use confirmed offsets for scroll targeting plus reading-rate samples. The cyan live highlight therefore matches the current transcript span instead of a read-ahead phrase.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `4.1v` after live narration testing showed ASR skips could match similar prose in later paragraphs and pull the viewport away from the reader. After the first confirmed live follow match, the alignment service now builds a local follow search window from the current anchor block through only the immediately following block, filters same-block candidate starts around the last confirmed token, limits next-block starts to the beginning of that paragraph, and logs the active search window. Matches outside that window leave the session recovering on the previous live follow selection; this is runtime-only alignment behavior and does not mutate manuscript text, saved take anchors, or project persistence.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `4.1w` after follow-scroll could still jump when noisy ASR supplied a short phrase such as "the captain" that matched the next visible paragraph better than the current read position. The alignment service now scores a candidate and then applies a stable-anchor acceptance guard before it can update `followSelection`: candidates below tracking confidence, weak next-block transitions, and backward same-block repeats are rejected as recovery evidence and written to the `rejectedCandidate` debug context. Rejected viewport candidates no longer stop the search, so a stronger current-anchor phrase can still win from the near-viewport pass. Rendering and scrolling keep using the previous stable runtime span until a guarded candidate is accepted; manuscript text, saved take anchors, audio files, and project persistence are unchanged.
- Process update (2026-08-04): added Feature `4.1y` after live testing showed the follow cue could trail behind normal narration speed. Browser Web Speech and desktop Sherpa snapshots now pass their `changedTranscript` through to the follow aligner; the aligner tries that newest two-or-more-word delta before the full transcript tail, and falls back to the full tail when the delta is too short or fails the existing confidence/window guards. The local desktop live fallback now sends 700 ms PCM chunks with a 950 ms buffer cap instead of waiting for longer live chunks. This changes runtime cursor responsiveness only; saved take anchors, media, transcripts, and Whisper cleanup remain unchanged.
- Process update (2026-07-23): added Feature `4.3l` after narration panel testing showed saved-take playback could be started but not explicitly stopped or reviewed in context. The voice recording preview controller now owns stop/seek/playhead state for saved audio previews, the Audio metadata tab exposes Stop for the active preview, and Play opens an above-manuscript review strip with the persisted Whisper cleanup transcript mapped across the matched manuscript line range. The review viewport stretches runtime play points across transcript words so clicking a word seeks and starts playback from that estimated point; because saved recordings currently persist transcript text and duration but not word timestamps, exact Whisper segment timing remains a future persistence extension.
- Process update (2026-07-23): refined Feature `4.3l`/`4.3n` after review-word playback testing showed some blob-backed audio elements ignored `currentTime` before metadata load and replayed from zero. `apps/editor/public/features/voice/voice-recording-preview-service.js` now keeps the requested word/review start time pending, briefly waits for metadata when early seeking is not accepted, and reapplies the seek in `onloadedmetadata` before playback starts. The regression test uses a delayed-metadata fake audio element so word-click playback is guarded without adding persisted word timestamps.
- Process update (2026-07-23): refined Feature `4.3l`/`4.3n` after manual review playback showed Stop could lose a race against an in-flight `audio.play()` promise and word clicks could move the review marker while media restarted at zero. The preview controller now uses a playback sequence token to ignore stale async play completions, hard-detaches the audio source during Stop/replace cleanup, reapplies the requested review-word time after `play()` resolves, and retries delayed media seeks once the browser accepts them. The shell logs Play/Stop/slider/word-click requests with viewport click geometry and actual audio state, and starts a one-second playback-position telemetry timer while a saved take is loading or playing; all of this remains runtime-only and does not change saved take anchors, media paths, or transcript persistence.
- Process update (2026-07-23): repaired the Feature `4.3n` shell adapter after manual clicking on a review word such as "brave" still restarted audio from zero. The voice recording action service was already passing `recordingId` and `startTimeSeconds`, but the `app.js` adapter into `voiceRecordingPreviewController.playBlob` only forwarded the blob; it now forwards the playback options object as well, so a stopped/idle review word click starts the media at the clicked cursor estimate instead of the take beginning.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `4.3q` so opening a saved take prepares durable transcript word timings from the actual saved audio. The narration alignment service decodes the saved WebM blob locally, detects speech-active intervals, maps transcript words across audible time rather than total duration, stores `transcriptAlignment` on the voice recording record when the transcript hash matches, and the review renderer prefers those persisted timings before falling back to estimates. This first local speech-activity alignment pass prevented word navigation from landing in leading silence, long gaps, or blank/low-energy noise.
- Process update (2026-07-29): refined Feature `4.3q` and added Feature `4.3r` after testing the `(Athos) Mission Brief - The lost facility` take showed word timings could cross long silent gaps and move the review cursor from early speech to the final highlighted word. The alignment DTO now uses schema version 3, assigns contiguous word ranges per speech island for fallback maps, rejects legacy maps so existing flawed records are regenerated on open, validates segment-bounded word timings before use, and snaps oscillogram silence clicks to the nearest speech boundary before seeking.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `4.3s` to replace the heuristic path as the preferred saved-take word timing source. The editor now injects a local `whisper.cpp` word-timestamp provider into saved-take alignment processing, encodes the browser-decoded recording to WAV for the desktop bridge, receives parsed model word offsets, aligns those recognized words back to the saved primary transcript with a deterministic word matcher, and stores direct provider timings separately from lower-confidence interpolated misses. The speech-island timing path remains a labeled fallback when the local word-timestamp provider is unavailable or cannot match enough transcript words.
- Process update (2026-07-30): refined Feature `4.3s` and added Feature `4.3t` after the `John's Dream` opened-take review showed the highlighted word `waking` still seeking to a speech-activity fallback at about 17.5s even though the word falls in the later waveform phrase around 26s. Saved-take alignment now uses schema version 4, treats old browser-energy maps without word-timestamp provider metadata as stale, reruns local `whisper.cpp` word-timestamp processing on open, and records explicit provider-attempt status when the provider fails or returns an unusable map. The regression test pins the `waking` case by aligning a saved transcript phrase against observed Whisper words that hear the nearby audio as `Seeing from what...`; the saved word `waking` is now interpolated only between neighboring provider-backed anchors in the later phrase instead of inheriting the earlier speech-island estimate.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `4.2k` after manual narration testing showed the good live/browser transcript could be replaced seconds later by a poor Whisper cleanup transcript. Saved recording records now preserve the primary `transcript`, store delayed Whisper text separately as `cleanupTranscript`, and reset reused desktop realtime PCM scratch files before appending new audio so stop-time cleanup cannot transcribe stale temp audio. Opened-take review words now come from the primary saved transcript and show a generic pending state if no primary transcript exists.
- Process update (2026-07-30): added Feature `4.3u` after review showed the shared scene editor let authors type into the manuscript while viewing Narration + Voice. Narration mode now passes a read-only flag to the textarea editor host, so the script remains selectable/copyable for recording and span arming while browser typing cannot edit it. The shared manuscript input controller and paste insertion fallback also refuse read-only editor surfaces, preventing scripted paste or stray input events from committing scene text outside the Manuscript page.
- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `4.3g` after testing showed saved-take span navigation could look inert when the author was already on the same scene. The voice recording action service now returns a range-aware navigation plan, and the editor shell turns that plan into an active narration selection plus a focused manuscript scroll to the saved span.
- Process update (2026-07-17): clarified the Narration + Voice pane boundary. Narration still uses the speech-tracker status inside recording tools, but it no longer inherits the manuscript proof-read panel, proof-read settings, draft-proof text underlay, or writing session tracker metric from the shared manuscript chrome; those controls remain manuscript-only until a dedicated narration/voice proofing workflow is designed.
- Process update (2026-07-17): added Feature `4.3h` by moving narration recording status, armed verse, tracker text, and saved-take blocks out of the manuscript surface and into a dedicated right-hand Audio tab whenever Narration + Voice is open. The manuscript scene editor still derives saved-take coverage overlays from persisted take offsets, but Play/Stop/Delete cards and Start/Clear controls now live in `features/narration/narration-metadata-panel.js` and refresh through the console renderer when selection/session state changes.
- Process update (2026-07-18): added Feature `4.3i` so each saved-take card in the Narration Console Audio tab has a Delete action. The action runs through the voice recording action and collection services, asks the narration media service to remove the project-media file through the desktop host, still clears stale records when the media file is already gone, persists the active project record, and refreshes the Audio tab count plus saved-take manuscript overlay.
- Process update (2026-08-04): added and refined Feature `4.3y` after screenshot review showed narration-mode manuscript overlays could clutter the read-only script while saved-take coverage still needed to remain visible. The narration manuscript viewport masthead now has separate Manuscript and Narration checkboxes persisted with the existing narration follow settings key; disabling Manuscript hides author marks, diagnostics, grammar marks, and anchored-record paint without hiding narrated passage coverage, while disabling Narration hides saved-take and live-follow paint without changing manuscript decoration state. Neither switch changes saved take anchors, opened review state, live follow data, manuscript marks, diagnostics, or Manuscript pane rendering.
- Process update (2026-05-28): documented `MobileFriendlyArchitecture` as a planned mobile authoring companion. It separates new-prose dictation from narration takes, defines viewport-based phone/tablet layouts and native capability adapters, and requires transcript review, anchored insertion, local-first recovery, and mobile/desktop conflict handling before implementation.
- Next work: exercise the full desktop microphone loop against a real narration take, tune Sherpa frame size/endpoint settings from `NarrationFollowTracker`, `realtime-speech`, and `whisper-cpp` logs, add pause/recover state transitions, improve spoken-word-to-verse tracking under repeats/skips, and continue splitting the manuscript shell into feature-owned modules so parallel work can happen without editing the same monolith. For Feature `4.5`, begin only after dictation contracts and host-neutral manuscript command/persistence boundaries are defined.

## Feature 05 - Character Voice Narration

### Feature Definition

Allows the author to produce full audiobook performances directly inside the writing suite by assigning distinct voices to different characters and narration roles across the manuscript. The system can either generate speech from text or convert the author's own spoken performance into selected character voices, making it possible for a single user to voice an entire cast while keeping dialogue delivery, speaker identity, and audio production linked directly to the manuscript structure.

### Process Header

Character voice narration maps manuscript speaker assignments to voice profiles and render jobs without hardwiring the editor to a specific speech engine.

### Testable Subfeatures

5.1 Voice profile and speaker binding model

   Character voice narration starts by separating manuscript speaker identity from the audio engine that may eventually render it. The code normalizes voice profiles and speaker bindings through provider-neutral service contracts so narrator, character, and future conversion voices can be swapped without changing manuscript data.
   5.1a Voice profiles normalize names, roles, and provider references.
   5.1b Speaker bindings map manuscript speakers to voice profiles.
   5.1c Provider details remain behind service boundaries.

5.2 Voice render job lifecycle

   Voice output is modeled as explicit jobs rather than hidden async UI state. The code moves preview and chapter render jobs through draft, queued, rendering, rendered, failed, and cancelled states so long-running audio work can be inspected and retried.
   5.2a Preview and chapter render jobs are created from manuscript context.
   5.2b Queue transitions are explicit and status-bearing.
   5.2c Failed and cancelled jobs remain inspectable.

5.3 Voice narration storage

   Generated or recorded voice assets need to remain traceable to the manuscript locations that produced them. The code stores local voice narration records with output pointers and metadata so project media can be reopened or reconciled later.
   5.3a Voice narration records persist profile/job/output metadata.
   5.3b Stored outputs retain project-media pointers.
   5.3c Storage normalizes legacy and current voice record shapes.

5.4 Editor voice narration controls

   The editor exposes voice controls where the author can inspect bindings, create previews, and review saved narration records. The code keeps those controls connected to voice services while avoiding direct coupling to a specific TTS or conversion engine.
   5.4a The right-side voice rail exposes voice narration actions.
   5.4b Saved recording cards display preview/open actions.
   5.4c Editor job helpers create service-backed narration jobs.

5.5 Audiobook recording model foundation

   Audiobook production is modeled as project-owned sections, clips, selected takes, and nondestructive timeline items before those records are wired into the live recorder. The model keeps user-defined manuscript sections scene-aware for playback, stores preferred source recording settings as WAV 48 kHz 24-bit mono, and separates human source takes from future narrator-master, character-conversion, speech-synthesis, and rendered-master lanes so character-specific synthesis or voice conversion can be added without overwriting original recordings.
   5.5a Manuscript audio sections are user-defined records with canonical manuscript anchors and source-text fingerprints.
   5.5b Recording clips preserve take numbers, source media paths, production kind, production lane, and optional character/voice-profile derivation references.
   5.5c Section take state tracks the latest clip, selected clip, approved clip, and approval status separately.
   5.5d Chapter timeline items reference selected clips and store trim, gain, fade, and pause metadata without modifying source files.
   5.5e Playback settings default to scene-scoped preview with a rolling three-item preload budget and explicit continue-across-scene behavior.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented.
- Repository coverage: characters, speaker assignments, legacy voice routing, narration voice profiles, narration jobs, queue transitions, placeholder rendering, local voice narration storage, right-side voice rail controls, speaker bindings, preview jobs, chapter render jobs, the editor Voice Narration foundation panel, saved voice-recording cards with preview/open actions, and the first standalone audiobook recording schema for sections, clips, take state, production lanes, timeline items, playback settings, and legacy saved-take migration.
- Process update (2026-07-29): added Feature `5.5` as the non-invasive audiobook recording architecture foundation. `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts` now defines project-owned audiobook metadata, user-defined manuscript audio sections, human source and derived production lanes, selected/approved take state, nondestructive timeline item metadata, scene-scoped playback defaults, preferred WAV 48 kHz 24-bit mono source recording settings, project-owned recording paths, and a legacy mapper from existing `workspace.voice.recordings` records. The current live narration-follow and WebM saved-take recorder are not rewired by this slice.
- Next work: wire the existing recorder to create audiobook clips through the new schema while preserving legacy saved-take compatibility, add a section/take manager UI, then add safe temp-file promotion and source WAV capture/conversion behind the recording service boundary.

## Feature 06 - World Spine View

### Feature Definition

Gives the author an interactive worldbuilding workspace built around a horizontally scrolling chronological node map rather than flat notes pages. Every scene appears as a primary node on the main spine using scene metadata such as location, child location, orbital position, date, time, people present, scene beats, critical events, and user-created metadata; the scene context menu and temporary node detail card both expose the World Spine metadata entry form so authors can correct missed characters, assign scene beats, or add custom scene metadata from either workflow. Authors can also create worldbuilding catalogue records and manuscript-free timeline events from a minimal Dream Scaping icon strip before a manuscript exists; the active entity/event entry form floats as a compact centered dialog over the visible application shell, then crafted event drafts can be dragged onto the spine before, between, or after existing scene and world nodes. Right-clicking whitespace in the central timeline opens World Spine-only actions for inserting a structured event at that chronology slot or opening a minimum-details prompt before adding another parallel timeline tier, so the author can track what happens across multiple locations or worlds without disturbing manuscript scene order. The prompt now captures only required location, character references, starting marker, and notes before the new tier persists as structured world data; the durable lane name derives from the location, and each split location lane renders a minimal location chip such as `Europa` or `Insert Location Name`. Named location rows can also use a matching Location or Planet catalogue image as passive left-side reference art, stored as local project-media references for new uploads, lazy-loaded by URL, and floating beneath the row banner and behind event nodes. Right-clicking an event-like World Spine node opens a manual catalogue-assignment menu; choosing Ships expands the ship/vehicle catalogue and assigning one records a reviewed timeline-presence link for that event until later AI passes can infer the same relationship from anchored passages. Primary scene event blocks can also be dragged before or after other primary scene blocks to rearrange the manuscript scene order through the same binder persistence path. Scene beats, important in-scene events, and location changes branch from their parent scene as smaller nodes, and world information such as location or character records appears as smaller dashed reference nodes. The timeline can split each spine into location rows, letting the author see what happens where without changing chronology or manuscript order; naming a row or dropping an event into a named row updates the event's broad stored `location`, while finer ship, station, orbit, or facility detail stays in separate child-location/orbital metadata. The left event rail groups selectable story events under manuscript chapter separators, the compact manuscript pane stays fixed, and only the central timeline canvas scrolls horizontally by default; holding Shift while wheeling over the World Spine surface temporarily switches the same gesture to vertical scrolling for stacked timeline tiers and location rows. The author can resize those fixed rails with per-project, per-window-size settings. The central event pane supports Ctrl+wheel zooming so authors can magnify or compress the visible node map without changing structured world data. The central event pane keeps a fixed corner anchor that names the manuscript chapter and scene represented near the current horizontal viewport. Keyboard-focusing or explicitly selecting a node can open a temporary, interactive event-detail card beside that node, replacing the permanent inspector panel; plain pointer hover does not open cards so connector dots remain easy to target.

### Process Header

The world spine view represents chronology, location, passage links, and causality as structured scene, event, reference, spine, node, and edge records rather than flat notes.

- Process update (2026-07-30): added Feature `6.3x` RHS event manuscript section. The World Spine right pane now includes a Section mode beside Passages and Cards; selecting a timeline event and switching to Section shows the event's anchored manuscript context with the exact source block highlighted. This is render-only scene/block projection plus project-settings mode persistence, not a mutation of manuscript text, world nodes, or catalogue data.
- Process update (2026-07-30): added Feature `6.3aa` World Spine location filter pane. The header action area now opens a compact Timeline Filter checklist of derived location rows; filtering to one location fades other timeline rows/nodes/drop zones, recenters the central viewport on the selected location, and keeps cross-location implication links clickable so following an implication pivots the filter to the target event's location. The filter selection persists as project UI settings under `worldSpineLocationFilter`; it does not mutate `workspace.world`, manuscript scene text, timeline nodes, or timeline edges.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined Feature `6.3aa` World Spine location filter viewport fitting. When the author filters to one or two location timelines, the central canvas now uses a render-only fitted projection that places only the selected rows in the viewport's primary row positions and hides nonselected rows instead of preserving the full vertical row stack. Pointer hit-testing, right-click insert slots, drop zones, and node selection use the same fitted projection while `workspace.world`, scene metadata, timeline nodes, and timeline edges remain unchanged.
- Process update (2026-07-30): refined Feature `6.3r` inserted timeline events. Saving a new event from the World Spine Insert event here workflow now creates a manuscript scene draft at the clicked timeline slot, copies the event's title, synopsis, location, time, people, and critical-event metadata into scene World Spine metadata, leaves the manuscript body blank for the author, and anchors the saved world node to the draft scene's first block so the event is immediately navigable from the timeline, RHS Section pane, and manuscript scene order.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined Feature `6.3ad` World Spine location row imagery. The Location row form now accepts PNG/JPG/WebP/GIF images up to 10 MB, writes new uploads to local project media under `assets/images/worldbuilding/...` when a project path or root is available, stores only `WorldCatalogueImage` reference metadata on the matching Location/Planet entity, and renders the image through a lazy host-explicit `/api/project-media/file/...` URL while legacy inline data-URL images still render.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined Feature `6.3ad` location row image placement. Location row labels and their assigned reference images now float at the left edge of the World Spine timeline while the author scrolls horizontally, and each semi-transparent image is clipped to the row's available lane space beneath the location banner so it stays behind events without bleeding into the next location row.
- Process update (2026-08-12): added Feature `6.3ae` World Spine location-row assignment and child locations. Named rows persist `location`, `locationRowLabel`, `locationRowKey`, and `locationScope` together on scene and world-node records, while child-location/orbital fields remain available for finer places such as ships, facilities, or orbits under the broad row location.
- Process update (2026-08-26): refined Feature `6.3ae` with the floating Unplaced events dock. Author workflow: events with the default row identity render once in a collapsible viewport-bottom dock, and deleting a named real row remains available whether that row is empty or populated; populated-row deletion confirms before moving its scene/world members there without deleting manuscript, catalogue, or graph data. Service ownership: `world-spine-panel.js` owns partition/layout/render projection, focused row/world services own unplacement fields, and `app.js` only coordinates confirmation, project persistence, one history transaction, and the persisted collapse setting.
- Process update (2026-08-29): hardened Feature `6.3ae` across chunked browser reloads. The complete `projectIndex` now retains non-body scene placement metadata and supplies a metadata-only `loadedProjectSceneStore` projection while only the active scene body is hydrated; named rows and non-active event placements therefore survive refresh without eager manuscript loading. Delete eligibility now matches scene-only, primary-event, and world-only populated rows, and a scene-card location drop forwards its explicit changed scene IDs through binder-move persistence. Project and writing-goal totals now resolve from the complete index plus actual live body overrides in the focused `project-metrics.js` module, so hydration alone cannot collapse the manuscript count.
- Process update (2026-09-02): hardened Feature `6.3ae` row-assignment persistence during overlapping saves. If an explicit World Spine flush arrives while a project-file write is already in flight, the autosave controller records that request and immediately writes the accumulated dirty snapshot after the current save finishes; successive scene placements therefore survive together instead of waiting for or losing a later timer cycle. The controller remains behind `ProjectPersistenceService`, and project/service storage tests reconstruct all assigned rows after reload.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Feature `6.3ae` World Spine row/event location persistence. Node selection, filtering, fitted-row projection, and drop-zone generation read row identity fields for lane placement; row naming and named-row drops now write that row name back into the event's stored `location`, and the unnamed prompt row is never stored as the literal event location.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Feature `6.3ae` row renaming to synchronize scene-backed event data objects. A row save now writes the row location to each affected scene draft, live scene record, anchored `EventTag.location`, matching structure draft scene, and retained scene-store chunk before the project persistence boundary runs, and World Spine rendering rebuilds from a draft-overlay scene snapshot so stale imported scene mirrors cannot relabel cards as `Unplaced location`.
- Process update (2026-08-14): tightened Feature `6.3ae` project-file persistence for row/event location DTOs. Scene World Spine drafts now mirror child-location aliases, legacy sublocation, orbital, and row identity fields at the top DTO level as well as inside `worldSpineMetadata`, and row rename/drop mutations request an immediate `ProjectPersistenceService` autosave flush after the canonical project record is updated so browser refresh or reopen cannot reload an older unnamed-location JSON payload.
- Process update (2026-08-14): hardened Feature `6.3ae` row-location persistence after runtime autosave logs showed metadata-only row updates could collapse retained manuscript body chunks. Row-location scene-store upserts, browser-cache changed-scene writes, and project-file snapshot assembly now merge metadata-only location DTOs onto existing or line-backed scene bodies instead of replacing `editorText` and `blocks`, so renaming a row can persist event locations without causing the manuscript-body safety guard to block the save.
- Process update (2026-08-14): hardened Feature `6.3ae` reload row identity canonicalization. World Spine row grouping, filter fitting, label/drop-zone datasets, and scene-card row drops now derive the durable row key from a named row label or event main location when older DTOs still contain stale prompt/default keys such as `unplaced-location`, and the row-assignment write helper normalizes those stale keys before scene/event DTOs are saved.
- Process update (2026-08-14): closed Feature `6.3ae` split-storage reload gap. World Spine render refresh and row-save mutation now overlay metadata-only runtime scene drafts onto the retained loaded scene-store bodies before building scene records, and direct browser-handle/file-path save helpers use the same recovery-capable project snapshot builder as autosave/manual save, so a renamed row cannot be left only in browser memory because a blank-body draft mirror tripped the persistence guard.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Features `6.3w` and `6.3ae` event location card semantics. Cards mode now treats the selected event's row name as the main `Location` and the event's finer place as `Child location`; place-like catalogue matches are scoped to that saved location hierarchy instead of every place name mentioned in the prose, row renames prune stale place presence links from affected event nodes, and the Location card can open the existing World Spine metadata editor for adding or correcting a child location.
- Process update (2026-08-14): hardened Feature `6.3ae` scene DTO autosave forcing. `ProjectPersistenceService` now treats explicit changed scene IDs from World Spine row renames/drops as a persistence write signal even when the project-record comparison itself appears unchanged, so scene-store-only row repairs still mark autosave dirty and flush to the project JSON.
- Process update (2026-08-14): prepared Feature `6.3ae` for first-class child-location implementation by adding a canonical `TimelineNodeLocationPlacement` DTO to the world schema and shared timeline snapshot records. Browser-created event drafts now collect Child location and Orbital position, dropped event nodes store both flat compatibility fields and the nested placement DTO, inserted-event scene drafts copy child location, orbital, and row identity into `worldSpineMetadata`, and the World Spine render model can read nested-only placement records without requiring legacy flattened fields.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Features `6.3w` and `6.3ae` so the RHS Cards Location group exposes an inline `Add child location` option beside the group count. Saving the small form creates or reuses a structured `Location` catalogue entity, assigns it to the selected event through a reviewed `timeline-presence` link, patches scene-backed event metadata when a manuscript scene owns the event, mirrors placement onto stored world nodes when the event is world-authored, and persists the change through the normal project boundary.
- Process update (2026-08-14): renamed the author-facing nested-location convention to `Child location` across World Spine forms, Cards pane labels, event-draft detail rows, node badge tooltips, and add-child-location status messages. New scene and world-node mutations write `childLocation`/`childLocationLabel` aliases while continuing to read and preserve legacy `sublocation` fields for existing project files.
- Process update (2026-08-14): exposed World Spine category Location-role properties in the Dream Scaping catalogue inspector. Entity catalogue classes such as Vehicle now show a compact `Location role` control with `Catalogue only`, `Main location`, and `Child location`; saving writes `world.locationTheme` and `world.timelineSettings.locationTheme`, refreshes the World Spine renderer, and makes child-location-capable classes feed the child-location picker and the RHS Cards `Location > [main location]` group while keeping their original catalogue class available.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Feature `6.3ae` fixed child-location catalogue data. Every entity catalogue form now offers `Parent location` and `Fixed child location` fields, Character forms also offer `Home`, and saving writes those values into the entity DTO field array while preserving the stable `parentPlace` JSON key. Fixed child-location and Home quick-reference lists are derived from catalogue child-location records and per-item fixed-location fields, then scoped to the selected parent main location so child places from Europa do not appear when the author is assigning a Khepri/Earth child location.
- Process update (2026-08-14): added Feature `6.3af` World Spine workspace-pane persistence. Selecting the World page now writes the active workspace pane to project `app-settings` as `activePane`, so browser refreshes and project reloads return the author to World Spine when that was the last active workspace without mutating manuscript text or world graph records. Boot reconnect also preserves a same-project cached `activePane` when an older explicit desktop project file has not yet written the new field, while project files that explicitly store `activePane` remain authoritative.

### Testable Subfeatures

6.1 Structured world model

   Worldbuilding data is stored as typed templates, entities, spines, nodes, edges, and links instead of decorative notes. The code gives chronology and location stable IDs so world events can be filtered, linked, and reused by manuscript and continuity workflows.
   6.1a World models contain typed templates, entities, spines, nodes, and edges.
   6.1b Timeline nodes and edges preserve stable IDs.
   6.1c World links can reference manuscript and timeline anchors.

6.2 Template-driven entity instantiation

   Templates act as blueprints for reusable world entities such as planets, factions, cultures, or technologies. The code preserves template ancestry, typed fields, and user-edited values so created entities remain structured and portable.
   6.2a Templates define typed entity fields.
   6.2b Instantiated entities get stable IDs and template ancestry.
   6.2c User-edited field data stays attached to the entity record.

6.3 Timeline spine rendering

   The world spine view renders a fixed-left event rail, a fixed-right pane that can switch between compact manuscript passages, a focused selected-event manuscript section, and event-related catalogue cards, and a horizontally scrolling central timeline canvas so the author can reason about chronology visually without losing passage context. The code derives primary scene nodes from scene metadata, creates branch nodes for accepted event tags, user-entered scene beats, critical events, location changes, and linked world nodes, renders dashed reference nodes for location/character/world records, keeps a fixed chapter/scene anchor in the event pane as the horizontal viewport moves, and exposes node metadata through temporary focus/selection detail cards rather than a permanent inspector. The lower chronology tick axis remains available for scene labels, but the canvas no longer draws a separate centre guide line through the empty event field.
   6.3a Primary scene nodes render along a vertically centered chronological event row from scene records and scene metadata, with the lower axis reserved for tick labels rather than a separate mid-canvas guide line.
   6.3b In-scene event nodes and world reference nodes branch from parent scenes with clear size hierarchy and dashed reference links.
   6.3c Focus/selection event-detail cards stay attached to their node, remain interactive, and can open linked manuscript passages without appearing on pointer hover.
   6.3d The compact manuscript pane highlights linked passages and scrolls independently of the central timeline.
   6.3e The scene-list context menu includes a World Spine button that expands into compact scene metadata fields for location, child location, orbital position, date, time, people present, critical events, and location changes; empty fields use neutral author prompts such as where the event takes place rather than seeded manuscript examples, and the people-present field offers existing project and World Spine character names in a type-filtering dropdown while still accepting comma-separated free text. Saving stores those fields on the scene draft and immediately feeds the horizontal node map.
   6.3f The fixed World Spine event rail and linked manuscript pane expose narrow drag handles; completed drags store the rail widths in project settings using browser-window-size profiles, while passive render/window changes restore and clamp those profiles without overwriting them.
   6.3g Scene-list World Spine actions open the scene metadata form for scene-backed records; the form includes people-present fields plus one free-text field for each user-created metadata definition, saves those values under the scene draft's `worldSpineMetadata.customMetadata`, and rerenders custom metadata badges in the timeline model without putting edit controls inside the event summary card.
   6.3h The global side-panel focus mode can temporarily hide the fixed World Spine event rail, linked manuscript pane, and resize handles while leaving timeline data and saved rail-width profiles intact.
   6.3i ManuScriptInfographicLane World Spine markers render as minimal earth icons for accepted event tags, saved world nodes, and scene World Spine metadata anchored to a scene block; multiple markers on one line stack horizontally, and clicking one opens the matching World Spine timeline node.
   6.3j Dream Scaping hosts a minimal World Spine studio as a slim infographic icon strip in the header lane beside Story-Fit Ideation. Authors left-click a small category icon to open a scrollable catalogue of saved items for that category without changing world data, or right-click a category icon to temporarily open compact tab-friendly entry fields in a centered form floating over the visible application shell. The catalogue and entry popovers use opaque surfaces instead of translucent overlays. The catalogue opens and reclamps with its bottom edge above the catalogue icon row, can be repositioned by dragging its header, can be resized from its bottom-right handle, and remembers the last accepted size and position as the user's `editorPrefs.worldbuildingCatalogueBounds` layout preference for the next open. A compact `+` button in the catalogue header opens the same add-new form for the category currently being viewed, such as Ships from a ship/vehicle catalogue or Characters from a character catalogue. The catalogue heading also exposes category properties for entity classes, letting the author save a class as `Catalogue only`, `Main location`, or `Child location` without editing individual item records. Clicking a row in that catalogue opens a detail pane for the selected entity, event draft, or custom category, showing structured fields, notes, source/provenance, and manuscript anchors where the saved record provides them; saved entity and event-draft details also expose compact icon-only Attach image, Edit, and Delete actions in the detail header. Edit reopens the same minimal top-right-dismissable form with the record prefilled and saves changes back to the same stable item ID, while Delete removes the saved catalogue record and clears any reviewed timeline-presence links or node display mirrors that referenced a deleted entity. Attach image validates a PNG, JPG, WebP, or GIF upload up to 10 MB, stores reference metadata on the editable catalogue item record, saves new binary files through local project media, and lazy-renders that image in the left media slot of catalogue item cards. Detail sections separate fields with deliberate row spacing for readability. The number under each category icon is the current catalogue count for that category, using existing Scrivener-derived world records as well as newly created native records; when a World Spine event, beat, reference, world node, or scene node is selected, the numbers and left-click catalogue narrow to items relevant to that selected node. The entry path can create categorized worldbuilding records, define custom categories with reusable field-name rows, add extra custom fields before saving, craft standalone timeline event drafts with location/child location/orbital/date/time/people/deaths/responsible-party/witness/faction/location metadata, and drag small event-draft chips onto the main horizontal spine. Relationship fields across built-in category forms offer typed dropdowns that filter as the author types while preserving free-text entry: character-backed fields include crew, captain, passengers, members, founder, owner/holder/creator-style fields, Home, deaths, witnesses, and responsible party; faction-backed fields include governance, ruling faction, controller, faction control, allies, enemies, affected factions, and vehicle faction; location-backed fields include settlements, Parent location, Fixed child location, and linked location. Fixed child-location suggestions are scoped to the selected Parent location, and linked items and targets can search the full world catalogue. Form placeholders use neutral `Enter <field name>` prompts instead of seeded story examples. Dropped drafts persist as structured World Spine nodes with sequence placement, flat compatibility location fields, and the nested `TimelineNodeLocationPlacement` DTO, while manuscript-linked nodes can expose separate start and end boundary markers in the ManuScriptInfographicLane.
   6.3k Event-draft placement drags show a faint node-shaped copy of the drafted event as the author positions it between timeline events.
   6.3l Timeline event blocks show a faint node-shaped copy when the author drags the block body, while connector dots remain reserved for implication links and never create the faded block preview.
   6.3m Primary scene event blocks can be dragged before or after another primary scene block to reorder the manuscript, writing the same scene-order overlay and linked metadata updates as the Manuscript binder drag workflow. Child, reference, and world-only event blocks remain visual/linking nodes and do not mutate manuscript order.
   6.3n Scene World Spine metadata includes scene beats; saving stores them on `worldSpineMetadata.sceneBeats`, selecting a scene can show those beats in the detail card, and the timeline derives smaller beat child nodes from the parent scene without mutating world-only timeline records.
   6.3o The central World Spine event pane shows a fixed corner anchor for the chapter and scene nearest the visible horizontal viewport center, updating after scroll, wheel-pan, drag-pan, and render restoration without changing saved timeline data.
   6.3p Catalogue category counts derive from normalized `workspace.world` records rather than icon order, including legacy Scrivener template aliases such as `Ship` and `Station`, and selected World Spine nodes provide a transient scope for per-event/per-scene catalogue counts.
   6.3q Holding Ctrl while scrolling up or down over the World Spine panel zooms the central timeline canvas between 70% and 160%, preserves the cursor's timeline position where possible, keeps chapter-anchor and jump-to-node calculations on unscaled model coordinates, and remains transient render state without mutating manuscript or world persistence.
   6.3r Right-clicking World Spine timeline whitespace opens an event-pane context menu with Insert event here and Add parallel timeline actions. Insert event here opens the structured event form pinned to the clicked chronology slot and target spine, then saving places the event directly as a `TimelineNode` and creates a manuscript scene draft at the corresponding scene-order slot; Add parallel timeline opens a minimal prompt for required location, character references, starting marker, and notes, with Location and Characters offering quick-reference dropdown suggestions from matching world catalogue/project indexes while still accepting free text. Saving that prompt derives the `TimelineSpine` label from Location, stores the authored construction metadata, renders each spine as another stacked tier with minimal location chips plus tier-aware drop/context slots, and lets the author add nodes to any tier from the same insert-here flow.
   6.3s The left Story Timeline rail derives non-clickable chapter separator rows from primary scene nodes' `chapterId` and `chapterTitle`, emits each chapter separator only on its first occurrence, and keeps selectable event rows numbered by event order beneath those separators without changing timeline nodes, manuscript records, or world persistence. This prevents stale or non-contiguous scene chapter metadata from making the rail appear to jump from Chapter 1 to Chapter 2 and back again.
   6.3t Holding Shift while wheeling over the World Spine surface routes the same wheel gesture to vertical scrolling instead of the default horizontal timeline scroll, while Ctrl-wheel remains reserved for transient zoom. This lets authors move between stacked parallel timeline tiers without changing manuscript, world, or layout persistence.
   6.3u Manuscript-created World Spine event pins carry scene-derived `location`, and the timeline can split each spine into location rows. Scene nodes, manuscript event pins, world-only nodes, drop zones, and insert-here context menus all preserve the chosen row location so authors can scan what is happening in each place without changing manuscript order or creating separate spines for every place.
   6.3v Right-clicking an event-like World Spine node, including primary scene event blocks, scene beat child nodes, location-change child nodes, and world-only event nodes, opens an app-owned menu with an Assign catalogue item section instead of the browser context menu. The menu initially lists every populated entity catalogue category, keeps Ships first when ship/vehicle records exist, and opens that category's assignable records in a right-side item pane after the author left-clicks it. The right pane focuses a filter input at the top of the list so the author can type a ship, character, location, or other catalogue item name to narrow the records, then click or keyboard into the visible list to assign one reviewed `timeline-presence` entity link for that event. Assignment avoids duplicates, updates the event detail metadata, and persists through the normal World Spine history/project-record path without changing manuscript text.
   6.3w The fixed World Spine RHS pane offers Passages, Section, and Cards modes. Passages preserves the compact linked-manuscript pane; Cards derives event-scoped catalogue groups from the selected timeline node, labels the selected setting as `Location > [main location]`, and keeps the event's finer setting as `Child location` detail rather than presenting main places through storage buckets such as Planets. Place-like cards match the event's saved `location`, `childLocation`/legacy `sublocation`, or linked location fields instead of every place name mentioned in manuscript prose, while non-place groups such as Characters, Weapons, Outfits, and custom pluralized categories continue to derive from explicit event scope. A related card expands only after the author clicks it, revealing its deeper fields while keeping the default RHS pane dense; the Location group heading also exposes an inline `+` action next to the count badge so the author can add a child location directly to the selected event without leaving Cards mode, while the event Location card's edit icon still opens the full World Spine metadata editor for broader corrections. The mode persists as `worldSpineRightPaneMode` in project settings, the expanded card and child-location composer are transient UI state, and the cards otherwise remain render-only projections of structured catalogue items and reviewed timeline-presence links. When the author clears the selected event by clicking blank World Spine canvas, the Dream Scaping catalogue icon strip returns to full-project catalogue totals rather than event-scoped counts.
   6.3x The fixed World Spine RHS pane's Section mode shows the manuscript section for the clicked or selected timeline event. The section derives from the selected node's canonical `primaryBlockId` and compatible manuscript-anchor block IDs, resolves the containing scene, shows surrounding manuscript blocks with the exact event block highlighted, and keeps each block as a normal select-line navigation target. The `Open passage` detail-card action switches the RHS pane into Section mode before scrolling the anchored block when one exists. The selected mode still persists through `worldSpineRightPaneMode`; the section body itself is render-only and rebuilds from current scene blocks plus the selected timeline node in `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/world-spine-panel.js`, with shell routing in `apps/editor/public/app.js` and styling in `apps/editor/public/styles.css`.
   6.3y The Dream Scaping catalogue strip includes a visible RHS custom-catalogue action that opens the existing structured custom category/item form without relying on a right-click gesture. Saving still writes declared custom category schemas to `workspace.world.worldbuildingCategories` and catalogue items to `workspace.world.entities`. Declared custom category IDs are preserved during worldbuilding normalization, so assigning a custom item to a World Spine event shows that category in the event assignment browser with the same type-to-filter item list as built-in categories. Event metadata fields whose custom metadata label matches a custom catalogue category render as free-text catalogue-backed inputs with a category-specific datalist, so typing in a `Religion` event metadata field can search `Religion` catalogue items while still allowing author-entered text. The picker index lives in `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/world-spine-character-options.js`, the Dream Scaping form/catalogue behavior lives in `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/worldbuilding-studio.js`, and the scene/event metadata field rendering lives in `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/scene-world-spine-metadata.js`.
   6.3aa The World Spine header includes a compact Timeline Filter pane that lists derived location rows as a checklist. When the author narrows the checklist to one or two locations, matching timeline rows and nodes remain fully visible and are fitted into the viewport's primary row positions instead of leaving empty vertical space for hidden timelines. Nonmatching rows, nodes, and drop zones are hidden and noninteractive in the fitted view, while the filtered timeline remains a render-only projection over the structured source rows and nodes. Cross-location implication links still use bridge routing for navigation context; left-clicking one selects the implication target, switches the checklist to that target location, and recenters the view so the formerly filtered event becomes visible again. The selected checklist state persists through `worldSpineLocationFilter` in project settings, while filtering does not mutate `workspace.world`, manuscript scene text, timeline nodes, or timeline edges.
   6.3ab Location row chips, including `Insert Location Name`, are clickable naming controls rather than passive canvas labels. Clicking a chip opens a compact Location row form instead of starting drag-pan; saving writes the chosen name to each row event's stored `location` plus `locationRowLabel`, `locationRowKey`, and `locationScope` in scene draft World Spine metadata, live scene event records, anchored manual `EventTag` records, retained scene-store chunks, or world-only node metadata, while empty lanes still update the owning `TimelineSpine` locality metadata. The rendered row carries source node, scene, and world-node IDs only as action routing data, while the persisted change stays on structured scene/world/event records and enters the World Spine history/project persistence path.
   6.3ac Inserted World Spine events materialize manuscript scene shells. When the author uses Insert event here to create a new timeline event, the event save path writes a scene draft into `structureDrafts.sceneOrder` at the clicked chronology slot, copies event metadata including location, child location, orbital position, and row identity into `worldSpineMetadata`, keeps `initialText` blank, selects the new draft scene/block, and writes `primaryBlockId` plus manuscript anchors back to the saved world node.
   6.3ad Location row images persist as catalogue-backed place imagery. The Location row form's Attach image action validates a PNG/JPG/WebP/GIF up to 10 MB, saves new binary image files through local project media, writes reference metadata to a matching `Location` or `Planet` `WorldEntity` by row name, creates a structured `Location` entity when needed, and renders the image as lazy-loaded non-interactive left-side artwork under the row banner so it stays behind events, catalogue badges, and connector handles.
   6.3ae Location-row assignment owns the event's broad locality while child location stays separate. The renderer defaults core row categories to planetary catalogue entities and child-location categories to Location and Vehicle records, and the Dream Scaping catalogue properties row now lets authors persist those category roles per project. Scene metadata can record a broad location, a child location such as a ship or facility, and an orbital position such as low orbit; a catalogue child location with `parentPlace` resolves under its planetary row without a warning. Canonical world-schema nodes can now carry a `TimelineNodeLocationPlacement` DTO that keeps display location, row identity, core location, legacy sublocation compatibility, and orbital band in one structured payload, while shared timeline records expose `childLocation` aliases plus flat compatibility fields for the current editor renderer. The RHS Cards Location group can create a child location in place: the saved value becomes a structured `Location` catalogue entity with a `parentPlace`, a reviewed `timeline-presence` link to the selected event, scene-level `worldSpineMetadata.childLocation` plus compatibility `worldSpineMetadata.sublocation` for scene-backed events, and nested/flat placement fields for stored world-node events. Vehicle and other categories saved as `Child location` feed the child-location picker and can appear under `Location > [main location]` in Cards mode while still remaining available as their own catalogue class. Any entity catalogue item can also store a fixed child-location assignment with `parentPlace` plus `fixedChildLocation`, and Character records can store `Home`; those fields persist as entity DTO fields and their quick-reference lists only surface child places attached to the selected parent main location. Selection rerenders, location filtering, fitted viewport projection, and drop-zone sorting use row identity fields only, while naming a row or dropping an event into a named row updates the event's stored `location` to that row name across the event's scene draft, live scene record, anchored event tags, structure draft mirror, and retained scene-store chunk instead of leaving any event data behind in the unnamed prompt row. Primary events whose explicit row identity is `Unplaced location` are removed from normal location-row projection and render once as normal-sized cards in a collapsible dock fixed to the World Spine viewport bottom; their global chronological X coordinates remain shared with the scrolling/zooming canvas, a real-location filter hides the dock, and the persisted collapse state changes presentation only. Deleting a populated real row confirms before writing the explicit unplaced row identity to its scene/world members, preserving event-local location/child/orbital detail, manuscript text and anchors, IDs, chronology, implication edges, non-place relationships, catalogue locations/images, and empty parallel spines; Undo restores the single captured transaction. Delete remains reachable whether a populated projected row is represented by primary-node, scene, or world-node membership. Project-file export now merges metadata-only runtime scene DTOs into retained scene-store chunks so row rename or unplacement data reaches the saved `.abe-project.json` scene DTO without requiring the runtime draft to duplicate the manuscript body; chunked storage normalization and scene-record rebuilds preserve `location`, row keys, child-location aliases, legacy `sublocation`, orbital-band, and `worldSpineMetadata` fields when the browser refreshes or a project JSON is loaded. The manifest's complete scene index retains those non-body placement fields and projects them into the loaded scene store after refresh while scene bodies remain lazy. Explicit changed scene IDs from row renames, row deletions, and scene-card drops force the persistence boundary to mark autosave dirty even if the project-record comparator would otherwise no-op, including when the same drop also succeeds in reordering binder structure. Empty saved parallel rows that duplicate a populated main-location row are hidden in the render model so dragging an event into Europa does not produce two visible Europa rows, while genuinely empty new timelines remain available until populated.
   6.3af The top-level workspace tabs persist the last active authoring page as project UI state. When the author leaves Manuscript for World, `activePane` is normalized into `projectSettings`, committed through `ProjectPersistenceService` under `app-settings`, and restored during project activation so refresh/load reopens the World Spine page when it was last selected.

6.4 Cross-spine causality links

   Events on different spines can influence one another and should be represented as explicit links. The code models timeline edges and entity introductions so causality, location, and presence can be followed across planets, factions, characters, or story threads. Authors can now drag from a small connector dot on one timeline node to another node, follow a live ComfyUI-style curve between the source edge and pointer target, type the implication/effect in a compact composer, and save that reviewed relationship as a structured `TimelineEdge` with `kind: "implicates"`. Saved implication links render as faint connector-dot-to-connector-dot curves in the event-block field rather than as loud lines on the lower time axis; child/reference endpoints are visually lifted to their owning primary event block, and links that skip over intervening primary event blocks route as above/below splines around those blocks. Hovering an implication line adds a faint glow, right-clicking the line opens Edit/Delete actions, and World Spine graph/layout changes move through a bounded undo/redo stack from the page header or Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y while persistence stays behind the project-record boundary.
   6.4a Timeline edges connect events across spines.
   6.4b Entity introductions link entities to timeline/manuscript anchors.
   6.4c Link rendering foundations expose cross-spine relationships.
   6.4d Node connector dots support direct drag-to-link implication drafting.
   6.4e Saved implication effects persist on `world.edges` and render as labelled paths between event-block connector points.
   6.4f Saved implication paths stay faint and route as node-graph handle splines between the visible connector dots on the owning primary event blocks, avoiding the lower timeline axis even when the durable edge was created from a child/reference node or an older endpoint record has to fall back to the nearest event block. When a saved implication skips later events, the route planner detects intervening primary blocks and moves the path into an above or below spline lane so the connector goes around skipped blocks instead of crossing through them. Routed implication splines now use eased approach/exit handles with rounded SVG caps and joins so the above/below lanes read as smooth node-graph curves rather than squared-off path turns. After each World Spine render, the browser-side interaction controller re-aligns saved implication SVG paths from the actual event-block button offsets, and the SVG overlays are sized to the same pixel coordinate plane as the event block buttons so the visible lines connect to the block connector points the author sees.
   6.4g Active implication drags render only the live node-graph curve so connector targeting stays unobstructed.
   6.4h The implication composer Save action remains first-click actionable after a drag-created link draft; post-drag click suppression only consumes the synthetic canvas/node click and does not block composer buttons or fields.
   6.4i Saved implication links use the connection line itself as the action target: hovering the line shows a faint glow, right-clicking opens Edit and Delete actions, Edit reopens the reviewed implication composer against the same edge ID, and Delete removes only that `TimelineEdge` from `world.edges` while leaving both endpoint timeline nodes intact.
   6.4j The World Spine header exposes Undo and Redo controls, and Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y route to the same bounded history while the World pane is active. The history restores world graph data, implication create/delete actions, inserted or placed event nodes, scene metadata/event-pin changes, scene-node manuscript reorder state, panel rail widths, timeline scroll context, and transient timeline zoom without projecting world proposal queues onto manuscript text.

6.5 Reviewable world suggestions

   AI worldbuilding assistance can suggest templates, entities, and links but must not mutate canonical world data by itself. The code keeps suggestions advisory and evidence-linked so the author can accept or reject structural changes deliberately.
   6.5a Analysis can suggest world templates and entities.
   6.5b Suggested cross-spine links remain reviewable.
   6.5c Canonical world data changes only after explicit acceptance.

Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/worldbuilding-studio.js`, `node --check test/worldbuilding-studio.test.mjs`, focused `runWorldbuildingStudioTest`, and focused `runWorldSpinePanelTest`.
