## Feature 07 - Dream Scaping

### Feature Definition

Dream Scaping is an outlier feature for moments where the writer has an idea or scene they feel is powerful and wants to integrate it into the story. Dream Scaping uses the AI assistant to inspect the overarching story and suggest how the idea might fit into the current manuscript. Later iterations can work with the worldbuilding spine.

### Process Header

Dream Scaping lets an author submit a powerful loose idea or scene and receive reviewable story-fit proposals against the current manuscript and world spine evidence.

### Testable Subfeatures

7.1 Dream-scaping request and result contracts

   Dream Scaping starts with a loose author idea and turns it into structured suggestion data. The code records the idea input, fit classification, placement target, and suggestion status so later UI and acceptance flows can handle it predictably.
   7.1a Idea input is represented as a typed request.
   7.1b Fit and placement results classify where the idea may belong.
   7.1c Suggestion status stays explicit and reviewable.

7.2 Reviewable story-fit suggestion generation

   The analysis service compares the loose idea against manuscript and world evidence before proposing how it could fit. The code returns evidence-linked suggestions instead of directly inserting scenes or timeline nodes, preserving author control over story structure.
   7.2a Analysis builds story-fit suggestions from the idea text.
   7.2b Suggestions include evidence records and placement reasoning.
   7.2c No manuscript or world mutation happens during suggestion generation.

7.3 Dream Scaping panel rendering

   The editor panel presents pending Dream Scaping suggestions for review. The code renders fit, placement, and evidence summary data so the author can inspect the proposal before future accept/reject actions are added.
   7.3a Pending suggestions render as review cards.
   7.3b Fit and placement labels are visible in the panel.
   7.3c Evidence summaries remain tied to the suggestion record.
   7.3d The Dream Scaping panel can host the World Spine studio as a cross-feature authoring surface, so writers can craft structured worldbuilding catalogue items and event drafts from the ideation area while durable entities, event drafts, and placed timeline nodes remain owned by the World Spine/world data model.
   7.3e Saving a World Spine studio catalogue item, custom category, event draft, or insert-here timeline event can play the shared catalogue-item milestone sound when sound effects are enabled. The sound is runtime-only; the durable catalogue, draft, and timeline mutations still persist through `workspace.world` and the normal World Spine history path.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented on 2026-04-24.
- Repository coverage: `DreamScapeSuggestion`, `DreamScapeIdeaInput`, local `exploreDreamScape` analysis flow, dream-scaping job trigger, desktop workspace snapshot, editor Dream Scaping panel, cross-feature World Spine studio host markup, optional milestone sound feedback, and tests.
- Process update (2026-07-20): added Feature `7.3d` as a classification note for the World Spine studio hosted in Dream Scaping. The bottom panel renders the worldbuilding category row, tab-navigable fields, catalogue list, and draggable event drafts, but saved data persists into `workspace.world` as catalogue entities, custom categories, event drafts, and timeline nodes rather than into Dream Scaping suggestion records.
- Process update (2026-07-21): added Feature `7.3e` milestone completion sound feedback for World Spine studio saves hosted in Dream Scaping. Saving a structured catalogue item, custom category, event draft, or insert-here placed event can trigger the shared catalogue-item sound when enabled, while all durable mutations remain in `workspace.world` and the existing world persistence/history paths.
- Next work: add an author-facing idea submission form, accept/reject actions, and optional creation of reviewed timeline nodes or scene drafts.

## Feature 08 - Project Save File Loader

### Feature Definition

Allows a writer to open a local project save file or project folder into this application's canonical saved-project format. The loader preserves manuscript hierarchy, chapter and scene ordering, template sheets, source provenance, and whitespace in the loaded text so the manuscript can be loaded, reviewed, and revised locally without depending on any external source format at runtime.

### Process Header

The desktop project lifecycle lets the author choose a named folder package for New, Open, Save, and Save As; `ProjectPersistenceService` preserves the current authority until the host has written, reloaded, and semantically verified the replacement package. Legacy JSON and browser-file flows remain compatibility paths.

### Testable Subfeatures

8.1 Source project file import

   The author can load a local `.abe-project.json` or supported source project file into the application's canonical project model. The code preserves provenance, source path, retained template text, manuscript hierarchy, and project metrics while leaving the original file untouched.
   8.1a Desktop/browser load actions read project files through controlled routes.
   8.1b Imported records preserve source provenance and path details.
   8.1c Loaded manuscript hierarchy becomes the active project model.
   8.1d Scrivener project packages can be ported from the File menu into a normal ABE project while the `.scriv` package remains source-only provenance.

8.2 Project persistence service boundary

   All project save, load, autosave, import, export, restore, and canonical mutation behavior must route through `ProjectPersistenceService`. The code centralizes persistence so UI features do not write directly to browser storage, file handles, or ad hoc JSON blobs.
   8.2a Editor save/load commands call `ProjectPersistenceService`.
   8.2b Canonical project mutations go through the persistence boundary.
   8.2c Restore-last-opened behavior is owned by the persistence service.
   8.2d The desktop File menu presents a read-only active package location plus `New Project...`, `Open Project...`, `Save`, `Save As...`, and `Port Scrivener...`; package selection uses the app-owned folder dialog rather than an authority-changing path input, while recent projects remain available as a compatibility convenience.
   8.2e Clicking a recent-project row preserves that row's project ID through the pre-switch save of the currently open project, then activates the exact clicked record. This keeps recent-project switching behind the existing project-record activation route and does not add a new persistence path.
   8.2f Project-file saves verify the stored `.abe-project.json` after browser-handle or desktop-path writes by reading the file back and comparing it to the just-built canonical project snapshot before the save is considered durable.
   8.2g New/Open/Save As desktop package transitions are transactional: New and Save As write into host-owned sibling staging roots, the persistence service reloads and semantically verifies before an opaque host commit publishes the requested destination, Save As preserves project identity and managed owned files, and any pre-adoption failure leaves the previous package authoritative without publishing the failed destination.
   8.2h External packages use an explicit current-schema portable DTO: authored manuscript/world/task/note/metadata/revision state and deliberate project preferences persist, while machine paths, provider execution state, live jobs, cache, and transient UI state do not. Existing filesystem components are checked for symlink/junction escapes, and future schema versions are rejected before write or migration.
   8.2i Activating a portable New/Open/Save As project reconstructs safe analysis, narration, and voice runtime service shells after load while retaining durable voice profiles, bindings, and recordings; runtime providers, jobs, sessions, and render state remain outside the portable DTO.

8.3 Disposable browser cache policy

   Browser storage is treated as a temporary convenience cache, not the desktop source of truth. The code clears stale project-content cache when loading or switching projects, repopulates active state from the selected JSON project file, and treats loaded JSON/Scrivener snapshots as authoritative even if old browser scene chunks still exist.
   8.3a Loading a JSON project clears stale project-content storage.
   8.3b Cache hydration does not merge old project data into the new project.
   8.3c Active project cache reflects only the current manuscript version.
   8.3d Authoritative JSON loads and Scrivener ports do not borrow old browser scene bodies when cache replacement is incomplete.
   8.3e Browser refresh prefers the desktop host's explicit last-opened project file over stale browser-cache project paths before falling back to cached browser handles.
   8.3f The bundled Serva Vitae seed path is only a fallback. If browser storage has an active user project, boot does not merge or persist the repo-root seed path over that active project; browser-handle loads clear stale desktop last-file settings before the load completes.

8.4 Autosave and dirty-state control

   Autosave only marks work clean after a real save path succeeds. The code reports save failures, preserves the latest snapshot in browser cache when permissions are unavailable, retains an explicit blocked/out-of-sync state, and waits for a user-authorized retry instead of silently treating fallback storage as a synchronized project file.
   8.4a Autosave calls the persistence service rather than direct storage writes.
   8.4b Failed or cache-only saves keep dirty state active and report the JSON file as out of sync.
   8.4c Permission fallback behavior is explicit, avoids retry loops, and is test-covered.
   8.4d Autosave failure logs include the target project, file path or handle state, and exception message so a failed DTO write can be traced to the file layer instead of appearing as a generic autosave error.
   8.4e Metadata-only scene DTO mutations, such as World Spine row-location updates, preserve existing manuscript `editorText` and `blocks` in browser scene chunks and project-file scene stores; recoverable line-backed body text is merged before save so autosave does not fail on a self-inflicted collapsed scene store.
   8.4f Out-of-sync autosave status carries the originating write or verification failure cause from the persistence adapter, so the header can explain why the `.abe-project.json` did not verify while still preserving the latest DTO snapshot in browser cache.
   8.4g Normal package Save writes immutable generation-specific scene/metadata sidecars, verifies a temporary manifest, and atomically commits that manifest. Project transitions require external durability rather than cache fallback, and Save/Save As retain or flush mutations made while an earlier revision is in flight.

8.5 Project metrics derivation

   Project counts and dashboard metrics must be calculated from the loaded project record rather than stale browser cache. The code derives manuscript, world, task, timeline, template, and note metrics from canonical JSON data so refreshes and project switches show the current project accurately.
   8.5a Project index metrics are rebuilt from loaded project content.
   8.5b Passage-note counts derive from canonical notes.
   8.5c Metrics update after load, save, refresh, and project switch flows.

8.6 Scrivener project port

   The author can choose a Scrivener `.scriv` project package and port its Draft/Manuscript binder into the application's structured project model. The importer reads the `.scrivx` binder XML, resolves Scrivener v3 `Files/Data/<uuid>/content.*` documents and older `Files/Docs/<uuid>.*` documents, converts RTF/HTML/plain text into scene drafts, and stores imported scenes under chapter-aware structure drafts. Recognized Scrivener custom metadata fields become ABE custom metadata definitions; scene-level Scrivener metadata is preserved on the imported scene draft as `scrivenerMetadata`, `customMetadata`, and `worldSpineMetadata.customMetadata`. Scrivener inline comments and footnotes are parsed from `content.comments` sidecars into a generated `Comments and Footnotes` Metadata Console group, with scene folders and manuscript anchors when the matching `scrivcmt://` field result can be resolved. Recognized Scrivener Research/WorldBuilding reference documents under category folders such as Characters, Ships, and Stations become structured World Spine catalogue entities with source provenance, so the Dream Scaping catalogue is populated from the reference file as soon as the import activates. Project-level provenance, editor font preferences, file manifests, counts, and warnings live in `importReport`, `editorPrefs`, and `sourceArchive`. The original `.scriv` package is never used as an ABE save destination; after import, the persistence service immediately opens the normal Save As flow so the author can name and write a `.abe-project.json` project file.
   8.6a File menu exposes `Port Scrivener` beside New, Load, and Save project actions.
   8.6b Scrivener binder order and chapter grouping become `structureDrafts.scenes` plus `sceneOrder`, with manuscript text persisted as scene drafts.
   8.6c Scrivener custom metadata fields and scene values survive project activation for metadata and World Spine workflows.
   8.6d The source package remains untouched and is retained only as import provenance until the author saves an ABE project file.
   8.6e Recognized Scrivener Research/WorldBuilding reference documents become structured `workspace.world.entities` catalogue items with category IDs, source paths, extracted fields, and import counts.
   8.6f Scrivener comments and footnotes are imported into a generated `Comments and Footnotes` Metadata Console group with scene-folder grouping, note provenance, and resolved manuscript anchors where possible.
   8.6g RTF manuscript conversion preserves paragraph breaks as blank-line spacing, decodes Scrivener Unicode fallbacks without leaking fallback bytes, keeps en/em dashes, and preserves visible field-result text so imported first scenes match the Scrivener reading flow more closely.
   8.6h Scrivener project editor preferences map Times-style manuscript fonts into the app's `Manuscript Serif` font option and carry compatible font-size preferences into the imported project settings.
   8.6i After activation, the Scrivener port opens Save As with the imported project title as the suggested `.abe-project.json` filename, writes the chosen project file or download fallback, and reports saved/cancelled/fallback status without treating the `.scriv` package as a destination.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented.
- Process update (2026-09-03): implemented the normal desktop workflow as a transactional folder-package lifecycle. The author chooses a project name and package location before New Project mutates state, Open Project validates a selected package directory, Save writes the established root, and Save As copies managed owned files and verifies the new package before authority changes. The active absolute package path remains runtime/cache context and is excluded from external portable snapshots.
- Process update (2026-09-04): closed the folder-package persistence contract around the real production New Project candidate. Normal Save now publishes verified generation-specific structured sidecars through an atomic manifest swap; New/Open transitions refuse cache-only, blocked, or destinationless dirty state; Save As can establish the missing durable authority and flushes edits made during staging to the adopted package. The folder dialog invalidates stale child listings after direct path edits until Enter/Browse host-validates the location. Portable snapshots are explicit current-schema DTOs, preserve JSON semantics, reject non-finite values/future schemas, and host reads/writes/deletes reject existing symlink or junction escapes.
- Repository coverage: same-origin desktop package browse/create/load/Save As routes, read-only File-menu package location, app-owned folder selection, semantic readback verification, bounded autosave draining, managed asset/transcript/revision copying, portable external snapshots, legacy JSON/browser-file compatibility, and isolated physical package tests.
- Process update (2026-05-14): introduced a browser storage adapter + repository + `projectService` boundary with schema-versioned snapshot migration and a lightweight project index so UI flows can keep evolving while persistence can move to folder/SQLite/native backends later.
- Process update (2026-05-14): migrated persistence toward a chunked project package model where `project.json` holds manifest/index data and scene bodies are stored as per-scene records/files, with legacy monolithic `.abe-project.json` treated as migration input.
- Process update (2026-05-15): project-file save/load now treats `.abe-project.json` as a single active file-backed project, derives the canonical project ID/title from the loaded filename, remaps the active scene store under that identity, and recalculates workspace chapter/scene stats from the project index.
- Process update (2026-05-16): `ProjectPersistenceService` now owns browser file-handle recovery, filename display hydration before manuscript render, write-permission checks for Ctrl+S/autosave, and durable typed-path precedence so refreshes do not silently lose the active project identity.
- Process update (2026-07-17): the desktop project-library seed now hard-checks the real `project-serva-vitae.abe-project.json` file before falling back to generated browser seed data, stamps the resolved JSON path into project settings, and the File-button hover no longer reports a handle-backed loaded JSON file as path unavailable.
- Process update (2026-07-17): simplified the File menu project controls for Feature `8.2`. The menu now has one Project section with a path field, `Load project`, `Save`, and `Autosave`; `Load project` opens the project-file chooser through `ProjectPersistenceService.chooseProjectSnapshotFileForLoad`, `Save` calls the current project save path, and `Ctrl+O` focuses the path field instead of the removed saved-project selector.
- Process update (2026-07-18): exposed the existing new-project workflow in the Feature `8.2` File menu. The Project menu now stacks `New project`, `Load project`, `Save`, and the autosave setting in a compact vertical list; `New project` dispatches the existing `createProject` path, which prompts for a title, creates a blank structured workspace record, activates it, and retargets the suggested JSON path through the persistence boundary.
- Process update (2026-07-18): added a recent-project hover submenu to the Feature `8.2` `Load project` row. Hovering or keyboard-focusing `Load project` now opens a compact dropdown of up to five recent project-library records sorted by project timestamps, with each entry showing the project title and recorded JSON path and dispatching the existing selected-project activation route.
- Process update (2026-07-19): added Feature `8.6` Scrivener project port. The File menu now exposes `Port Scrivener`, which routes through `ProjectPersistenceService.chooseScrivenerProjectForImport`; the importer reads `.scrivx` binder metadata and Scrivener text files into scene drafts, preserves custom metadata/provenance, activates the imported ABE project, and leaves the Scrivener package as a read-only source.
- Process update (2026-07-20): refined Feature `8.6` Scrivener reference import. Recognized non-manuscript Research/WorldBuilding text documents now promote into `workspace.world.entities` as `scrivener-reference` catalogue items with built-in category IDs, source paths, extracted `Label: value` fields, and `worldCatalogueEntityCount` in the import report. These records remain source-linked world data and do not enter manuscript lines.
- Process update (2026-07-21): refined Feature `8.6` Scrivener manuscript fidelity. The importer now keeps Draft imports focused on real text-bearing manuscript items, converts RTF paragraph breaks into manuscript blank-line spacing, removes Scrivener Unicode fallback-byte artifacts around curly punctuation, preserves dash control words, imports `content.comments` records into the Feature `1.5k` `Comments and Footnotes` metadata group, and maps Scrivener Times project preferences to the app's `Manuscript Serif` editor font option. A dry import of the copied Serva Vitae `.scriv` package now starts at `John's Dream`, reports 56 comments, 1 footnote, and 57 resolved comment anchors, and leaves the source package untouched.
- Process update (2026-07-21): refined Feature `8.6i` Scrivener port persistence. After the imported snapshot activates, `ProjectPersistenceService.chooseScrivenerProjectForImport` calls the normal Save As flow with the imported title as the suggested `.abe-project.json` filename, writes the selected browser handle or download fallback, stores the resulting project-file destination in project settings, and reports whether the ABE project file was saved, downloaded, preserved in cache, or cancelled.
- Process update (2026-07-21): tightened the Feature `8.2` recent-project menu interaction. The submenu now stays visible while the pointer or keyboard focus moves from `Load project` into the recent-project list, recent rows wrap long Windows paths instead of truncating them, and the Project status readout can wrap long save paths without changing the project-file persistence route.
- Process update (2026-07-21): corrected the Feature `8.2` recent-project hover hitbox. The `Load project` recent list now opens in normal flow from a constrained hover/focus zone rather than an absolute overlay, so blank space beside the load row dismisses the list and the `Port Scrivener` action remains usable. This is a render-only chrome adjustment; selected-project activation, file loading, autosave, and Scrivener import routes are unchanged.
- Process update (2026-07-23): hardened Feature `8.3` after repeated stale-cache regressions in JSON load and Scrivener port workflows. `project-repository.js` now treats `replaceExistingCache` writes as authoritative replacement loads and skips the normal incremental scene-body backfill from old browser chunks, while the app-level project-source wrapper forwards storage options instead of dropping cache-replacement flags. Regression coverage simulates failed browser-cache removal and verifies the active loaded project does not inherit a stale cached manuscript body.
- Process update (2026-07-23): corrected the Feature `8.2` recent-project ordering after a Scrivener port could be hard to find in the five-item recent list. The File menu now pins the active project-library record above timestamp sorting, so a just-loaded or just-ported project remains visible even if its record timestamp is older than other recent entries. Persistence coverage also verifies the Scrivener Save As path writes the chosen `.abe-project.json` destination back to the active project-library record.
- Process update (2026-08-04): corrected Feature `8.2e` recent-project activation. The delegated `load-project` handlers now pass the clicked `data-project-id` into `loadSelectedProject`, and `loadSelectedProject` captures that ID before `persistCurrentProjectRecord` saves the project being left. After the save boundary resets active selection state, the captured recent-project ID is restored and resolved exactly, so the author loads the row they clicked. Desktop smoke coverage guards the ordering of selection capture before persistence.
- Process update (2026-08-14): hardened Feature `8.2f` and `8.4d` after autosave diagnostics showed project-file writes could fail without proving whether the stored DTO snapshot changed. Browser-handle and desktop-path saves now read the destination `.abe-project.json` back and compare it with the canonical snapshot that contains manuscript, world, settings, and World Spine DTO mutations before clearing dirty state. A mismatch is treated as a failed project-file sync, the latest snapshot is preserved in browser cache, autosave remains dirty/blocked, and the log records the exact target and exception.
- Process update (2026-08-14): refined Feature `8.4f` autosave out-of-sync diagnostics. Browser-handle, desktop-path, and Save As cache-fallback paths now pass the original write or readback-verification error into the blocked autosave state, and the top autosave status note renders that cause so authors can distinguish permission prompts, browser background-write limits, and verified JSON mismatch failures without inspecting raw logs.
- Process update (2026-08-14): hardened Feature `8.4e` after the autosave failure `Project manuscript body store looks collapsed` showed row-location DTO saves were reaching persistence but carrying metadata-only scene records for most scenes. The persistence layer now treats metadata-only scene DTOs as overlays, preserves stored manuscript bodies during changed-scene browser-cache writes, reconstructs line-backed scene bodies before project-file save when possible, and, when an already-open browser session has a collapsed in-memory scene store, reads the current project file to recover body chunks before retrying the save. The existing collapsed-body guard still fires for genuinely unrecoverable empty manuscript stores.
- Process update (2026-07-29): hardened Feature `8.3` refresh reconnect after stale browser state could select a different project file on browser refresh. Boot now preserves a valid desktop `lastProjectFilePath` without syncing or persisting a cached record path first, and `ProjectPersistenceService.restoreLastOpenedProject` chooses that explicit desktop path ahead of `state.projectFilePath`. The browser cache remains disposable: reconnect hydrates from the explicit JSON path, replaces the active project cache, and only falls back to remembered browser handles when no durable path exists or the desktop load fails.
- Process update (2026-07-29): hardened Feature `8.3f` after the bundled repo-root `project-serva-vitae.abe-project.json` path could still win over an active browser-loaded project with the same ID. `loadInitialProjectLibrary` now lets the browser active-project cache win when the explicit desktop path matches the bundled seed path, clears that stale desktop setting before reconnect, and avoids syncing the ignored seed path from the cached record. Project-record merge now preserves the stored destination string before the seed path, and `ProjectPersistenceService.setActiveProjectFileDestination` returns awaited side-effect work so browser-handle loads clear desktop last-file settings before the load flow finishes.
- Next work: surface richer load diagnostics and build a dedicated retained-template browser for loaded source template sheets.

## Feature 09 - External Music Integration

### Feature Definition

Lets the writer connect an external music account as an optional writing-environment companion, browse playlists, search for songs, play tracks through the authoring workspace, inspect tempo reference data, and queue tracks to their Spotify playback session. The integration must remain separate from manuscript, world, narration, and project source-of-truth data: browser playback devices, tempo lookups, and queue history are session/runtime scoped; the refreshable Spotify token cache and the small last-track resume hint are local to the author's browser profile; and the public Spotify app ID is either desktop-host configuration or a local browser fallback outside project snapshots.

### Classification Note

This feature does not belong cleanly to the seven core story-data pillars because it is an external ambience/tooling integration rather than manuscript, world, analysis, or audiobook source data. It is tracked as a separate project-shell/editor-environment feature so future connectors can share the same optional-integration boundary without weakening local-first project persistence.

### Process Header

The writer manages a Spotify-backed writing soundtrack from the top chrome gap beside the workspace tabs without turning Spotify credentials, browser playback devices, playlist browsing, tempo estimates, queued songs, or full listening history into project data. Spotify login persists in local browser storage on the author's machine so reopening the application from a project file restores the account when Spotify's refresh token remains valid, and the last active track/position is saved as a local resume hint so a browser refresh can recreate the ABE player and continue from the prior song position.

### Testable Subfeatures

9.1 Spotify music playback and queue panel

   A compact Spotify strip appears in the top chrome as an optional external integration panel. The author clicks Sign in with Spotify, connects through browser PKCE, searches Spotify's catalog from the strip or popover, browses their playlists, plays selected tracks through an ABE-hosted Spotify Web Playback SDK device, uses visible BPM averages as a writing-tempo reference, and can still queue selected tracks to their Spotify playback session.
   9.1a The Spotify strip renders in the top chrome gap beside the workspace tabs, folds the signed-in author's Spotify avatar into the compact now-playing card when the profile image is available, and opens a popover for the account profile menu, catalog results, playlists, tempo reference, and session queue history; the former Metadata Console Music tab is benched.
   9.1b Spotify authorization uses browser PKCE with a desktop-host or advanced local browser app Client ID, a generated verifier, Web Playback SDK/playback-control scopes, session-scoped PKCE verifier storage, and local-machine token storage for refreshable account continuity.
   9.1b.1 The Spotify redirect URI is canonicalized to the current app origin/path with local launch query parameters removed, so launcher-only values such as `sandpit` or `ubi` do not need to be registered in Spotify's dashboard.
   9.1c Search results are normalized into track cards with title, artists, album, duration, tempo when available, and album art.
   9.1d Queue actions send the selected track URI to Spotify's playback queue endpoint, record only session-local queue history for panel feedback, and translate Spotify's no-active-device response into an author action to open Spotify and start playback on the same account.
   9.1e Spotify credentials, access tokens, refresh tokens, queued-track history, and last-track resume hints are not written into project snapshots, and Spotify is no longer part of persisted Metadata Console side-panel visibility.
   9.1f The panel is sign-in-first: app Client ID entry and redirect URI setup are hidden under the top-chrome `Version: Test` developer-options dropdown, and the desktop host can provide `spotifyClientId` from local settings or `ABE_SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID` so ordinary authors do not need to enter it.
   9.1g In-app playback creates an ABE-named browser Spotify Connect device through the Spotify Web Playback SDK, then sends selected track URIs to the playback endpoint with that device ID so music starts from inside the authoring workspace. The top-chrome player card shows current soundtrack art and title, signed-in status, Start when the ABE player is idle, play/pause, previous, next, and a compact seek slider; SDK player-state updates remain authoritative, while a runtime-only one-second UI ticker advances the displayed position between SDK snapshots.
   9.1h The popover has Search and Playlists source tabs; the Playlists view loads the signed-in author's playlists, renders playlist cards that start playback on click using Spotify's playlist context, loads owned/collaborative playlist tracks for inspection with the same Play and queue controls as search results, and labels followed playlists whose item lists Spotify hides as Tracks hidden instead of implying playback is blocked.
   9.1i Tempo reference enrichment requests Spotify track tempo data when the provider allows it, normalizes raw BPM into a useful writing-reference band, displays per-track BPM labels, and shows a duration-weighted average, median, spread, and stability label for the visible search or playlist track set. If Spotify refuses the deprecated tempo endpoint, playback and playlist browsing continue and the panel reports tempo as unavailable instead of failing the integration.
   9.1j Spotify account continuity is local-machine state: access and refresh tokens are promoted from the old session cache into durable browser storage, refreshed on app boot when possible, profile display/avatar data is reloaded at runtime for chrome and account-menu display, cleared only by Disconnect, and never serialized into `.abe-project.json`.
   9.1k Browser refresh playback continuity is local-machine state: SDK player-state changes, direct track starts, seek changes, and page-hide checkpoints save a normalized last-track resume hint with optional playlist context, playback position, duration, paused/playing state, and timestamp. On boot, ABE hydrates the chrome player from that hint; if the hint was playing, it reconnects the Web Playback SDK device and sends Spotify Start/Resume Playback with the stored track URI and `position_ms`. If the hint was paused, the player card stays ready at that position until the author presses Play. Disconnect clears the hint.

### Progress

- Status: Foundation implemented on 2026-07-20.
- Repository coverage: Spotify Music feature slice, top-chrome Spotify strip registration, app-shell command routing, PKCE/token/search/playlist/tempo/playback/queue service methods, Spotify Web Playback SDK loading and device readiness bridge, light/dark strip and popover styling, benched right-console Music registration, focused service/chrome tests, and desktop route/style regression coverage.
- Process update (2026-07-20): added Feature `9.1` Spotify music queue panel. The panel stores only the Spotify client ID as a local browser preference, exchanges authorization responses into session storage, uses Spotify Web API search to render normalized track cards, and posts selected track URIs to the user's playback queue. Project save/load and autosave remain untouched; no Spotify token or queue history is serialized into `.abe-project.json`.
- Process update (2026-07-20): moved Feature `9.1` to the top chrome gap requested by the author. `renderEditorChrome` now places the Spotify strip between the workspace tabs and Local AI, `app.js` toggles a chrome popover instead of selecting a right-console Music tab, and the old side-panel registration is benched with the Spotify service logic intact.
- Process update (2026-07-20): revised Feature `9.1` to be sign-in-first. The top strip now says Sign in/Signed in, the popover promotes Sign in with Spotify as the main action, App setup holds the Client ID and redirect URI fallback, and desktop settings can provide a public `spotifyClientId` from `ABE_SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID` without writing it to project files.
- Process update (2026-08-04): fixed Spotify setup from launched sandpit URLs. `resolveSpotifyRedirectUri` now strips all query parameters and hash fragments from the app URL before displaying the Redirect URI or starting PKCE authorization, so the author registers only the stable loopback route such as `http://127.0.0.1:4310/` while launcher-specific query parameters remain local runtime context.
- Process update (2026-08-04): improved Spotify queue failure guidance. The Spotify service now maps the provider's no-active-device queue response to an explicit instruction to open Spotify, start playback on the same account, and retry the queue command, while preserving the session-only queue history boundary.
- Process update (2026-08-04): added in-app Spotify playback. The panel now requests Web Playback SDK-capable authorization scopes, can start an ABE-named browser Spotify Connect device, and each track card exposes a Play command that targets the ABE device through Spotify's playback endpoint; the `+` queue command remains available as a secondary action.
- Process update (2026-08-04): added playlist browsing and tempo reference. The panel now requests playlist-read scopes, has Search/Playlists source tabs, loads the author's Spotify playlists and selected playlist tracks, enriches visible tracks with optional Spotify BPM data, and computes a duration-weighted average, median, spread, tempo bucket, and stability label for the currently visible track set. Tempo data is runtime-only and degrades to an unavailable message when Spotify refuses the deprecated audio-features endpoint.
- Process update (2026-08-04): hardened playlist selection after Spotify returned Forbidden for followed playlists. Playlist loading now fetches the current Spotify profile, marks playlists not owned by the author and not collaborative as track-list hidden, keeps those playlist cards playable through a playlist-level Play command, uses Spotify's current `/playlists/{playlist_id}/items` route with the `item` field for readable playlists, and maps remaining 403 responses to a track-inspection explanation instead of blocking playlist playback.
- Process update (2026-08-04): refined third-party playlist playback after user testing showed the earlier Limited label was confusing. Playlist cards now expose separate Inspect and Play controls; Inspect is disabled only when Spotify hides the item list, while Play sends the playlist URI as a Spotify playback context to the ABE Web Playback SDK device so followed/public soundtracks can start inside the application.
- Process update (2026-08-04): made Spotify login survive app/project reopen on the same machine. `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/browser-storage-adapter.js` now owns a durable local token store backed by browser `localStorage` with one-time migration from the earlier `sessionStorage` token key, and `app.js` wires that adapter into the Spotify service; PKCE verifier state remains session-only, Disconnect still clears the stored token, and no Spotify credentials are written into project JSON files.
- Process update (2026-08-04): reworked Feature `9.1` Spotify panel ergonomics. The top-chrome strip and panel account button now render the Spotify profile avatar when the runtime profile call returns one, while Reconnect and Disconnect live behind the panel account avatar menu instead of an exposed account card. Spotify Client ID and redirect URI setup moved out of the user panel into the `Version: Test` developer-options dropdown. The in-app player now shows current track or playlist artwork and title, Play/Pause, Previous, Next, and a seek slider wired through the Web Playback SDK; duplicate now-playing status cards were removed from the popover body. Playlist cards now start playback directly with the playlist `context_uri` and then refresh readable playlist tracks for inspection, leaving hidden followed playlists playable but marked Tracks hidden. These states remain runtime-only and are not persisted into project snapshots.
- Process update (2026-08-12): refined Feature `9.1g` Spotify chrome playback. The separate Spotify signed-in chip and top now-playing status card are rolled into one compact top-chrome player with profile avatar, album/playlist artwork, title, Start, play/pause, previous/next, and a small seek slider. The popover no longer duplicates the transport card and instead stays focused on account, source tabs, playlist/search results, tempo reference, and session queue history. `app.js` now keeps displayed playback position moving with a runtime-only DOM/state ticker while Spotify is playing, so the seek point updates without requiring a click inside the old dropdown player; Spotify SDK state remains the source of truth and no playback progress is persisted to project data.
- Process update (2026-08-12): added local Spotify refresh resume. The Spotify service now owns a browser-local playback resume-state key separate from project persistence, `app.js` saves the current track/playlist context/position from SDK state, direct Play, Seek, and `pagehide`, then hydrates and optionally resumes the ABE Web Playback SDK device after a browser refresh. This preserves the author's soundtrack continuity on the same machine while keeping `.abe-project.json` free of Spotify credentials, device IDs, queues, and listening history.
- Next work: add currently-playing/queue readback, playlist pagination beyond the first page, clearer first-run setup guidance, and a desktop bridge for redirect handling if the Tauri shell needs a custom callback path.

## Feature Implementation Index

This index is the code-map anchor for debugging, extraction work, and the future desktop port. Update it during `finalise work` when a feature or workflow is implemented, completed, meaningfully changed, or moved.

Also update this index immediately when the user says `Feature working`; that confirmation is the trigger that the workflow should be recorded with its current implementation lines.

### Entry Template

- Feature: `Feature N.N - Specific workflow name`, for example `Feature 1.3 - Manuscript spellcheck and project dictionary`
- Workflow: concise workflow name, for example `Scene drag/drop reorder`
- Status: `Planned`, `In Progress`, `Implemented`, `Partially Implemented`, `Needs Review`, or `Deprecated`
- Code locations: `path:start-end` with the main function or block name
- Execution flow: functions/blocks in chronological order from user action to state update
- Flow-on effects: render updates, persistence, autosave, dirty-state, logging, tests, and related service calls
- Extraction/port notes: ownership target such as feature slice, adapter, service, shared helper, or desktop bridge

### Indexed Workflows

- Feature: `Feature 1.10p - Selected manuscript text replacement`
- Workflow: Typed text replaces the selected manuscript passage without leaving the prior passage in canonical scene data
- Status: `Implemented`
- Code locations: `apps/editor/public/features/manuscript-editor/manuscript-input-controller.js:22-146` (pre-input selection capture and text-edit callback context), `apps/editor/public/features/manuscript-anchors/manuscript-edit-transaction-service.js:4-202` (selection-aware replacement transaction), `apps/editor/public/features/manuscript-editor/manuscript-block-text-service.js:40-87` (scene-block reconciliation), and `apps/editor/public/app.js:1037-1113` plus `4048-4062` (shell callback and delegated browser event wiring)
- Execution flow: the author selects manuscript text and types -> the browser's `beforeinput` event captures the selected offsets without preventing native typing -> the textarea replaces the selected text and emits `input` -> `ManuscriptInputController` forwards the captured pre-input span with the updated text -> the edit transaction validates and records the exact deleted and inserted text -> canonical scene blocks reconcile against that replacement while the existing refresh and persistence paths continue.
- Flow-on effects: native textarea undo and IME behavior remain browser-owned; the captured range is runtime-only context, while the resulting scene text and addressable blocks follow the existing project persistence path. Verification: `test/manuscript-input-controller.test.mjs` covers capture/forwarding of a selected-text replacement; `test/manuscript-anchor-services.test.mjs` covers repeated-character replacement transaction resolution.
- Extraction/port notes: selection capture stays in the manuscript input feature controller; the browser shell only delegates DOM events and the anchor transaction service remains DOM-free.

- Feature: `Feature 6.3af - World Spine workspace-pane persistence`
- Workflow: Refresh and project load return to the last selected World Spine workspace
- Status: `Implemented`
- Code locations: `apps/editor/public/state/editor-ui-state.js:4-16` and `69-82` (workspace pane IDs plus `voice` alias normalization), `apps/editor/public/app.js:732`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:14424`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:14617-14670`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:14755-14805`, and `apps/editor/public/app.js:17592-17642` (runtime default, legacy/settings snapshot normalization, project-setting candidate capture, click handler persistence, and event log), `apps/editor/public/state/project-activation-state.js:1-3` and `147` (loaded project pane restore), `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:92-111` (storage-side pane normalization without importing UI state), `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:245` (app-settings dirty comparison includes `activePane`), `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:1452-1507` (boot reconnect preserves cached same-project `activePane` only when the loaded file lacks an explicit field), `test/editor-ui-state.test.mjs:113-115`, `test/project-activation-state.test.mjs:41` and `144`, and `test/project-persistence-service.test.mjs:297-352`
- Execution flow: the author clicks the World workspace tab -> `selectWorkspacePane` normalizes the requested pane, updates `state.activePane`, commits `workspace-pane-selected` through the `app-settings` persistence domain, and rerenders -> `createProjectSettingsSnapshotFromState` writes normalized `activePane` into the canonical project record -> `ProjectPersistenceService` treats that field as part of the comparable app-settings payload and writes browser/project-file autosave state -> project activation reads `projectSettings.activePane` and restores `state.activePane` before the shell renders. On browser refresh with an explicit desktop project path, boot first applies the browser-cached active project, then reconnects the JSON file; if that same project file lacks an explicit `activePane`, `hydrateProjectLibraryFromLoadedSnapshot` carries forward only the cached pane value so the older file does not reset the page to Manuscript before idle autosave writes the setting.
- Flow-on effects: refreshing the browser or loading the same project can reopen directly on World Spine when World was the last active page; invalid pane IDs fall back to Manuscript while the historic `voice` alias still maps to Narration; project files that explicitly store `activePane` remain authoritative during reconnect, and the fallback is limited to same-project boot reconnect rather than explicit user file loads. This is project UI state only and does not mutate manuscript scene records, World Spine nodes, location rows, catalogue links, or timeline edges. Verification: `node --check` on `app.js`, `editor-ui-state.js`, `project-activation-state.js`, `project-persistence-service.js`, and the three focused tests; focused `node --test test/editor-ui-state.test.mjs`, `node --test test/project-activation-state.test.mjs`, and `node --test test/project-persistence-service.test.mjs` passed.
- Extraction/port notes: pane ID normalization belongs to `state/editor-ui-state.js`; the remaining `app.js` code should eventually move behind an editor-shell controller with the same project-settings persistence contract.

- Execution flow: the author names a World Spine location row -> `saveWorldSpineLocationRowFromForm` resolves the row context and creates the broad event locality DTO -> `applyWorldSpineLocationToSceneRows` first hydrates a metadata-only runtime draft from the retained loaded scene-store body, then writes scene-backed event location fields -> `applyWorldSpineLocationAssignmentToWorldPlaceLinks` removes stale place-like `timeline-presence` links such as an old Europa card from affected event nodes when the row is now Earth -> scene-backed events receive the new `location`, `childLocation`, legacy `sublocation`, `orbitalBand`, `locationRowLabel`, `locationRowKey`, and `locationScope` in their body-preserved scene draft, live scene record, matching structure draft, retained scene-store chunk, and any anchored manual `EventTag` records -> world-only row members continue through `applyWorldSpineLocationRowNameToWorld` -> dragging a scene card into a named row reuses the same scene assignment and place-link cleanup before any binder-order move is persisted -> dragging a crafted event draft onto a named row makes `createTimelineNodeFromEventDraft` store the target row as the node `location` and `metadata.location` -> row-assignment helpers canonicalize a named row label or main event location over stale default keys before scene/event DTOs are written -> `persistCurrentProjectRecord` updates the canonical project DTO, passes the changed scene IDs, and passes `flushProjectFileAutosave: true` for row rename/drop placement mutations -> `ProjectPersistenceService` treats those changed scene IDs as an explicit scene persistence signal, marks the project file dirty, and immediately runs the autosave flush through the same save boundary -> direct save helpers also build snapshots through `buildProjectSnapshotForSaveFileWithRecovery` instead of the old non-recovery builder -> `buildWorldSpineModelForState` rebuilds the timeline from a draft-overlay scene record set whose blank runtime drafts are merged over retained scene-store bodies -> row assignment reads and `resolveLocationRowKey` canonicalize loaded scene/world-node row identity before grouping, filtering, hit-testing, or drop-zone generation -> `normalizeWorldSpineTimelineLocationRows` suppresses empty duplicate rows that mirror an already-populated main location -> file export calls `mergeRuntimeSceneDraftForExport` so metadata-only scene DTOs update retained scene-store chunks without erasing body text -> chunked storage writes and reloads those scene DTO fields through `normalizeSceneDraft` -> `buildSceneRecords` carries loaded top-level row fields and nested `worldSpineMetadata` into render scenes -> `renderTimelineNode` exposes the saved row/event/child-location data without treating the unnamed prompt row as a real location
- Flow-on effects: naming a row now intentionally updates all events assigned to that row, and dropping an event into another named location row intentionally changes that event's stored location to the row name across event-facing records rather than only a draft overlay; selecting a node, refreshing the browser, or loading a saved project cannot relabel the card as `Unplaced location` when the scene DTO contains the row location, and stale `locationRowKey: "unplaced-location"` values are ignored when the loaded DTO also has a named row label or main location; the explicit autosave flush prevents an older `.abe-project.json` from overwriting browser-cache DTOs during immediate refresh/reopen tests; saved `.abe-project.json` scene-store DTOs keep full manuscript text while receiving updated `location`, child-location aliases, legacy `sublocation`/orbital data, row key, and `worldSpineMetadata`; metadata-only `sceneDrafts` no longer block row saves or World Spine render recovery when the retained `sceneStore` has the manuscript body; dragging an event from Europa to Earth also removes stale Europa place-card links from the event; the unnamed prompt row remains an empty authoring target rather than a persisted event location; empty parallel timelines remain visible until a populated main row owns the same location key, at which point the duplicate empty render row is hidden without deleting the underlying `TimelineSpine`; catalogue-backed ships, facilities, or other child locations with `parentPlace` can still sit under broad row locations via `childLocation` aliases, legacy `sublocation`, and `orbitalBand`; focused verification includes DTO helper coverage, World Spine source guards, project persistence immediate-flush coverage, stale default-key row canonicalization, direct save-helper recovery, syntax checks, focused tests, and full `npm test`
- Extraction/port notes: row assignment mutation now belongs mostly to `world-spine-location-row-service.js`; the remaining shell code only finds the active row context, writes compatibility caches, and routes persistence/history. Category role editing is now owned by the Dream Scaping catalogue properties row, which writes `world.locationTheme` and `world.timelineSettings.locationTheme` so the World Spine renderer can treat selected catalogue classes as main-location or child-location categories.

Execution flow: the studio form appends standard location fields for any entity class -> picker option sets build parent-keyed child/fixed-child indexes from child-location catalogue records and per-item `fixedChildLocation` fields -> typing in Parent location triggers a render-only datalist refresh -> Save collects the form and `createWorldbuildingEntityFromValues` writes the fields to the entity DTO -> `saveWorldbuildingStudioItem` persists the world mutation through the existing project boundary. Persistence/rendering implications: `parentPlace` remains the stable JSON key while the UI says `Parent location`; `fixedChildLocation` and `home` are normal entity field DTOs, not transient UI; fixed child-location search is strict by parent main location, so unrelated child places stay out of the suggestion list while free-text entry remains allowed. Verification: syntax checks for `world-spine-character-options.js`, `worldbuilding-studio.js`, `app.js`, and `test/worldbuilding-studio.test.mjs`; focused `runWorldbuildingStudioTest`, `runWorldSpinePanelTest`, and `runSceneWorldSpineMetadataTest` passed.

- Feature: `Feature 5.5 - Audiobook recording model foundation`
- Workflow: Project-owned audiobook sections, clips, takes, lanes, and timeline metadata
- Status: `Implemented foundation`
- Code locations: `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:209-224` (preferred WAV source format and scene-scoped playback defaults), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:227-253` (`createAudiobookProjectModel`), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:256-299` (`migrateLegacyNarrationRecordingsToAudiobookModel`), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:303-342` (`createTimelineItemsFromSelectedTakes`), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:346-358` (`buildProjectOwnedRecordingPath`), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:362-403` (`createDerivedRecordingClip`), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:433-489` (legacy section and clip creation), `packages/audiobook-schema/src/index.ts:492-512` (section take-state selection), `test/audiobook-schema.test.mjs:13-167`, and `test/run-tests.mjs:7`, `test/run-tests.mjs:134-135`
- Execution flow: existing saved narration records can be passed to `migrateLegacyNarrationRecordingsToAudiobookModel` -> the model creates project-owned audiobook metadata with preferred source recording/export defaults -> legacy manuscript range data becomes user-defined scene-aware `ManuscriptAudioSection` records with source-text fingerprints and anchors where available -> each saved recording becomes a `RecordingClip` on the `human-source` lane with explicit take numbering and legacy media format metadata -> `SectionTakeState` selects the newest saved clip without marking it approved -> `createTimelineItemsFromSelectedTakes` derives nondestructive chapter preview items that reference selected clips and carry trim/gain/fade/pause metadata -> future character conversion or speech synthesis can create derived clips through `createDerivedRecordingClip` with `sourceClipId`, character, speaker, and voice-profile references
- Flow-on effects: the current Narration Follow, Sherpa/Whisper translation cleanup, WebM recording, saved-take cards, and playback paths are unchanged in this slice; existing `project-media` paths remain valid as legacy clip paths while `buildProjectOwnedRecordingPath` defines the future package-local `recordings/<chapter>/<section>/<clip>.wav` layout; source recordings remain nondestructive because timeline items reference clips rather than rewriting files; focused tests cover WAV defaults, scene playback defaults, legacy migration, failed-take exclusion from selected takes, timeline derivation, character-conversion source references, and project-owned path generation
- Extraction/port notes: this foundation belongs in `packages/audiobook-schema` as a source-of-truth domain package for later editor, audio-service, voice-service, and desktop storage work. The next implementation should adapt recording finalization to emit the new clip model behind a compatibility boundary rather than adding more audiobook-specific state directly to `app.js` or overloading `VoiceRecordingRecord`.

- Feature: `Feature 1.8g/7.3e - Optional milestone sound effects`
- Workflow: Optional milestone sound feedback for writing goals and World Spine catalogue completion
- Status: `Implemented`

- Execution flow: the author keeps Milestone sounds enabled in Project Settings -> scene edits calculate the previous/current manuscript word counts -> `selectWritingGoalMilestoneSoundEffects` compares the edit transaction against session and daily writing-target baselines -> `app.js` queues the generated session or daily tone through `createMilestoneSoundEffectsService`; in Dream Scaping, saving a World Spine studio catalogue item, custom category, event draft, or insert-here placed event calls the same service with the catalogue-item effect after the world mutation and history snapshot are recorded
- Flow-on effects: sound feedback is runtime-only and generated with the browser Web Audio API, no audio assets or project sound records are persisted, the preference defaults on for existing projects, disabling `editorPrefs.milestoneSoundEffectsEnabled` prevents playback before an audio context is created, World Spine catalogue data continues to persist through `workspace.world`, and focused tests cover threshold detection, disabled playback, tone scheduling, editor preference normalization, Project Settings rendering, and harness registration
- Extraction/port notes: sound generation and milestone detection are owned by `apps/editor/public/features/milestone-sounds`; `app.js` only orchestrates user-action hooks and preference checks, while future desktop-native audio output should stay behind this feature service or an adapter with the same effect-type contract.

Playback SDK device, no-active-device guidance, and markup coverage), `test/editor-chrome.test.mjs:99-129`, `test/run-tests.mjs:86` and `443-447`, `test/editor-ui-state.test.mjs:23-110`, `test/project-activation-state.test.mjs:62-128`, and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:76-80`, `428-454`, `855-876`, and `1171-1176` (service, chrome, harness, side-panel state, activation, static route, durable token adapter, playlist playback route, markup, settings, and styling coverage)
- Execution flow: author opens the top-chrome Spotify strip -> clicks Sign in with Spotify when a desktop-host app ID is configured, or opens App setup once to save a public app Client ID as a local browser fallback -> `resolveSpotifyRedirectUri` strips launcher-only query/hash state from the current URL and shows the stable loopback callback -> Sign in starts PKCE authorization with Web Playback SDK, playback-control, and playlist-read scopes -> the Spotify redirect returns to the app URL -> boot exchanges the code for a token and stores it in the local durable token adapter -> later project/app opens call `initializeSpotifyMusicState` and `ensureSpotifyMusicToken`, which read the local token, migrate any legacy session token, and refresh it when near expiry -> the author either searches from the compact strip/popover or switches to Playlists -> `loadSpotifyCurrentUserProfile` gets the account ID -> `loadSpotifyPlaylists` calls `/me/playlists`, compares each playlist owner/collaborative flag to the current user, and renders playlist cards with Inspect and Play controls -> Inspect calls `loadSpotifyPlaylistTracks` only for readable playlists, reads Spotify's current `/playlists/{playlist_id}/items` response through the `item` field, and renders normalized track cards -> followed playlists whose item lists Spotify hides keep the Play control active and show Tracks hidden rather than a disabled playback surface -> visible search or readable playlist tracks pass through `enrichSpotifyTracksWithTempo`, which requests Spotify audio-features, normalizes raw BPM into a writing-reference band, and computes duration-weighted average/median/spread/stability for the panel -> clicking track Play or playlist Play ensures the ABE Web Playback SDK player is loaded and ready, captures its Spotify Connect `device_id`, and calls `startTrackPlayback` with a track URI or `startPlaylistPlayback` with a playlist `context_uri` so playback begins inside ABE -> clicking Queue calls `queueSpotifyTrack`, posts the track URI to Spotify's playback queue endpoint, writes a session-only queue-history row on success, converts a no-active-device provider response into an actionable panel message, rerenders the strip/popover, and logs through `SpotifyMusicService`
- Flow-on effects: Spotify remains an optional online connector outside local-first project data, the `.abe-project.json` persistence boundary is not used for credentials, playlist browsing results, tempo estimates, browser playback device IDs, or playback history, Spotify tokens persist only in local browser storage on the author's machine and are cleared by Disconnect, track-hidden followed playlists can still start playback through Spotify's player context endpoint, the old right-console Music tab is benched and no longer participates in side-panel visibility, the top strip and popover have light/dark theme coverage, and focused/static tests verify the service contract, playlist/tempo degradation, playlist-context playback, durable token adapter, SDK bridge, and static shell registration. Manual smoke checked that the served Spotify feature module at `http://127.0.0.1:4310/features/spotify-music/spotify-music-service.js` contains the playlist and tempo hooks.

- Feature: `Feature 1.2 - Context-aware scene insertion`
- Workflow: Context-aware scene insertion
- Status: `Implemented`
- Code locations: `apps/editor/public/app.js:7547-7561` (`markSceneEditorAsCurrent`), `apps/editor/public/app.js:11104-11148` (`addSceneDraft`, `getSceneIdForNewSceneDraftAnchor`), `apps/editor/public/editor-model.js:400-455` (`insertStructureSceneDraftAfterAnchor`), `test/editor-model.test.mjs:203-222` (scene-order insertion coverage)
- Execution flow: the manuscript editor records the active scene on pointer, focus, and typing -> `addSceneDraft` resolves the active editor scene as the insertion anchor -> `insertStructureSceneDraftAfterAnchor` creates a complete `structureDrafts.sceneOrder` with the new draft scene inserted immediately after that anchor -> the editor writes `EDITOR_STRUCTURE_KEY`, refreshes scene records, and selects the new scene
- Flow-on effects: the new scene stays in the same chapter as the active scene, binder order survives refresh/autosave/project persistence through `structureDrafts.sceneOrder`, the manuscript render selects the new scene after creation, and automated editor-model coverage verifies the persisted order
- Extraction/port notes: classified under Feature 1.2 because it is a manuscript editor/binder workflow that preserves author navigation and scene addressability; the insertion-order helper already lives in `editor-model.js`, while the UI event capture should move with the future scene-editor feature slice

- Extraction/port notes: Feature 1.1 spans `packages/manuscript-schema`, `services/analysis`, shared suggestion contracts, and the editor scene/console surfaces; future extraction should move remaining issue-console orchestration out of `app.js` into a dedicated feature slice, and any future `suggestion` channel must use `AnchoredManuscriptSuggestion` with explicit accept/reject commands

derivation, window states, menu actions, shortcut registration, and desktop static serving). Workflow: the author right-clicks a selected single manuscript word and chooses Dictionary, right-clicks an unselected manuscript word and chooses Dictionary, or presses `Ctrl+T` while typing to inspect the last word behind the caret. Execution flow: the editor host preserves any explicit selected range during context-menu handling -> the dictionary controller resolves a selected one-word range first and falls back to pointer/caret targeting only when needed -> the shell opens transient loading state and clears competing context menus -> the lexicon service loads the local OEWN manifest -> lookup fetches only the relevant first-letter shard from editor-public static assets and caches it -> lookup applies simple inflection candidates and generated irregular exception aliases -> `dictionary-window.js` renders found, not-found, or error content in `#dictionary-window-slot` with the OEWN source label. Persistence/rendering implications: dictionary lookup does not mutate manuscript text, issue records, spellcheck project dictionary words, editor preferences, or project save data; it is disposable render-only UI state reset during project activation, and the bundled dictionary is read-only application data. Verification: focused dictionary regression tests passed after reproducing the selected-word versus pointer-word drift; `npm test` was run afterward but the current harness failed in an unrelated `worldbuilding-studio.test.mjs` category/count assertion.

- Execution flow: author creates or selects an anchored task/note -> durable record retains its scene/range/evidence metadata -> `AnchoredRecordNavigationController` resolves or repairs the record range through injected callbacks -> `selectManuscriptProjections` emits an active `task` or `note` preview -> the textarea host paints/focuses the matching manuscript range without making the visual preview durable. For console customization, the author right-clicks blank whitespace inside the active right-console panel -> `app.js` opens the checklist from `side-panel-customization.js` -> checkbox changes update normalized `sidePanelVisibility` -> the active tab falls back to the first visible feature or the all-hidden overview -> the setting is written to browser compatibility storage and project `app-settings`. For custom metadata, the author creates a named metadata definition such as Lore with a highlight color -> the definition is normalized into project settings and rendered as a dynamic Metadata Console tab -> the sticky tab strip remains accessible while long Research lists scroll -> the manuscript right-click menu exposes `Add Lore` for selected or target text -> the inline passage-note draft creates a `metadata-lore` record with label/color fields and scene/range/evidence -> panel selection and hover previews navigate back to the manuscript and paint the configured highlight. For folders, the author opens a metadata panel -> creates a root folder or child folder -> adds project-level notes that persist without manuscript text -> optionally selects manuscript text and points an individual folder note to that verse -> the stored folder-note anchor can later reopen the scene/span through normal manuscript navigation. A loose anchored note can also be dragged onto a matching folder card -> `app.js` builds a folder-note input from the original note, creates the nested folder note, deletes the loose note through `AnchoredRecordService`, and persists both record sets -> desktop path saves write each folder and child folder under the matching physical `metadata/<project>/<group>/<folder>/...` path with `_folder.json` and note JSON files, and project-folder loads hydrate those files back into the active metadata folder tree.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `1.5h`): scene render asks `createManuScriptInfographicLanePreviewsForScene` for task, research, custom metadata, and world-linked anchors -> `selectManuscriptProjections` converts them to non-durable `ManuScriptInfographicLane` projections -> the textarea host groups markers by visual line using the same wrap metrics as the gutter -> exclamation markers open tasks through `navigateTaskAnchor`, magnifying-glass and scroll markers open passage notes through `selectPassageNote`, and earth markers open World Spine nodes through `selectWorldSpineNode`.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `1.5j`): the author clicks the floating ManuScriptInfographicLane eye -> delegated shell routing calls `toggleManuScriptInfographicLaneVisibility` -> `editorPrefs.manuScriptInfographicLaneVisible` is normalized, written to browser compatibility storage, and persisted through the current project record -> the scene editor rerenders the lane shell as visible or collapsed -> hidden mode suppresses `ManuScriptInfographicLane` projections and the editor host clears only the marker track, while visible mode derives and repaints the anchored markers.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `1.5k`): the author ports a Scrivener package -> the Feature `8.6` importer pairs each manuscript `content.rtf` entry with its sibling `content.comments` sidecar -> sidecar bodies are converted from Scrivener RTF/XML into folder-note text -> `scrivcmt://` field results in the manuscript RTF are matched against the imported scene text -> matching records receive scene/span anchors, while unresolved records remain source-provenance folder notes under the generated `Comments and Footnotes` metadata group.
- Flow-on effects: review items are actionable diagnostics rather than disconnected notes, passage notes are grouped into inspiration, research, and user-defined metadata panels, hidden console feature buttons do not delete or mutate task/note records, custom metadata definitions and folder notes hydrate with the active project, nested folder note counts contribute to metadata tab badges, deleting or moving scenes still synchronizes anchored task/passage-note metadata while project-only folder notes remain intact, drag-to-folder moves preserve manuscript anchors as folder-note anchors, folder note edits persist through the `metadata-folders` project-record domain, desktop source/save bridges preserve nested folder records, desktop folder-package saves expose inspectable physical metadata folder trees, and project reload hydrates the chosen Metadata Console layout.
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `1.5h`): anchored tasks, research, and metadata are discoverable directly on the manuscript line where the author attached them, the lane remains a render-only projection so project saves continue to store durable data in tasks, passage notes, and event/world records, and same-line markers stay compact enough to preserve the manuscript reading measure.
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `1.5k`): imported comments and footnotes land in the same Metadata Console folder model as native metadata notes instead of becoming manuscript text, source comment IDs and Scrivener binder paths remain available for audit, anchor-backed records can navigate back to the selected manuscript verse after import, and focused tests plus the Serva Vitae dry import verify comment count, footnote count, and anchor resolution.
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `1.5j`): authors can temporarily remove vertical infographic noise from the manuscript editor without deleting or changing any task, research, metadata, event, or World Spine anchors; the compact restore eye remains available in the same left-margin location and the visibility setting survives refresh/project reload through existing app-settings persistence.
- Extraction/port notes: navigation and preview projection derivation, task/note context-menu rendering, custom metadata definition normalization/form rendering, folder record normalization/rendering, composer planning, typed inline-note range policy, panel grouping/rendering, AI title request DTO planning, console item markup, delete-confirmation rendering, and console feature customization rendering/state normalization are extracted; inline manuscript insertion, persistence effects, drag/drop shell orchestration, async AI title calls, metadata definition deletion, folder delete confirmation, and canonical anchor promotion remain later anchored-record or metadata-console slice work

- Supplemental code locations (2026-08-04 Feature `1.7i` chapter context rail controls): `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:187-236` (`renderCompactGrammarCheck` and `renderSceneEditorContext`), `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:343-356` (scene render calls the context rail before the scene-title masthead), `apps/editor/public/app.js:7280-7323` (existing live scene/chapter/selection word-count sync against data attributes), `apps/editor/public/styles.css:4328-4444` (desktop context grid, compact grammar chip, and chapter word-count pill), `apps/editor/public/styles.css:12964-12998` (narrow viewport stacking), `test/scene-editor.test.mjs:91-104` (render order guard), and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1540-1546` (CSS selector guard). Workflow: the author sees Grammar Check, the current chapter title, and the live chapter word count in one chapter-scoped rail above the editable scene title. Execution flow: `renderSceneEditorHTML` resolves chapter title and chapter word count -> `renderSceneEditorContext` emits left grammar controls, centered breadcrumb, and right chapter count -> the masthead renders only the scene title -> grammar clicks still dispatch the existing grammar panel action -> typing still calls `syncSceneEditorWordCountReadouts`, which updates the moved chapter count through `data-scene-editor-chapter-word-count`. Persistence/rendering implications: this is a render-only layout move; it does not change manuscript text, spellcheck dictionary state, grammar-panel state shape, project metrics, word-count calculation, or saved project records. Verification: focused scene-editor render test and static desktop CSS guard.
- Execution flow: editor interaction resolves the active textarea host -> CSS gives the textarea the blank horizontal manuscript band while padding preserves the selected text width -> host helpers capture selection, bookmark, viewport, wrap metrics, and offset scrolling using the padded text measure -> `ManuscriptSelectionController` normalizes selected text, context ranges, bookmarks, and persisted scene selection defaults -> shell workflows invoke host range/scroll capabilities for find results, workspace defaults, task/note navigation, whitespace focus, and note deletion restore -> project saves receive normalized defaults rather than transient DOM objects. For side-panel layout, boot and project activation hydrate `panelResizerLayoutProfiles` -> each layout sync reports a reason such as `window-resize`, `render`, `console-collapse`, or `panel-resize` -> sync seeds a legacy `default` profile before automatic resize clamping -> passive resize/maximize restores the active workspace-size bucket, the nearest saved profile within one bucket, or the fallback profile before clamping for render -> if the selected wide profile has the same below-default panel widths as a compact bucket, restore emits a `LayoutState` recovery warning and lifts the binder back to the normal wide default -> profile persistence is applied only when `persistProfile` is requested by a completed real panel drag and the reason is exactly `panel-resize`; any legacy or non-resizer persistence request emits `panel-layout.persist-blocked` and leaves `panelResizerLayoutProfiles` untouched.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `1.7f`): the author clicks the compact top-chrome side-panel icon -> `app.js` toggles normalized `sidePanelsHidden`, writes the browser compatibility key, persists through the project `app-settings` path, logs the layout event, and applies `is-side-panels-hidden` to the app root -> CSS removes the active page's side rails and resize handles from layout -> manuscript and World Spine layout syncs keep CSS variables current but skip profile restore and persistence while the rails are hidden.
- Supplemental execution flow (2026-07-21 Feature `1.7f` compact title placement): the author still clicks the same side-panel focus button, but the button now renders in the stacked title tool row beside the environment badge and appearance control; only chrome layout CSS changes, so the existing `sidePanelsHidden` persistence and rail-hiding flow are unchanged.
- Flow-on effects: autosave and reload can preserve author context, issue/task/note navigation lands in useful manuscript positions, authors can begin selections from the line-aligned whitespace beside manuscript text, pane-local scrolling avoids browser-page jumps, visual-line math and size-scoped side-panel profile math are testable outside `app.js`, moving or maximizing a browser window no longer writes compact panel widths into the full-size bucket, previously contaminated wide profiles no longer trap the author in the compact binder width after maximize, Developer Logs expose profile restore/persist decisions and browser geometry for monitor-change debugging, the scene editor keeps manuscript metadata and writing controls visible without a redundant project-file row, and a later editor adapter can provide equivalent navigation capabilities without rewriting feature controllers
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `1.7f`): authors can clear both side rails on Manuscript, Narration, and World pages from a minimal always-available control, the saved panel-width profiles remain available when the rails return, and the hidden flag follows project load/save/autosave without altering manuscript, issue, narration, or world data.
- Extraction/port notes: selection policy and textarea host mechanics are extracted; residual non-host focus/scroll work belongs with individual panels or shell chrome rather than the manuscript editor host
