- Supplemental code locations (2026-07-17 highlight colour memory): `apps/editor/public/editor-model.js:39-120` defines stronger preset colours and default highlight preference state, `apps/editor/public/editor-model.js:201-294` normalizes `highlightRecentCustomColors`, dedupes/caps recent custom RGB colours, and resolves preset/custom paint values, `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:561-662` renders the highlight palette, live custom RGB sliders, and recent custom swatches, `apps/editor/public/app.js:1743`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:2684-2685`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:2907-2911`, and `apps/editor/public/app.js:6997-7057` route recent-swatch clicks, live slider previews, committed slider storage, preference persistence, and deduped recent selection, `apps/editor/public/app.js:7069-7106` keeps palette active state matched by RGB rather than the shared custom id, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:3147-3252` styles the stronger highlight swatches and recent custom row, and `test/editor-model.test.mjs:293-332`, `test/scene-editor.test.mjs:125-127`, and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:787-792` plus `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1039` cover preference normalization, recent palette rendering, app wiring, and stylesheet exposure.
- Execution flow: author drag-selects manuscript text and presses toolbar Bold, Highlight, or Clear Decorations -> shell captures the current scene-editor selection before browser focus changes and refreshes drag selections on pointerup -> the command reconciles visible textarea text into scene draft text/blocks when a fresh draft scene is ahead of state -> the command prefers the live textarea selection and falls back to the cached same-scene selection if the click collapsed the live range -> selected Bold/Highlight routes to the canonical mark planner, while collapsed Bold/Highlight toggles the pending switch for incoming text -> selected Clear Decorations switches off pending format tools, switches on the eraser, subtracts the selection from author-owned canonical marks and legacy inline-format ranges, and leaves the eraser active for the next drag-selected range -> retained same-kind compatibility marks are promoted to durable IDs before legacy ranges for that mark kind are removed, while erased compatibility marks are rebuilt from the post-erase inline ranges by scene sync -> `workspace.project.marks` receives added/removed/split/promoted `ManuscriptMark` records and updated mark sequence state -> the mark history service stores before/after project-mark and scene-range snapshots for selected decoration commands, eraser commands, or one grouped snapshot when multiple active formats are paint-applied in a single gesture -> project persistence records the mutation through the existing scene/project save path -> projection selector emits author-mark projections from canonical marks -> textarea host repaints the author-mark layer -> shell restores the previous viewport and selected range without centering -> editor-scoped `Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` pops the runtime mark history before native text history and reapplies the relevant snapshot through the same project/render path.
- Flow-on effects: literal `<strong>`, `<em>`, and `<u>` tags are no longer inserted into manuscript text, italic/underline/strikethrough remain implemented beside the canonical Bold/Highlight controls, the old scene-wide italic CSS class is no longer applied, current author formatting survives JSON-backed scene storage, user highlights and selected Bold marks are canonical marks rather than legacy ranges, selected decoration commands and eraser removals can be undone/redone without persisting runtime history into project files, formatting and erasing no longer re-center the manuscript viewport, stacked paint-applied decoration groups undo in one editor-scoped step, stronger highlight presets improve manuscript scanability, recent custom colours persist with editor preferences through the app-settings project path without adding duplicate swatches, future non-compatibility marks are preserved during scene sync, and the direct mutation planner is now exercised by apply and clear author-facing mark workflows.
- Extraction/port notes: this is classified under Feature 1 because it is core scene-editor authoring behavior; user highlights and Bold now use the Phase 7 mark/projection/persistence boundary end to end, while italic/underline/strikethrough still need migration from `inlineFormatRanges` to direct `ManuscriptMark` mutations. The current browser slice keeps text history native and mark history app-owned, so a later ProseMirror host should replace this with one unified editor transaction history.

- Execution flow: manuscript input captures previous and next scene text -> edit transaction derivation identifies the changed range -> active task/note records shift through offset-backed record APIs while issue, event-tag, narration-session, and narration-alignment anchors map the same scene edit into block-local canonical anchor patches -> changed anchor status/evidence is committed to normal project state before scene draft persistence -> scene draft update synchronizes compatibility inline ranges into canonical `workspace.project.marks` -> activation, idle validation, and task/note navigation validate or recover records through the shared anchor service -> accepted repair patches persist updated bounded evidence/status through `ProjectPersistenceService` -> projection selection regenerates disposable decorations from renderable validated anchors and canonical marks
- Flow-on effects: revision-pass and future highlight/decorations work have a shared anchor lifecycle, pure offset shifts stay cheap during typing, current durable owners carry bounded hash/context metadata where needed, stale load/navigation results are not painted, long ranges use hash plus bounded context instead of giant excerpts, idle validation uses one debounced scheduler, generic revision-marker records can use the canonical update helper when their durable collection is introduced, schema-shaped manuscript marks now use the same projection/index/drift path, user highlights are synchronized and directly mutated as project data, and runtime-only spellcheck/search/narration-follow visuals stay outside project persistence
- Extraction/port notes: this refactor phase now has direct canonical mark writes for user highlights and Bold without a separate Decorations right-console panel. Next decoration work should stay behind canonical mark/record DTOs and avoid adding new right-console surfaces unless explicitly requested.

- Execution flow: the author opens the Project button in the Manuscript top chrome -> `renderProjectSettingsMenu` renders a Project Settings dropdown with stacked Proof read and Local AI actions -> selecting Proof read opens `draft-proofing-settings-window.js` without changing project data until a setting is edited -> the author presses the new-run icon in the Manuscript top proof-read panel -> `startDraftProofRun` calls `startNewDraftProofRun` to create a fresh durable run with inherited fallback visual settings -> successful creation turns `draftProofMarksVisible` on before the panel/editor refresh -> the author presses the pause icon or play icon -> `toggleDraftProofRun` pauses the active run after capturing a scene-offset resume point, resumes the latest paused run, or continues the latest completed run without creating a second pass -> resume/continue turns `draftProofMarksVisible` on and navigates to the saved resume point or latest covered span -> while a run is active, normal click-drag text selection in the Manuscript pane calls `addDraftProofCoverageRange`, while Shift+drag selection calls `removeDraftProofCoverageRange`; the same gesture path exits early on Narration + Voice -> `draft-proofing-service.js` merges or subtracts coverage intervals by scene -> active manuscript text edits call `updateDraftProofCoverageForTextEdit`, shifting existing spans and adding the edited range -> project persistence stores `draftProofing` with durable run records, `resumePoint`, project fallback `settings.*`, and each run's `settings.backdropColor`, `settings.backdropColorPresets`, and `settings.recentBackdropColors` -> browser refresh/project activation normalizes the run and restores `draftProofMarksVisible` only when `activeRunId` points at an active run -> the proof-read eye toggles `draftProofMarksVisible` in UI state -> `selectManuscriptProjections` derives a `draft-proof` projection only while the eye is open and the scene editor is in manuscript mode, including the source run's backdrop colour -> the textarea host paints or clears the transparent underlay using the per-range run colour with the project setting as a fallback -> the Project menu or proof-read pencil icon opens `draft-proofing-settings-window.js` only from Manuscript, where the control-only window lets the author select a proof-read iteration from the header dropdown, colour changes and preset edits call `updateDraftProofRunSettings` for that run, committed custom colours update that run's recent row, recent swatches restore stored colours without duplicating them, colour picking turns `draftProofMarksVisible` on for manuscript preview, checked Version Data rows call `deleteDraftProofRuns` through the normal `draft-proofing` persistence domain, and clearing all run/version data still calls `clearDraftProofRunData` behind the existing confirmation.
- Flow-on effects: proof-read coverage is simple durable run state rather than a notes/warnings layer, author highlights remain visually stronger, autosave/project save/load preserve runs, resume points, project fallback proof-read visual settings, per-run visual settings, and per-run recent custom backdrop colours, active browser refresh keeps approved proof-read coverage visible, unchanged legacy neutral presets migrate to more visible defaults, legacy project-level colours are copied into existing run settings during normalization, the latest completed run remains recallable through an explicit eye toggle and continuable through the completed-state play icon, scene deletion removes dead scene coverage, the Project settings menu scans as a vertical dropdown instead of a horizontal pill row while keeping the existing Proof read and Local AI commands, the Project settings window no longer acts as an information panel with run/scene/span counters, targeted deletion can remove stale or mistaken iterations without touching retained runs or fallback visual settings, destructive proof-read data clearing stays away from the main proof-read panel, Narration + Voice no longer exposes or paints the manuscript proof-read workflow until a dedicated narration/voice proofing feature exists, the pause/resume/continue control can no longer accidentally create another proof-read pass, passive viewport/focus/caret movement no longer creates proof-read coverage, Shift+drag can erase mistakenly covered spans inside the same scene, starting a proof-read run visibly opens the coverage underlay immediately, continuing a proof-read run visibly shows prior marks and returns the author to their saved proofing location, and `npm test` covers lifecycle, explicit new-run creation from a paused state, new-run marker visibility, completed-run continuation, resume-point persistence, active-refresh visibility restoration, coverage add/remove, edit expansion, project and per-run backdrop/preset/recent settings mutation, targeted and clear-all run deletion, projection colours, host rendering, panel visibility/settings/icon controls, settings-window control rendering without summary cards, iteration dropdown/checklist rendering, state hydration, persistence shape, pane-specific chrome/projection gating, and desktop module exposure.
- Extraction/port notes: classified under Feature 1 because it is a manuscript review workflow. Domain state lives in `features/draft-proofing`; the shell still owns DOM observation and persistence scheduling until a future scene-editor controller can absorb those orchestration callbacks.

- Supplemental code locations (2026-07-21 Feature `1.13` compact title placement): `apps/editor/public/shell/editor-chrome.js:78-83` places the appearance segmented control inside `.desktop-title-tools` under the program name; `apps/editor/public/styles.css:181-207` owns the stacked title/tool row, and `apps/editor/public/styles.css:1306-1382` scopes compact title-row appearance-control dimensions; `test/editor-chrome.test.mjs:99-106` and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1323-1325` guard the grouped chrome markup and CSS hooks.
- Execution flow: author presses an appearance button in the top chrome -> the delegated shell action calls `setAppearanceModePreference` -> the selected mode is normalized into `editorPrefs.appearanceMode` and written through `EDITOR_PREFS_KEY` -> the current project record receives the normal editor-preference persistence update -> `syncAppearanceMode` resolves Light/Dark/System into `data-theme` and `data-appearance-mode` on `document.documentElement` and `#app` -> CSS variables and dark-mode selectors repaint the active shell, manuscript editor, panels, overlays, modal surfaces, task/narration console controls, floating grammar-check review window, World Spine timeline surfaces, Dream Scaping cards, and the Writing Goals dashboard's settings, calendar, selected-day, archive/list, notes, and footer surfaces while preserving textarea/overlay projection layering and without writing render objects to project data.
- Supplemental execution flow (2026-07-21 Feature `1.13` compact title placement): the author still presses the same Light, Dark, or System button, but the segmented control now renders below the program name in the title tool row beside the environment badge; only render placement and sizing change, so preference normalization, project persistence, and document theme resolution stay on the existing path.
- Flow-on effects: Light remains the compatibility default for old projects, System follows `prefers-color-scheme: dark` through a browser media-query listener, theme state is an editor preference rather than manuscript/world/audio data, dark-mode rendering is tokenized for future feature slices, inline task titles stay embedded instead of becoming black input blocks, World Spine node and linked-passage cards remain readable, Grammar Check review rows and suggestion controls stay readable, Writing Goals dashboard month/week/list states no longer show pale cards in dark mode, inline-format overlay text no longer double-paints with the native textarea in dark mode, and tests guard preference normalization, chrome options, the document theme bridge, dashboard hooks, projection transparency, and dark console/world/grammar-control CSS hooks.
- Extraction/port notes: classified under Feature 1 because it is an editor IDE shell workflow used while reviewing, navigating, and writing manuscript content across panes. The shell still owns the global event route and document attribute side effect; a future app-store facade can move this into a small appearance controller without changing the `editorPrefs.appearanceMode` contract.

- Feature: `Feature 1.14 - Shared form dismissal behavior`
- Workflow: Minimal top-right form cancel control
- Status: `Implemented`
- Code locations: `apps/editor/public/styles.css:98-130` (shared `.form-dismiss-button` and dark hover/focus treatment), `apps/editor/public/styles.css:7770-7778`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:9190-9197`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:9306-9310`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:9837-9846`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:10610-10613`, and `apps/editor/public/styles.css:11137-11144` (heading padding and placement guards), `apps/editor/public/features/anchored-records/task-context-menu.js:53-72` (anchored task/note composer cross), `apps/editor/public/features/metadata-console/custom-metadata-panel.js:24-72` (custom metadata form cross and Create-only action row), `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/scene-world-spine-metadata.js:130-164` (scene World Spine metadata cross), `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/worldbuilding-studio.js:1286-1305` (Dream Scaping entry form cross), and `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/world-spine-panel.js:419-442`, `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/world-spine-panel.js:780-806` (event catalogue assignment and implication composer crosses), `apps/editor/public/app.js:2047-2049`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:2140-2142`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:2197-2212`, and `apps/editor/public/app.js:17269-17275` (owned context-surface click boundary), plus `test/task-context-menu.test.mjs:41-47`, `test/custom-metadata-service.test.mjs:68-71`, `test/scene-world-spine-metadata.test.mjs:86-89`, `test/scene-world-spine-metadata.test.mjs:133-140`, `test/worldbuilding-studio.test.mjs:43-45`, `test/world-spine-panel.test.mjs:692-699`, `test/world-spine-panel.test.mjs:756-761`, and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1219`.
- Execution flow: the author opens one of the converted transient forms -> the renderer emits the same existing dismissal `data-action` on an absolute top-right `.form-dismiss-button` cross -> delegated shell handlers continue to close/cancel the form through the prior route -> field clicks inside binder-backed scene World Spine metadata or World Spine context surfaces return before the generic no-action menu teardown -> action clicks inside those owned surfaces reach their specific Save/Create/Close handlers while the live form DOM is still present -> save/create buttons remain in the form action row and still call the existing persistence or mutation paths -> CSS reserves heading space and applies the same minimal light/dark interaction treatment. The behavior is render-only until the author presses Save/Create, so no manuscript anchors, metadata definitions, World Spine links, implication edges, or project records change on dismiss.
- Flow-on effects: form cancellation now scans consistently across manuscript context composers, Metadata Console creation, Dream Scaping catalogue entry, and World Spine event/link forms; destructive confirmations and dashboard-specific cancel semantics are left on their existing controls because they are not routine transient forms.
- Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/scene-world-spine-metadata.test.mjs`, and the focused `runSceneWorldSpineMetadataTest` command passed. Full `npm test` was run; the new scene metadata and World Spine tests passed, while the harness still reports unrelated existing `5 !== 4` failures in `test/desktop-application.test.mjs` and `test/project-source.test.mjs`.

- Execution flow: the author opens Project -> Local AI or the Models button beside the Local AI toggle -> `openLocalAiPanel` renders the panel and calls the desktop `/api/local-ai/models` route -> the desktop host scans the configured model root for category folders, `abe-model.json` manifests, and loose local artifacts -> the panel renders folder readiness, registered models, unregistered artifacts, validation messages, and Hugging Face browse links -> editing the model root updates UI draft state only -> Save sends the root to `/api/local-ai/model-settings`, which persists desktop-local settings and returns a fresh scan -> Create folders calls `/api/local-ai/models/ensure-folders`, creates only the owned category folders, persists the normalized root, and refreshes the panel.
- Flow-on effects: the browser version gains a ComfyUI-style local model folder surface without direct filesystem ownership, project manuscript JSON is not dirtied by model-root changes, model manifests become the reviewable bridge between downloaded artifacts and task routing, loose files are visible but not silently accepted for AI tasks, and Hugging Face shortcuts are available from the settings panel while actual model execution remains behind provider adapters.
- Extraction/port notes: classified under Feature 2 because it supports Local Writing Assistant provider setup. The next step is to teach `LocalAiRouter` to select configured model descriptors from the library and to add runtime launch/stop services under the desktop/local-ai boundary rather than the editor shell.

- Extraction/port notes: event records belong in `packages/manuscript-schema`; the manual World Spine composer DTO is feature-owned under `features/world-spine`, while remaining persistence scheduling and richer taxonomy controls should move out of `app.js` as the event workflow matures

Workflow: author clicks Stop once -> command service marks the runtime `isStopping`, sets the session to disabled `finalizing`, and calls `MediaRecorder.stop()` -> app starts tracker cleanup as a promise but does not await it before saving the canonical long take -> finalization persists the media file and saved take with the strongest live transcript available -> the background cleanup drains the desktop tracker and calls the desktop stop route -> the desktop bridge writes the full WAV, splits long PCM into overlapping Whisper chunk WAVs, transcribes each chunk, stitches duplicate overlap words, and returns cleanup metadata -> the app stores cleanup text separately on the saved recording, refreshes the session monitor if the same take is still selected, and saves the project snapshot. Persistence/rendering implications: the full saved media file remains the durable audio source; temporary Whisper chunks remain desktop scratch artifacts under the realtime-speech temp path; the `finalizing` status and cleanup promises are runtime-only; only `cleanupTranscript`, cleanup timestamps, and the project snapshot update after cleanup completes. Persistence/rendering implications: decoded peaks, waveform zoom, click geometry, and playhead position are runtime-only and are cleared/regenerated from project media; re-recording creates a new anchored take through the normal finalization path and does not overwrite or delete the source take. - Execution flow: narration pane reuses the manuscript scene editor in `editorMode: "narration"` -> the chrome derives a narration-safe writing-target summary, omits proof-read entry points, and filters the manuscript writing session tracker -> the scene renderer requests narration-friendly projections without `draft-proof` coverage and includes saved-take coverage in narration mode -> the right console switches from normal Metadata Console tabs to a single Audio tab for the active scene -> author clicks a manuscript line or drags an exact span to arm recording; a plain click is resolved to the clicked wrapped line inside the canonical block and line labels are measured from the textarea geometry used by the gutter -> author starts recording from the Audio tab -> recording runtime stores a scene/block/span-aware take session and line-aware listening status -> the live speech tracker selector creates the desktop-local `sherpa-onnx` tracker, starts the local sidecar through the desktop bridge, and streams Web Audio PCM frames rather than browser speech-recognition results -> the selected tracker metadata appears in runtime/session state and the RHS Tracker engine field -> local Sherpa snapshots update live transcript fields and the Audio tab monitor in place before or alongside transcript alignment -> transcript snapshots preserve final/interim/changed provider result metadata and call the narration follow alignment service -> the app estimates the manuscript offsets currently visible in the narration viewport and passes that range into alignment -> the service resolves transcript tails against canonical scene blocks using exact/fuzzy word-fit metrics, live progress bias, repeated-text scoring, and a visible/near/full-scene search order -> the app enriches that selection with the current visual manuscript line, keeps cumulative take coverage separately from current tracking offsets, derives a display-only lead span when recognition lags, paints confirmed already-read coverage without painting the predicted current phrase, samples manuscript word progress from the confirmed viewport offsets every three seconds against the active words-per-visual-line density, and optionally scrolls with paced forward movement plus short viewport pulses when Follow scroll is enabled -> Stop moves the runtime into an immediate finalizing state without replacing the button DOM -> stop/finalize runs repo-local Whisper cleanup, stores that cleanup text separately from Sherpa live text, and saves the take against the high-confidence follow verse or falls back to the armed clicked-line/span -> saved take offsets render as a `narration-recording` overlay and the RHS take card calls the voice preview/navigation services for Play/Stop/Delete/Re-record -> Play resolves a range-aware plan, selects the saved manuscript span, opens a review strip whose word buttons come only from the saved primary transcript while the manuscript anchor supplies labels/highlight context, and prepares a transient oscillogram from the media blob when decoding is available -> transcript word clicks, oscillogram clicks, the seek slider, and Play from cursor operate through the preview controller without changing the saved recording -> Re-record maps the selected transcript/waveform time range back onto the saved anchor and creates another take through the normal recording/finalization path -> Delete asks the action service to remove the project-media file and active-project recording record, then the shell persists the project snapshot and refreshes the Audio tab count plus overlay coverage
- Flow-on effects: narration state is separate from editor rendering state, recorded takes remain tied to manuscript anchors, live speech tracking can move independently from the initially armed recording target without native-selection snap-back, plain clicks no longer save or highlight an entire paragraph unless the line itself spans that range, display line numbers, provider labels, visible-range search context, Sherpa live text, review playhead/cursor/waveform state, and tracking offsets remain non-persistent UI/session metadata, while Whisper cleanup text is stored separately as recording evidence when available. Follow scroll is a local editor preference rather than project manuscript data, transient backward ASR matches no longer make the viewport jump upward by default, saved audio coverage is a render-only projection derived from persisted recording offsets and disappears when the anchored take record is deleted, recording-time panel text updates do not recreate Stop/transport controls on every ASR snapshot, the manuscript surface now shows only the opened-take review strip rather than saved-take card lists, the right-hand console exposes only narration Audio metadata while Narration + Voice is active, Sherpa snapshot, local audio-frame, stop-time whisper cleanup, reading-rate, alignment, search-pass, playback seek/stop/waveform, and viewport debug traces are available through gated developer-log and desktop-log sources, alignment jobs and realtime speech sessions have typed service coverage, media persistence and deletion are service-owned, recorder resource cleanup is shared across normal stop, failed start, re-record start, and project switching, armed verse selection is feature-owned, take/session/final-record data now come from feature-owned DTO helpers, recorder/speech events no longer mutate runtime state directly from shell-defined handlers, start/stop command sequencing has focused service tests, final media-save failures are mapped outside the shell, saved recording collection mutation is feature-owned, preview/navigation/stop/delete/re-record actions resolve through service plans, structure-change metadata re-anchoring is feature-owned, and manuscript-only proof-read/session-tracker surfaces no longer appear on the narration page before a dedicated narration/voice proofing feature exists
- Extraction/port notes: project persistence side effects still live in `app.js`; the service boundary exists in `services/audio`, and remaining persistence scheduling should move into a narration or voice-recording command as local engine tuning continues

- Feature: `Feature 4.5 - Mobile dictated writing companion`
- Workflow: Phone voice-first authoring with reviewable anchored manuscript insertion
- Status: `Planned - architecture documented; not implemented`
- Architecture locations: `docs/architecture/mobile-friendly-architecture.md` (`MobileFriendlyArchitecture` plan), `docs/architecture/editor-application-roadmap.md` (`Parallel Product Track: MobileFriendlyArchitecture`), `docs/product/feature-map.md` (`Mobile Authoring (Planned)`)
- Planned execution flow: mobile host loads a local project snapshot through a persistence adapter -> writer selects a scene/span insertion target and records speech through a capability adapter -> audio service returns a transcript candidate under an explicit locality policy -> writer edits or accepts the candidate -> accepted prose routes through an anchored manuscript edit and revision/persistence path -> stale revisions or unresolved anchors remain reviewable conflicts for phone or desktop resolution
- Flow-on effects: mobile can extend the authoring environment without inventing flat phone-only manuscript data, mandatory cloud speech processing, or unsafe synchronization behavior
- Classification note: this is tracked under Feature 04 because microphone/STT orchestration is audio-service-owned, while its accepted output is a new manuscript edit rather than Narration Follow Mode alignment

- Execution flow: world data is modeled as typed templates, entities, spines, nodes, edges, and links -> analysis can suggest templates/entities/cross-spine links without mutating canonical data -> the author can right-click a scene, click World Spine, fill timeline metadata, and save normalized `worldSpineMetadata` into that scene draft -> `buildSceneRecords` carries scene-draft metadata into render-time scene records -> `buildWorldSpineTimelineModel` derives automatic primary scene nodes from scene metadata and creates branch/reference nodes from accepted event tags, user-entered critical events, location changes, saved world nodes, locations, characters, entity introductions, and scene custom metadata values -> `renderWorldSpinePanelHTML` renders a fixed event rail, horizontally scrolling central canvas, overlay summary-card layer, and fixed compact manuscript pane -> delegated focus/selection events open a temporary summary card attached to the active node while pointer hover is reserved for connector targeting -> scene metadata edits continue through the scene-list metadata form with project custom metadata definitions injected -> saving updates the scene draft and rerenders timeline badges and metadata-backed node signals -> wheel/trackpad/drag/scrollbar movement changes only the central canvas and recalculates card position -> selecting a linked node can show its manuscript context in the fixed right pane
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3f`): the author drags the narrow separator beside the event rail or manuscript pane -> delegated pointer handling starts a World Spine resize session -> `syncWorldSpinePanelLayout` clamps both fixed rails while reserving central timeline width -> completed drag writes a size-bucketed `worldSpinePanelLayoutProfiles` record plus current rail widths to browser compatibility storage and the project `app-settings` domain -> project activation, render, and window resize restore the nearest saved World Spine profile or wide fallback, then update workspace CSS variables without moving either fixed rail into the horizontal scroller.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3h`): the shared top-chrome side-panel focus toggle sets `sidePanelsHidden` -> the World Spine workspace receives `is-side-panels-hidden` from the app root -> CSS removes the fixed event rail, linked manuscript pane, and drag handles so the central timeline occupies the page -> `syncWorldSpinePanelLayout` skips profile restore/persistence while hidden and restores the saved rails when the flag is cleared.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3i`): the author right-clicks a manuscript verse and chooses Add World Spine event -> the context-menu composer creates an accepted `EventTag` anchored to the selected/clicked canonical block range -> scene render derives world markers from that event tag, saved world spine nodes, and scene World Spine metadata -> the ManuScriptInfographicLane paints earth icons on the first tied visual line -> clicking an earth marker switches to World Spine and calls `selectWorldSpineNode` for the matching timeline node.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3j`/`6.3k`): the author sees a minimal World Spine infographic strip in the Dream Scaping heading lane only -> right-clicking a category icon renders that category's compact entry fields, moves the dialog into a body-level portal, and centers it over the visible application shell -> the Custom category renders repeatable field-name rows, and Add field appends another `Enter field name` input before save -> Cancel or Escape collapses the dialog without changing `workspace.world` -> saving the form creates a structured catalogue entity, custom category, or event draft in `workspace.world`; custom categories preserve the ordered field names as reusable structured fields with neutral `Enter <field name>` placeholders -> the shell persists the `world` domain through `persistCurrentProjectRecord` -> event drafts render as small draggable chips in the same lane -> native drag start attaches a faint node-shaped drag image for placement feedback -> dropping a draft onto a World Spine slot creates a primary timeline node with `timelineSlotIndex`, `sequenceRank`, event metadata, linked item names, and optional manuscript boundary anchors -> the timeline lays scene and world-only primary nodes into one ranked sequence and the manuscript lane shows start/end boundary markers when anchors resolve.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3j` catalogue): the author left-clicks a Dream Scaping category icon -> delegated click routing opens `worldbuildingCatalogueCategoryId` state at the saved user bounds when available, otherwise at the pointer or source icon -> `buildWorldbuildingStudioModel` derives count badges and a read-only catalogue from normalized `workspace.world.entities`, `eventDrafts`, and author-created custom categories, mapping older Scrivener reference template names such as `Ship` and `Station` into built-in catalogue IDs -> if `state.selectedNodeId` resolves to a World Spine event, beat, reference, world node, or scene node, the shell passes that node plus scene text as transient scope so counts and catalogue rows narrow to matching people, places, linked items, anchors, critical events, and scene text -> `renderWorldbuildingStudioHTML` emits the count-badge icon strip and fixed, opaque, scrollable catalogue popover with a drag header, resize handle, and `+` add action -> the shell moves the catalogue to a body-level portal, measures the visible catalogue icon toolbar, and clamps the popover bounds while setting runtime size/max-height styles -> dragging the catalogue header or bottom-right handle updates `worldbuildingCatalogueBounds` and fixed `left/top/width/height` styles -> pointer release normalizes `editorPrefs.worldbuildingCatalogueBounds`, writes the user preference, and persists the `app-settings` domain without changing `workspace.world` -> pressing `+` calls the existing category-form route for the currently viewed catalogue category -> clicking a catalogue row stores only transient selected item ID/kind and rerenders the current filtered catalogue with a read-only detail pane for that record's fields, notes, source/provenance, and anchors, using spaced detail rows for scanability -> Escape, outside click, or the close button clears only the transient catalogue state and selected item while leaving the saved layout preference intact.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3s`): `buildWorldSpineTimelineModel` derives ordered primary nodes from scenes and world nodes -> `buildWorldSpineEventRailItems` walks that ordered sequence, inserts a chapter separator only when a scene-backed primary node enters a chapter key that has not already appeared in the rail, and assigns event numbers only to selectable event rows -> `renderEventRail` renders chapter rows as non-clickable separators and keeps event rows on the existing `select-node` action -> CSS styles the separator as a compact rail boundary in light and dark themes without altering timeline, manuscript, or world persistence.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.3n`): the author opens World Spine scene metadata from the binder scene context menu -> the Scene beats textarea is read by the existing generic `[data-scene-world-spine-field]` collector -> `buildSceneWorldSpineMetadataFromFormValues` normalizes beat labels into `worldSpineMetadata.sceneBeats` -> `applySceneWorldSpineMetadataToDraft` writes the scene draft without changing manuscript text -> `persistCurrentProjectRecord` records the manuscript metadata update -> `buildWorldSpineTimelineModel` derives beat counts, node badges, and smaller `Scene beat` child nodes from the parent scene at render time without creating duplicate `workspace.world` nodes.
- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `6.4d`/`6.4e`/`6.4f`/`6.4g`/`6.4h`): the author drags from a timeline node connector dot to another node -> `createWorldSpineInteractionController` renders a live node-graph curve and resolves the endpoints without cloning a node under the cursor -> the shell opens a midpoint implication composer -> the post-drag click guard consumes only the synthetic canvas/node click and lets composer controls through -> saving calls `applyWorldSpineImplicationEdgeToWorld` -> the project persistence boundary records the `world` domain mutation -> the next timeline model normalizes `world.edges`, keeps only resolvable endpoint pairs, resolves child/reference endpoint nodes to their owning primary event blocks for display, records visible connector-dot handle points from the upper event-card row, filters implication paths out of the structural timeline SVG, and renders the typed effect inside the same node-layer stack as the event block cards.
- Flow-on effects: chronology and worldbuilding remain structured rather than note-only, authors can enter World Spine scene metadata where they already manage scene records or directly from the timeline card they are inspecting, scene beats give scenes an internal planning layer without creating duplicate world-only nodes, user-created metadata definitions can annotate scene-level timeline context without becoming loose notes, event and world nodes stay traceable to manuscript blocks, the permanent inspector panel is removed from the live World Spine view, card metadata remains interactive and scrollable without stealing horizontal timeline scroll, connector dots are easier to target because node hover no longer spawns a blocking card, event placement drags and timeline block drags remain visually traceable through faint node-shaped previews, active link drags remain visually traceable through a live curve without blocking target nodes, the implication composer Save button remains a one-click persistence action after typing an effect, saved implication paths remain between the event blocks instead of colliding with the lower time axis, fixed rails keep event and manuscript context visible while the timeline moves, the left Story Timeline rail shows manuscript chapter boundaries without creating extra selectable nodes or repeating earlier chapter headers when stale/non-contiguous metadata appears later, rail sizing follows the same per-window persistence pattern as the manuscript side panels, entity introductions can still link to manuscript and timeline anchors, cross-spine causality and manual implication effects remain represented explicitly, and tests verify template instantiation, timeline nodes, entity introduction links, edge relationships, scene-derived metadata editing, scene beats, scene custom metadata editing, scene-derived node rendering, passage highlighting, labelled badges, detail-card placement, no-hover card opening, implication edge persistence/rendering, node-graph implication paths, child/reference endpoint lifting, composer click-suppression guard coverage, event-placement drag image coverage, timeline block ghost coverage, chapter-grouped event rail rendering, repeated-chapter rail suppression, and World Spine rail profile storage.
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `6.3j`): World Spine can be used before any manuscript scenes exist; worldbuilding records remain structured project world data rather than Dream Scaping suggestion state; custom item definitions can grow one field at a time without seeded example copy; category catalogues expose existing entities and event drafts without duplicating or mutating persistence; the catalogue header `+` provides an explicit add-new route for the visible category without changing the right-click category creation path; clicking catalogue rows reveals detail panes from existing normalized records without adding durable selected-item fields; dragging or resizing catalogue headers stores only user layout preference data in `editorPrefs.worldbuildingCatalogueBounds`; the catalogue no longer opens over its source icon row, its main surfaces are opaque, and detail rows have clearer spacing; category numbers now report real item counts instead of icon order; selected timeline nodes temporarily narrow those numbers and catalogue rows without adding persistence fields; existing detail cards, selected outlines, fixed rails, and compact manuscript pane continue to work; boundary markers use durable scene/block anchors; and focused tests verify right-click category creation, left-click catalogue rendering, header add routing, row detail inspection, catalogue drag/resize handle routing, layout preference normalization, opaque popover styling, neutral placeholders, custom field persistence, draft placement, node ordering, drop-zone markup, count badges, selected-event scoping, Scrivener alias population, and marker rendering.
- Extraction/port notes: schema ownership remains in `packages/world-schema`; the World Spine render model, DOM interaction controller, implication edge service, scene metadata form model, and Dream Scaping worldbuilding studio are now extracted under `apps/editor/public/features/world-spine`, while shell code only supplies runtime state, scene-draft persistence effects, persistence-neutral selection effects, delegated browser events, and project persistence calls. Direct node editing, accepted suggestion application, richer cross-spine filtering, edge editing/deletion, and manuscript-boundary assignment UI should move into adjacent world feature slices instead of reviving a permanent inspector panel.

- Feature: `Feature 7.1/7.2/7.3 - Dream-scaping contracts, suggestions, and panel rendering`
- Workflow: Reviewable dream-scaping story-fit suggestions
- Status: `Implemented`
- Code locations: `packages/shared-types/src/index.ts:134-168` (`DreamScapeFit`, `DreamScapePlacement`, `DreamScapeSuggestion`), `services/analysis/src/index.ts:47-50` (`exploreDreamScape` service entry), `services/analysis/src/index.ts:178-214` (`exploreDreamScape` job wrapper), `services/analysis/src/index.ts:315-424` (`buildDreamScapeSuggestions`, fit, placement, prompt helpers), `apps/editor/public/app.js:5109-5163` (`renderDreamScapingPanel`, `renderDreamSuggestion`), `test/analysis-service.test.mjs:224-233` (dream-scaping suggestion coverage)
- Execution flow: author idea input is represented as a dream-scaping request -> analysis builds a pending, evidence-linked story-fit suggestion -> proposed placement describes whether the idea belongs in manuscript, timeline, or world history -> editor renders the dream-scaping panel and pending suggestions for review
- Flow-on effects: dream-scaping does not silently mutate manuscript or world data, evidence records stay traceable, suggestion review state is explicit, and tests verify the dream-scaping job, fit classification, placement target, and evidence list
- Extraction/port notes: the analysis side is service-owned; the current editor panel is display-only in `app.js` and should become a dedicated feature slice when author input and accept/reject actions are added

- Feature: `Feature 8.1/8.2/8.3/8.4/8.5 - Project import, persistence, cache policy, autosave, and metrics`

- Execution flow: the File menu opens one Project section -> the path input updates the active project-file destination -> `New project` dispatches `create-project`, prompts for a title, builds a blank structured workspace record, activates it, and retargets the suggested JSON path without bypassing the project record path -> hovering or focusing `Load project` opens a recent-project submenu derived from durable `state.projectLibrary` records -> clicking a recent entry writes that entry's ID to `projectLibrarySelectionId` and dispatches the existing `load-project` selected-record activation path -> clicking the main `Load project` row still dispatches `load-project-file` into `ProjectPersistenceService.chooseProjectSnapshotFileForLoad` so the author can choose a project JSON before hydration -> project-library state normalization replaces stale browser cache and resolves an active durable record -> activation state hydrates runtime project data and activation controller coordinates shell refresh/render effects -> `Save` dispatches `save-project` into the current project snapshot save path -> save/autosave assembles a durable record from runtime state and routes it through the persistence service to the active project file or explicit compatibility cache fallback
- Flow-on effects: project JSON is the source of truth for desktop-style workflows, the load/save UI no longer duplicates saved-library/import/debug contexts, new project creation and recent-record switching are available from the same Project menu as file load/save, menu actions and autosave read as a minimal vertical list, browser cache is disposable and failure-aware, project metrics are derived from loaded records, autosave dirty state is explicit, and tests cover source provenance, imported counts, save/load/restore flows, simplified menu actions, visible new-project access, recent-project rendering/order/action IDs, and cache replacement behavior
- Extraction/port notes: Phase 1 ownership extraction is complete for the current browser host; remaining editor calls must continue routing through `ProjectPersistenceService`, while broader shell/state facade work is scheduled in the refactor roadmap

- Supplemental execution flow (Feature `8.2` active recent-project pinning): a JSON load or Scrivener port activates a project through `ProjectPersistenceService` -> Save As records the chosen project-file destination in the active project record -> the File menu recent list maps durable project-library records -> `buildRecentProjectMenuItems` marks the active record and sorts it before timestamp-based entries -> the active loaded/imported project is visible at the top of the recent submenu even when the five-item limit would otherwise hide it.
- Supplemental flow-on effects (Feature `8.2` active recent-project pinning): the recent menu remains a UI view over existing project-library records rather than a new persistence path, older loaded projects still sort by `updatedAt`, and Scrivener ports are discoverable immediately after import/save without changing the save-file source-of-truth rule.
- Supplemental code locations (2026-08-04 Feature `8.2e` clicked recent-project activation): `apps/editor/public/app.js:2336` and `apps/editor/public/app.js:3395` (delegated `load-project` actions pass the clicked `data-project-id`), `apps/editor/public/app.js:15289-15340` (`loadSelectedProject` captures the requested project ID before saving the current project, restores it after the save boundary, and activates the exact matching record), and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1491-1499` (static guard for selection capture before persistence). Workflow: the author opens File, hovers or tabs into `Load project`, and clicks a recent project row; the clicked row, not the previously active record, becomes the loaded project. Execution flow: `renderRecentProjectMenu` emits `data-action="load-project"` plus `data-project-id` -> delegated click handlers call `loadSelectedProject(target.dataset.projectId)` -> `loadSelectedProject` stores that requested ID before `persistCurrentProjectRecord` saves the project being left -> after persistence may reset `projectLibrarySelectionId` to the active record, the captured ID is restored and resolved exactly -> `activateProjectRecord` hydrates the clicked project through the existing activation controller. Persistence/rendering implications: the current project is still saved before switching; recent switching remains a project-library activation path, not a new file/cache writer; missing explicit project IDs are rejected instead of silently falling back to the wrong project. Verification: desktop smoke assertions plus syntax checks.

Workflow: the scene editor presents task, research, custom metadata, event, and World Spine anchors as compact first-line markers in the ManuScriptInfographicLane. Execution flow: `createManuScriptInfographicLanePreviewsForScene` derives anchor-backed previews -> `selectManuscriptProjections` emits the `ManuScriptInfographicLane` render-only channel -> the textarea host maps projections to wrapped visual lines and marker buttons -> delegated shell routing opens the owning task, passage note, or World Spine node without making visual marker state durable. Verification: focused selector, projection, host, scene, and desktop application evidence tests.
- Feature 1.5j - ManuScriptInfographicLane floating eye visibility: `apps/editor/public/editor-model.js:113`, `apps/editor/public/editor-model.js:193-220`, `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:259-275`, `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:354`, `apps/editor/public/features/scene-editor.js:379-401`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/editor-host/textarea-editor-host.js:133-139`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/editor-host/textarea-editor-host.js:503-519`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/editor-host/textarea-editor-host.js:1057-1088`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:2073-2074`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:9372-9392`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:11124-11136`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:4786-4898`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:9871-9891`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:10111-10234`, `test/editor-model.test.mjs:286-302`, `test/scene-editor.test.mjs:92-94`, `test/scene-editor.test.mjs:239-246`, `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:509-514`, `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1148-1150`. Workflow: the author clicks a floating eye in the left manuscript margin to hide or restore the vertical ManuScriptInfographicLane marker pane. Execution flow: delegated click handling calls `toggleManuScriptInfographicLaneVisibility` -> `editorPrefs.manuScriptInfographicLaneVisible` is normalized and persisted through browser compatibility storage plus the current project record -> scene rendering suppresses marker projections when collapsed and leaves a compact restore eye -> host sync clears or repaints only the render-only marker track. Verification: editor-pref normalization tests, scene visible/hidden markup tests, desktop route/CSS evidence tests, and focused ManuScriptInfographicLane test bundle.
- Feature 1.5k - Scrivener imported comments and footnotes: `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:4-6`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:887-916`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:1182-1532`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:1849-1850`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:1970-1974`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:2001`, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/scrivener-import-service.js:2029`, `test/scrivener-import-service.test.mjs:96-161`. Workflow: imported Scrivener comments and footnotes appear as a generated `Comments and Footnotes` Metadata Console group with scene folders. Execution flow: Scrivener port pairs manuscript content with `content.comments` sidecars -> sidecar RTF/XML bodies become folder notes -> `scrivcmt://` field results resolve selected text back to scene/span anchors where possible -> snapshot activation hydrates the notes through existing `metadataSubgroups`. Verification: focused Scrivener import coverage plus the Serva Vitae dry import confirm generated metadata, comment/footnote counts, provenance, and resolved anchors.

- Feature 1.7g - Centered manuscript measure with floating ManuScriptInfographicLane: `apps/editor/public/styles.css:4471-4494`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:4657-4704`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:4738-4751`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:5874-5899`, `apps/editor/public/styles.css:8912-8948`. Workflow: the scene editor keeps line numbers in the layout and floats the ManuScriptInfographicLane marker strip over the left editor margin so it does not shift the editable manuscript measure. Execution flow: existing scene-editor markup renders -> CSS grid variables assign only gutter and body tracks -> the ManuScriptInfographicLane is absolutely positioned outside normal grid flow -> marker buttons remain clickable while blank lane space passes through to the editor -> the textarea and render-only overlays keep their centered text-width measure. Verification: manual CSS inspection; no project persistence or JavaScript behavior changed.

- Feature 4.1t - Center-gated follow-scroll: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-viewport-tracker-service.js:55-176` (viewport center-line input, `center-wait` no-op plan, and developer-log context), `apps/editor/public/app.js:9416-9514` (measured live line resolution, viewport center-line calculation, scroll-plan dispatch, and no-op/scroll logging), `apps/editor/public/adapters/editor-host/textarea-editor-host.js:368-381` (measured textarea visual-line resolver), and `test/narration-viewport-tracker-service.test.mjs:109-145`. Workflow: while the author reads in Narration + Voice, an early live match near the top of the visible page stays on screen; the manuscript scroll only starts once the tracked line reaches the viewport center. Execution flow: speech alignment produces a runtime follow selection -> the shell resolves its visible textarea line and the current center line -> `createNarrationViewportTracker().planScroll` updates progress state but returns `center-wait` for premature downward scrolls -> the shell leaves `scrollTop` unchanged until a later center-line match permits the normal capped scroll plan. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-viewport-tracker-service.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/narration-viewport-tracker-service.test.mjs`, and the focused `runNarrationViewportTrackerServiceTest` command.
- Feature 4.1u - Lead display decoupled from live highlight and first-line follow-scroll: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-display-service.js:51-72` (confirmed viewport-offset resolver), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-display-service.js:74-94` (confirmed tracking-offset resolver), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-display-service.js:96-139` (lead selection keeps predicted offsets as `displayLeadStartOffset`/`displayLeadEndOffset` metadata while preserving confirmed tracking and viewport offsets), `apps/editor/public/app.js:9746-9819` and `apps/editor/public/app.js:9831-9890` (shell paints current/read projections and paces viewport from resolved confirmed offsets), `test/narration-follow-display-service.test.mjs:1-61` (lead metadata does not move spoken-word highlight or viewport offsets), and `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:310-359` (screenshot-shaped `Tex's`/`Texas` opening transcript still resolves to line 306 even when the viewport is lower). Workflow: while the author records in Narration + Voice, the cyan current highlight stays on the spoken transcript match and the softer read trail ends at confirmed coverage; predicted lead words can be calculated for diagnostics/future tuning but are not painted as current speech and do not become the scroll target. Execution flow: ASR alignment produces a runtime follow selection -> `createNarrationFollowLeadSelection` calculates capped lead metadata from `trackingEndOffset` -> confirmed tracking offsets remain on the selection used by `createNarrationFollowPreviewSelections` for the `narration-follow-current` projection -> confirmed viewport offsets feed `paceNarrationFollowViewport` and the center-gated viewport tracker -> saved take coverage and project persistence continue to use recognized anchors rather than predicted text. Persistence/rendering implications: this changes only runtime display/scroll offsets and tests; manuscript text, saved take anchors, audio media, transcripts, cleanup text, and project data are unchanged. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-display-service.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-display-service.test.mjs`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, direct focused `runNarrationFollowDisplayServiceTest`, `runNarrationFollowAlignmentServiceTest`, and `runManuscriptEditorHostTest`.
- Feature 4.1v - Local live-follow recovery window: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:13-16`, `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:184-216`, `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:263-323`, `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:421-507`, `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:736-787`, and `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:866-891`, plus `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:83-111`, `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:240-278`, and `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:321-345`. Workflow: while the author is recording and skips a line or two, the live cursor can recover within the current paragraph or the immediately following paragraph, but a similar later paragraph no longer becomes the active follow selection or scroll target. Execution flow: ASR transcript tails reach `alignNarrationTranscriptToScene` -> the service detects an existing `currentFollowSelection` and builds an active search window from that anchor block through one following block -> viewport/near/recovery passes are filtered to those block indexes -> same-block candidate starts are bounded around the last confirmed token and next-block starts are limited to the beginning of that paragraph -> out-of-window text leaves the session in recovering state with the previous follow selection, so rendering and follow-scroll remain near the reader. Persistence/rendering implications: this only changes runtime alignment selection and developer-log context; manuscript text, project records, saved take anchors, and persisted audio metadata are unchanged. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, and `node --experimental-strip-types test/run-tests.mjs narration-follow-alignment-service`.
- Feature 4.1w - Stable live-follow anchor acceptance: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:17-21` (stable-confidence and transition thresholds), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:750-818` (candidate acceptance guard and rejected-candidate selection), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:954-989` (search-pass continuation until an accepted candidate is found), and `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:1044` (developer-log rejected-candidate summary), plus `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:281-428`. Workflow: while the author records, a short noisy transcript fragment that resembles a later visible paragraph does not drag the live cursor or scroll target away from the last stable read position. Execution flow: ASR transcript tails reach `alignNarrationTranscriptToScene` -> each pass still finds its strongest candidate -> the candidate must pass confidence, next-block transition, and same-block backward-repeat guards before becoming `followSelection` -> rejected viewport candidates are retained in debug context and the near-viewport/current-anchor pass can still produce a stronger accepted match -> if no stable candidate exists, the runtime session remains recovering on the previous follow selection. Persistence/rendering implications: this is runtime-only alignment and logging behavior; it changes live overlay/scroll inputs by keeping them stable, but does not mutate manuscript text, saved take anchors, audio media, cleanup transcripts, or project data. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, direct `runNarrationFollowAlignmentServiceTest`, and adjacent follow display, viewport tracker, and speech-recognition focused tests.
- Feature 4.1x - Paragraph-start live coverage recovery: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:22-23` (short-prefix limits), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:637-689` (matched selection creation keeps `trackingStartOffset` on the confirmed token while expanding `startOffset` for coverage), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:701-730` (short prefix expansion guard), and `apps/editor/public/app.js:10075-10111` (cumulative live-follow range merge preserves the confirmed tracking offsets), plus `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:93-121`. Workflow: while recording, ASR may first align `his way toward...` even though the author spoke `Making his way...`; the live follow selection now keeps the current tracking cursor on `his` but expands read/saved coverage to include `Making`. Execution flow: transcript tail reaches `alignNarrationTranscriptToScene` -> `createSelectionFromMatch` resolves the first matched token -> a short punctuation-free prefix at the start of the block is included in `startOffset`/`selectedText` -> shell coverage merging keeps `trackingStartOffset` on the confirmed token for viewport/current-span behavior -> finalization can persist the expanded coverage as the saved take anchor. Persistence/rendering implications: live overlay coverage and new saved take anchors include short initial spoken prefixes; current tracking, viewport pacing, transcript timing, media files, and cleanup transcripts are unchanged. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, and direct `runNarrationFollowAlignmentServiceTest`.
- Feature 4.1y - Responsive changed-transcript live follow: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:3-4` (responsive delta threshold), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:56-64` and `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:402-440` (changed-transcript search-plan construction), `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js:915-1161` (alignment loop tries changed transcript plans before full-tail fallback and logs the matched transcript source), `apps/editor/public/app.js:1470-1518` (700 ms local live PCM chunks and ASR snapshot forwarding), `apps/editor/public/app.js:10264-10272` (shell passes `changedTranscript` into the alignment service), and `test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs:361-403` (fresh two-word delta advances the tracking cue). Workflow: while the narrator keeps speaking, the live cue can advance from the newest ASR delta without waiting for the older transcript tail to win scoring; if the delta is too short, weak, or outside the guarded local window, the normal transcript-tail recovery path still applies. Execution flow: Browser Web Speech or desktop Sherpa emits a snapshot -> the shell forwards both transcript and changed transcript -> `alignNarrationTranscriptToScene` builds a `changed-transcript` plan before `transcript-tail` -> the first accepted candidate becomes the runtime `followSelection` with `match.transcriptSource` for diagnostics -> local desktop fallback audio reaches the sidecar in shorter chunks so snapshots arrive sooner. Persistence/rendering implications: this changes only runtime alignment responsiveness, developer-log context, and live chunk cadence; manuscript text, saved take anchors, media files, transcript fields, cleanup transcript, and project persistence are unchanged. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, direct `runNarrationFollowAlignmentServiceTest`, and adjacent speech-recognition, desktop-speech, live-audio-frame, and follow-display focused tests.

- Feature 4.3x - Saved-take alignment save gate: `apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-recording-alignment-job-service.js:7-67` owns the shared in-flight job registry and save-wait diagnostics, `apps/editor/public/app.js:375`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:1411-1413`, `apps/editor/public/app.js:10917-10981`, and `apps/editor/public/app.js:15508-15521` wire the service into cleanup/review alignment and make project saves wait unless the save was triggered by alignment completion itself, `test/narration-recording-alignment-job-service.test.mjs:16-59` covers duplicate starts, save waits, and queue cleanup, `test/run-tests.mjs:54` and `test/run-tests.mjs:326-328` register the coverage, and `test/desktop-application.test.mjs:1045` guards the app shell save route. Workflow: the author stops a take or opens a saved take review -> cleanup/review processing starts one local word-timestamp alignment job for that recording -> if a project save starts while Whisper is still running, the save waits for the shared job to settle before building the project snapshot -> successful alignment upserts durable `transcriptAlignment` before the file write, preventing the next load from falling back to duration-ratio cursor timing. Persistence/rendering implications: `transcriptAlignment` remains the persisted recording metadata; pending job IDs and save-wait logs are runtime-only diagnostics; review rendering updates through the existing rerender path after alignment storage. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-recording-alignment-job-service.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/narration-recording-alignment-job-service.test.mjs`, direct `runNarrationRecordingAlignmentJobServiceTest`, and desktop smoke coverage.

Workflow: the author opens Narration + Voice -> the read-only manuscript viewport masthead shows Manuscript and Narration toggles beside the script controls -> unchecking Manuscript stores the user preference, rerenders the script with native text readable, and clears manuscript-origin paint while narrated passage coverage remains visible -> unchecking Narration clears saved-take and live-follow paint while manuscript-origin decorations remain controlled separately. Persistence/rendering implications: both settings are user preference data in the existing narration-follow settings storage key; neither mutates manuscript text, author marks, issue records, saved take anchors, media files, transcripts, live follow selections, opened saved-take review state, or Manuscript pane decoration rendering. Verification: focused scene-editor, narration-follow-settings, and editor-host tests cover persisted default normalization, the two viewport checkbox actions, manuscript-hidden with narration coverage preserved, narration-hidden with manuscript decorations preserved, right-panel absence, readable native text guard, stale transparent-class removal, and opened-review preservation.

Workflow: the author starts a narration take -> early browser ASR fragments such as a single `i` remain listening/recovering instead of creating a live highlighted span -> when the recognizer emits a real phrase, the alignment search resolves against the armed manuscript passage rather than the bad one-character anchor -> the textarea host receives the existing `narration-follow-current` projection and paints the spoken-word tracker normally. Persistence/rendering implications: this is runtime-only alignment policy; it does not mutate manuscript text, saved recording anchors, transcripts, cleanup transcripts, media files, or narration-follow settings. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/narration/narration-follow-alignment-service.js`, `node --check test/narration-follow-alignment-service.test.mjs`, focused direct narration alignment/display/host/speech-recognition runs, and full `npm test`.

Execution flow: the detail renderer emits Edit/Delete actions only for editable saved entities and event drafts -> Edit stores transient item ID/kind and opens the existing centered form with field values prefilled -> Save rebuilds the same record ID, preserves provenance/created timestamps where present, writes `updatedAt`, persists through `ProjectPersistenceService`, and pushes World Spine history -> entity name edits refresh saved node `linkedEntityNames` mirrors while keeping canonical `entityLinks` -> Delete removes the entity or event draft from `workspace.world`, filters deleted-entity presence links, clears node mirror IDs/names, persists, and records history. Verification: `node --check apps/editor/public/features/world-spine/worldbuilding-studio.js`, `node --check apps/editor/public/app.js`, `node --check test/worldbuilding-studio.test.mjs`, and focused `runWorldbuildingStudioTest`.

Execution flow: left-click open reads `editorPrefs.worldbuildingCatalogueBounds` and reclamps it against the current viewport/toolbar -> the studio renderer emits optional inline `left/top/width/height`, a resize handle, and a category-scoped `+` action -> drag and resize pointer moves update runtime `worldbuildingCatalogueBounds` and styles without touching catalogue records -> pointer release writes the normalized bounds through `EDITOR_PREFS_KEY` and persists the `app-settings` domain -> pressing `+` calls the existing add form for the current catalogue category -> row selection/detail expansion and window resize reuse the saved bounds while catalogue content remains a read-only projection of `workspace.world` -> close/Escape/outside-click clears only live inspector state. Execution flow: pointerdown on a timeline node body starts a transient session -> pointer movement beyond the threshold clones the node into the central viewport, removes connector dots from the clone, and marks the source as dragging -> pointerup removes the ghost and suppresses the accidental select click. Location and Characters expose type-filtering quick-reference suggestions from structured catalogue/project indexes while accepting free text. Execution flow: the World Spine controller derives pointer position into a tier-aware drop slot -> the shell stores a transient `timeline-form` context with picker option sets and focuses Location -> field clicks inside `[data-world-spine-context-menu]` return before the generic no-action context-menu hide path -> save reads the form, keeps it open with an error when Location is blank, then calls `addParallelWorldSpine` with the authored details and source context -> the service writes a structured `TimelineSpine` with stable ID/key, locality labels, construction metadata, and empty node lists -> the shell persists the world mutation, pushes World Spine history, and rerenders the timeline model so saved spines appear as stacked tiers with minimal lane chips such as `Europa` or `Insert Location Name` and tier-aware drop zones. - Feature 8.2g - Transactional desktop folder-package lifecycle: `apps/desktop/src/http-app.ts:501-582` and `apps/desktop/src/http-app.ts:2239-2320` (same-origin stage/commit/discard routes, opaque host-owned staging identities, explicit roots, contained managed-tree copying, strict package validation, scaffold, and explicit-root writer), `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-package.js:22-54` (desktop staging transport), `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-snapshot-verification.js` (storage-shape-independent semantic readback assertion), and `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:1700-1791` and `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:2473-2527` (shared stage/load/verify/commit ordering, New/Open transitions, Save As A-to-B adoption, and package-aware normal Save). Workflow: New builds a candidate without activation; New and Save As write only to unique sibling staging roots, reload and semantically verify there, commit through the host token only while the final destination remains absent, and adopt the committed root afterward. Open host-validates before preserving and replacing the current project; Save writes the established package. Persistence/rendering implications: failed writes, loads, verification, or commits discard only the registered staging directory and never publish the requested destination; Save As keeps A authoritative and copies only `assets/`, `transcripts/`, and `revisions/`; package roots and operation tokens remain runtime/machine context. Verification: `test/project-package-lifecycle.test.mjs:286-687` physically proves sibling staging, write/readback/verification cleanup, conflict-safe commit, managed-asset portability, and no worktree artifacts; `test/project-persistence-service.test.mjs:1195-1510` proves New and Save As write/load/verification/commit failures preserve authority and discard staging before adoption.
- Feature 8.2h - Portable package DTO and host containment: `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-portable-snapshot.js` explicitly selects current semantic project fields and project-scoped preferences while recursively removing machine roots, `json-persistence-boundary.js` enforces JSON omission/array semantics and rejects non-finite or non-JSON values, `project-migrations.js` rejects future schemas, and `apps/desktop/src/http-app.ts` validates supported schemas plus real filesystem containment for existing package components. Workflow: New/Save/Save As serialize the same portable DTO -> the host writes only validated package-relative paths -> editor readback verifies semantic state; a future schema or symlink/junction redirect fails before durable mutation. `project-package-dialog.js` clears stale child listings when a location is typed, `app.js` rerenders that invalidated list immediately, and confirmation remains disabled until Enter/Browse host-validates the path. Verification: `test/project-snapshot-verification.test.mjs`, `test/project-service-storage.test.mjs`, and the OS-temp `test/project-package-lifecycle.test.mjs` cover semantic sentinels, JSON edge values, schema migration/idempotency, future-version rejection, dialog invalidation/rendering, and outside-file read/write/delete protection.
- Feature 8.2i - Portable activation runtime hydration: `apps/editor/public/state/project-activation-state.js` owns explicit analysis, narration, and voice runtime defaults and merges them into every activated project without replacing retained voice profiles, bindings, or recordings. Workflow: a real New/Open/Save As snapshot crosses the portable package boundary without service jobs -> package readback activates through `applyProjectRecordToState` -> runtime hydration restores the shapes required by Dream Scaping, narration alignment, and voice render/recording consumers -> subsequent portable serialization strips those runtime providers, jobs, and sessions again. Verification: `test/project-activation-state.test.mjs` covers defaults plus durable voice merging, and `test/project-package-lifecycle.test.mjs` physically publishes and reloads the real New Project candidate before exercising representative analysis/narration/voice consumers and confirming runtime state stays non-portable.
- Feature 8.2f - Verified project-file writeback: `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:1318-1369` compares the saved browser-handle or desktop-path JSON against the just-built canonical snapshot, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:1401-1538` and `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/project-persistence-service.js:1546-1618` require that verification after each browser-handle or desktop-path write, `apps/editor/public/adapters/storage/autosave.js:233-267` retains dirty blocked state after cache-only fallback, and `test/project-persistence-service.test.mjs:255-285` covers stale readback after a reported-success write. Workflow: manual save and autosave both build the normal project snapshot, write the `.abe-project.json`, read the same destination back, and only clear dirty state when the stored data equals the canonical snapshot. Persistence implications: scene/world/settings DTO mutations, including World Spine row names and event locations, are verified as part of the whole project JSON; a stale or failed readback preserves the latest snapshot in browser cache, leaves autosave dirty/blocked, and reports the file-layer failure instead of silently claiming the project file is current. Verification: syntax checks for the changed storage/test files, focused project persistence and refresh tests, focused desktop and World Spine panel tests, and full `npm test`.
